Roadmap

The plan of Aeolus, shipped and open, on one page: a status checklist, what each milestone makes Aeolus, then one section per milestone in phase order. A shipped section keeps its record (what was built, the numbers it was measured at, the limitations carried forward); an open section is the plan. Milestones are named by content and match the GitHub milestones; issues for open phases are generated from this page (.github/scripts/generate_roadmap_issues.py).

Guiding principle for everything below

No scaffolds. Every phase ships a production-quality, validated capability.

The LES core deliberately traded depth for breadth — it wired interfaces so later work would have somewhere to plug in. That phase is over. From Phase 21 onward, a phase is only "done" when:

  1. The physics or numerics is implemented to the standard of the reference literature, not a toy approximation.
  2. It is validated against either an analytic solution, a published benchmark with reference data, or observations.
  3. It is stable in the flagship urban run and in the relevant canonical case at production resolution — not just at a tamed Δt or an inflated viscosity.
  4. It has docstrings, a docs page explaining the theory, a regression test, and a benchmark entry.

Items that cannot meet that bar get deferred, not shipped as scaffolds. Where Phases 1–20 left a scaffold, the corresponding phase among Phases 21–45 has the job to replace it with the real thing.

Scope

Roughly 5× the work completed so far, organised as 10 milestones of 8–12 phases each — about 100 phases, each 2–6 weeks. A small team could do this in 4–5 years; a solo contributor should plan on 8–10.

The ordering is deliberately physics-first: Phases 21–45 (numerics, radiation, clouds) close every approximation in the current physics before the project chases scale, ML, or applications. A high-fidelity single-node model is more useful than a fast wrong one.

Phases 101–112 are a future direction beyond that decade: kilometre-scale regional and climate modelling. It is listed so the architecture does not close the door on it, not as a commitment within the 100-phase plan.

✅ shipped · ☐ open. A phase is checked when all of it has shipped; a milestone when all its phases have. Partly delivered phases are split (67a/67b, 81a/81b, 84a/84b). The sections below follow the same order.

Milestones

AfterAeolus is…
Numerical foundations (Phase 31)numerically sound enough to run real-Reynolds urban LES without inflated viscosity
Radiation (Phase 38)radiatively accurate — 3-D urban radiation, real gas optics
Clouds and microphysics (Phase 45)cloud-physics-capable at research standard, superdroplet reference included
Land, urban and surface exchange (Phase 53)a credible urban and land-surface model, validated against field campaigns
Turbulence closure (Phase 59)defensible in the turbulence-modelling literature, including the stable boundary layer
Chemistry and aerosol (Phase 66)a real chemistry-transport model, driven by real inventories
Real-world data, forcing and validation (Phase 73)drivable from reanalysis, validated across the canonical benchmark suite, fully reproducible
Data assimilation and inverse problems (Phase 79)an inverse-problem and uncertainty-quantification platform
Performance, scale and portability (Phase 87)HPC-production: MPI + GPU at scale, high-order backends
Machine learning, applications, ecosystem (Phase 100)a community Earth-system-scale tool with an ML-native workflow and real applications
Kilometre-scale regional and climate modelling (Phase 112), future directiona convection-permitting regional climate model (~3 km) that nests seamlessly down to its own urban LES

LES core and physics scaffolds

Phases 1–20, before the long-range plan. A dry and moist LES core with pluggable pressure solvers, the first urban example, and scaffolds for everything else (MPI, GPU, spectral elements, nesting, radiation, land, chemistry). The rule that followed, "no scaffolds", made each of those scaffolds a later phase's job to replace; the checklist above says which.

After Phase 20 a refinement pass driven by the Alexanderplatz run (27 items: shadows and sky-view factors, wall temperatures, latent and anthropogenic heat, multi-layer soil, MOST, dynamic Smagorinsky, two-moment microphysics, four-species chemistry, aerosol, nesting and terrain scaffolds) closed the most visible gaps; the phases from 21 on replace its approximations with validated schemes. The original phase descriptions are in git history (docs/src/design/roadmap.md before 2026-11-06).

Numerical foundations

Replace every numerical compromise that currently forces artificially high viscosity or small time steps. Without this milestone, none of the physics work downstream can run at realistic parameters.

Status: complete. 2,094 validation tests, all green. Every phase ships a production implementation with measured validation against an analytic solution, a manufactured solution, or a convergence study — numbers recorded per phase below.

Phase 21 — Cut-cell immersed boundary method

src/CutCell.jl. Conservative cut-cell geometry from a level set by sub-cell sampling: wetted volume fractions, per-face open-area apertures, surface normals from ∇φ, wetted areas. Aperture-weighted divergence / gradient / Laplacian, small-cell volume floor plus optional flux-redistribution merging, exact no-penetration enforcement. Validated: discrete adjointness ⟨p,Du⟩ = −⟨Gp,u⟩ to 1.45e-15 relative on a sloping wall with an embedded sphere (this is what makes the projection exact on cut geometry); L = D·G to 2.2e-17 relative; half-cut cell volume fraction and area exact to 1e-14; tilted-plane normals to 1e-12; uniform-flow divergence past a cut wall < 1e-12. Unlocks ν = 10⁻⁵ in urban geometry — the whole point of the milestone.

Phase 22 — Ghost-cell wall reconstruction

src/CutCell.jl. Image/foot-point stencils on the true surface with trilinear donor weights (solid donors dropped, inverse-distance fallback), Dirichlet and Neumann reconstruction, wall shear stress on the true normal. Validated: linear profiles reproduced exactly (max error 0.0); quadratic-profile errors 1.06e-3 → 2.66e-4 → 6.64e-5 at Nz = 16/32/64, i.e. convergence ratios 4.0000 and 4.0 — clean second order; analytic wall stress νᾱ = 0.0375 recovered as 0.0375 with zero spread.

Phase 23 — Adaptive time stepping with substepping

src/TimeIntegration.jl. Per-process CFL diagnostics reporting each limiting dt separately (advective via the true per-cell sum form, diffusive, Brunt-Väisälä, Coriolis, SGS-scalar) plus which one binds; growth-limited adaptive dt; process substepping; and retry-on-failure with bit-exact preallocated state rollback. Validated: hand-computed advective dt to 1e-12 with the right limiter symbol; growth limiting never exceeded; deliberate over-large-dt blow-up triggers rollback and recovers a finite state.

Phase 24 — Stretched and terrain-following vertical coordinate

src/StretchedGrids.jl. StretchedGrid (uniform / geometric / tanh / two-wall-tanh / custom) and TerrainFollowingGrid (Gal-Chen–Somerville σ) as AbstractMesh subtypes, with metric-correct z-stencils added as new methods on the FiniteVolume internals so the public Operators façade picks them up by dispatch with no edit to it. Two distinct metrics (dzc cell thickness, dzf centre spacing) so div(grad) stays self-adjoint. Volume-weighted vertical interpolation. Validated: :uniform reduces to RegularGrid to 1.44e-15; Laplacian convergence ratios 3.976, 3.995; centre→face ∂z exact on a linear profile to 6.2e-17; volume-weighted interpolation 5.7e-14 where naive ½-averaging gives 0.270; flat terrain reduces to the stretched case to 1.7e-18 with J ≡ 1; ∂x of physical height over a bell hill 3.5e-15 with the metric cross-term vs 0.158 without; hydrostatic residual 3.75e-4 relative and unchanged over the hill, converging at ratios 3.99, 4.00 — and 86.5× worse if the wrong metric is used, which the suite asserts.

Phase 25 — Semi-implicit IMEX time integration

src/TimeIntegration.jl. Matrix-free implicit diffusion (I − dtν∇²) via Jacobi-preconditioned CG (scalar or per-cell ν, wall rows eliminated), plus ARK2 (ARS(2,2,2)) and Rosenbrock2 (ROS2 W-method) drivers that project at every stage so each stage value is divergence-free. Validated: manufactured implicit solve in 3 CG iterations, max error 9.1e-15 (34 iterations for variable ν); unconditionally stable at 100× the explicit limit — 20 backward-Euler steps, monotone decay, non-negative, mass drift 5.2e-12; measured convergence orders 2.0004 / 2.0002 (ARK2) and 1.986 / 1.993 (Rosenbrock2); divergence 4.3e-16 after stepping with walls + SGS + body force; 18.4× dt speed-up over the diffusive constraint.

Phase 26 — WENO-Z and TENO advection for all prognostics

src/AdvectionSchemes.jl. WENO-Z5/Z7 (τ-based Z weights), TENO5/TENO8 (ENO cut-off then optimal linear weights), corrected WENO-JS5 as an accuracy reference, high-order staggered momentum advection, and a positivity-preserving flux limiter. All coefficients derived in exact rational arithmetic; smoothness indicators evaluated in the a-basis so they keep relative accuracy as h → 0. Validated: measured orders WENO-Z5 5.00, WENO-Z7 6.86–6.98, TENO5 4.99, TENO8 6.95–7.19 (polynomial exactness is the sharper statement: TENO8 reproduces a degree-7 polynomial to 4.4e-16); critical-point accuracy — WENO-Z 1.19e-9 vs WENO-JS 8.73e-9 (7.3× better), the defect Z-weights exist to fix; square-wave total-variation ratios 1.0004 (Z5) / 0.99992 (Z7) / 1.00029 (TENO5) vs 4.11 for centred; positivity — without the limiter a high-order scheme reaches −1.84 (Z5) and −19.6 (TENO8), with it −1.7e-18 and −2.0e-28 at mass drift ≤ 3e-15.

Phase 27 — Anelastic and pseudo-incompressible dynamics

src/Anelastic.jl. ReferenceState base states (isothermal, isentropic, constant-N², arbitrary profile) constructed in discrete hydrostatic balance on the model's own grid, with a logarithmic-mean face density so the exponential case is simultaneously analytic and discretely balanced. DensityWeightedPoissonSolver (deflated-Jacobi PCG for ∇·(w(z)∇φ) = f) and one generic mass-weighted core aliased as AnelasticSimulation (w = ρ̄) and PseudoIncompressibleSimulation (w = ρ̄θ̄). Validated: hydrostatic residual 3.0e-13 … 6.8e-14 Pa/m across all four constructors (≤ 4.3e-14 relative); isothermal ρ̄ matches the analytic exponential to 2.2e-16; constant-N² recovered to 2e-17; variable-coefficient Poisson with a density ratio of 42.5 recovered to 1.25e-13 in 71 iterations, operator exactly self-adjoint (max|A − Aᵀ| = 0.0); constraint after a step (1/ρ̄)∇·(ρ̄u) = 1.1e-17 while plain ∇·u = 3.1e-5 — confirming the leftover is exactly −w ∂log ρ̄/∂z; Boussinesq-limit error grows linearly with density variation across three decades (9.96, 10.25 per decade); warm bubble rises in all three systems with anelastic ascent 3.1% stronger aloft.

Direct anelastic pressure solver (#309)

✅ SHIPPED (PressureSolvers.ColumnTridiagonalSolver, Anelastic.DirectWeightedPoissonSolver; #309 / PR #386). The density-weighted operator div(w(z) grad) is separable, so the sound-proof cores now project with a horizontal FFT/DCT plus one tridiagonal solve per wavenumber. It is the default (pressure_solver = :direct); the Jacobi-preconditioned CG stays as :cg. StretchedPoissonSolver delegates to the same column solver, so the FFT/DCT + Thomas code has one source.

Measured: manufactured solution recovered to 1.2e-15–3.9e-15 relative; agreement with the CG 8.7e-14–2.7e-13; a single solve at one thread 176 ms (367 CG iterations) → 1.05 ms at 64³ (166×) and 1868 → 8.1 ms at 128×128×64 (231×). Berlin, 64³, nest off, core = :anelastic: 391 → 200 ms per step, weighted divergence 6.4e-13 → 2.9e-17 s⁻¹.

Expectation that was wrong (merge train): logging_step_diagnostics assumed the old CG default of the sound-proof cores (an iterative solve with iteration counts) after PS-a made the direct solve the default.

Not built: a periodic vertical (the sound-proof cores reject one; the CG covers it).

Phase 28 — Energy- and enstrophy-conserving discrete operators

src/AdvectionSchemes.jl. Morinishi skew-symmetric momentum advection (exact discrete KE conservation for any velocity field), a variance-conserving scalar form, and the KE / scalar-variance budget and enstrophy diagnostics that serve as the validation instruments. Validated: KE production on a non-divergence-free field — skew 8.3e-17 vs flux form 0.176, a 2e15 separation; Taylor-Green advection-only drift over 2000 steps — skew −3.19e-11 (pure RK3 time error) vs flux form +0.223, ~7e9× worse; variance-conserving form −2.2e-16 vs centred −2.77e-2 when ∇·u ≠ 0; KE-budget closure residual 5.4e-16 relative; enstrophy satisfies νΣu·∇²u = −2ν·enstrophy to 1e-8, an independent check of the staggered curl. Bug found and fixed: the Phase-10 WENO-5 in src/FiniteVolume.jl wrote 2f0 / 3f0 in its smoothness indicators. In Julia those are Float32 literals, not 2*f0 / 3*f0. The scheme was therefore not translation-invariant (violation 0.0236), measured ~3rd order instead of 5th, and produced scalar variance where every other upwind scheme dissipates. The centre argument is now named fc so the mistake is unrepresentable; translation-invariance violation is now 0.0.

Phase 29 — Multigrid pressure solver

src/Multigrid.jl. Cell-centred geometric multigrid: V/W-cycle plus full-multigrid start, red-black Gauss-Seidel smoother, full-weighting restriction and trilinear prolongation, variable-coefficient support with coefficient coarsening, null-space projection at every level for singular (all-periodic / all-Neumann) systems, and a multigrid-preconditioned CG variant. Validated: cycle count flat at 7 from 16³ to 64³ — a 64× cell increase with no growth, which is the defining multigrid property and exactly what CG cannot do; per-cycle convergence factor 0.07–0.12; manufactured solution to 1e-9; agreement with the exact cosine-FFT solver to 1e-8 on channel BCs; variable-coefficient recovery 6.4e-11; MG-PCG converges in 8 iterations; divergence 3.7e-13 when used as the projection solver inside a running simulation; non-power-of-two grids degrade gracefully via semi-coarsening.

Phase 30 — Grid nesting with proper two-way feedback

src/AdaptiveMesh.jl. Prolongation via the primitive function (parent cell averages fix the cumulative integral exactly at parent faces), so conservation is structural rather than approximate; staggering handled per-direction so a face velocity is nodal along its own axis and conservative across the others; volume/area-weighted restriction; Berger-Colella flux registers and refluxing; a tapered nest sponge; and subcycled stepping with time-interpolated boundary data. Validated: prolongation orders 1.97 / 3.04 / 3.97 for requested 2 / 3 / 4; restriction conservation bit-exact at r=2 and 4.0e-16 at r=3; flux-preserving prolongation interface mismatch 1.5e-33; mass error 0.0 with flux correction vs 1.06e-3 without — the correction doing real, measurable work; nest sponge reduces reflected amplitude 12.6× (3.71e-2 → 2.94e-3). Since then the parent–child operators moved to src/Nesting.jl (#250) and gained multi-level hierarchies, sibling, non-aligned and stretched children (#328); see the two records below.

Nesting hardening: hierarchies, siblings, non-aligned and stretched children (#327–#329, #349)

✅ SHIPPED (src/Nesting.jl, src/nesting/; follow-ups of the Berlin nesting epic #246, whose N-* PRs #272–#292, #323, #330 and #343 are counted with Turbulence closure; #327 / PR #339, #329 / PR #348, #349 / PR #371, #328 / PR #394). What was built:

Measured:

  • Flux registers (M3), 100 steps with a WENO-Z5 tracer slot: composite tracer drift 1.2e-14 per step booked by the transport, against 2.9e-7 booked by the core hook (the pre-#327 way) and 2.5e-6 unbooked.
  • Two-way circulation (M5), 4–6 seeds per configuration: two-way minus one-way child-mean w at mid-depth −0.163 ± 0.057 m/s (6 of 6 seeds), and none at r = 1 (+0.034 ± 0.062 m/s). It is thermally direct: a standalone CBL at the child's spacing is 0.080 K cooler below 800 m.
  • Sponge before the projection: child max|∇·u| 2.8e-14 s⁻¹, against 1.6 s⁻¹ with the same relaxation after the step.
  • Memory and allocation: a 32³ child's field sets 5144 → 2144 KiB; per nested step one-way 5371 → 57.7 KiB and two-way 9009 → 78.0 KiB; a 32³ Boussinesq step! 1641 KiB → 0.
  • Limiters: an unlimited oblique 0/1 front gives [−0.250, 1.250] (order 3); :monotone keeps [0, 1] and the parent mean.
  • Hierarchies (three levels, two siblings at r = 2 and 3, r = 5/2 with off-face edges, a stretched child; one- and two-way; step! and step_rk3!): free stream max|u − U|/|U| ≤ 3.8e-15 and |∇·u|Δ/|U| ≤ 7.5e-16; composite θ with refluxing ≤ 5.2e-15 per step (3.5e-8 to 2.7e-7 without); prolongation order 1.99–4.00 for r ∈ {5/2, 3/2, 7/3}, and 1.98 / 2.99 / 3.98 on a stretched child. RMS error over the nest against an exact solution: coarse 0.235, child 0.106, grandchild 0.070, uniform 25 m reference 0.031.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • #287 had deleted the mixed-core two-way guard (M1): a Boussinesq parent with an anelastic child ran two-way, silently wrong.
  • The merged M2 flux kernel inferred as Tuple{Any,Any,Any}: 313 kB per call and about 43× slower. Now 0 B, with an @inferred test.
  • A vertically periodic open box never initialised its radiating outflow, so a :convective nest face stayed Dirichlet (residual 1.0 instead of 1.15e-7).
  • boundary_time and the two-way feedback did not invalidate the cached sponge target: two sponged RK3 runs differed by 1.4e-3.
  • Refluxing wrapped every axis with mod1, so a top-plane correction landed in the parent's lowest layer.
  • A sponged child driven through begin_child_step! got no velocity relaxation and no warning (max|Δu| 4.02e-3 in one step).
  • A first version made :positive the default of the low-level interpolators; the prolongation-order tests on products of sines failed. The code default was wrong, not the tests.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • The mass-measure refluxing test passed only because the triply periodic default wrapped a flux through the parent's ground into its top layer. Its registers are now physical (drift 3.86e-15 with refluxing, 1.71e-7 without).
  • "The two-way circulation has the opposite sign to the literature" (M5): the descent is a resolution effect, not a coupling bug.

Not built: overlapping siblings (refused; no priority rule); two-way feedback on a non-aligned child without a common-face conservation box (refused); escalating rollback; validation in the Berlin geometry (idealised cases only, Berlin runs paused). AdaptiveMesh keeps its own index-space proper-nesting check.

Nested Berlin: synthetic inflow on by default, checkpoint/restart (epic #246, open)

✅ SHIPPED (#268 / PR #415, #269 / PR #416). What was built:

  • Synthetic inflow on the Berlin nest (#268). The :inflow_turbulence addon puts Transition.InflowTurbulence on the child's lateral nest faces, on by default: the digital filter (Xie & Castro 2008) by default, FSD (free_stream_generator, now the single FSD factory) as an option; statistics from the parent's resolved field by default (ParentResolvedStatistics, which now accepts a StretchedGrid parent, bit-identical to a RegularGrid on a uniform grid; SGS TKE from each level's thickness), or parametrized. They are folded from a new nest window hook (fold_inflow_statistics!). budget! books the injected KE flux as :inflow_ke. Two-way feedback leaves out an inflow band of 2 max Ls (`excludeinflowfeedback!). A newCaseConfigfield[boundaries] analysisbuffer(default 140 m, the canopy-top estimate of the N-inflow-d fetch rule) andanalysis_columns(case)` cut the columns near lateral faces out of the observation-compared u* diagnostics.
  • Checkpoint/restart of the nested Berlin case (#269). Aeolus.IO.structural_state / restore_structural_state! record and refill the whole mutable state of an object graph, aliasing included. nest_state / restore_nest_state! cover a whole NestedRun: both domains with halos, closure memory, open-boundary buffers, the two parent time levels and the window, step-size memory, flux registers, statistics, boundary data and the inflow generator, with a parent step running ahead settled first. The Berlin driver checkpoints the whole case on the [checkpoint] schedule (on in berlin_summer.toml, every 1800 s of wall clock, 2 kept; Boussinesq core only) and run! resumes the newest checkpoint into a fresh build, NetCDF output continuing at the checkpoint's record (nc_append_record!(...; at)).

Measured:

  • Idealised nest (inflow_nest_longrun.jl, Hellsten-type CBL, parent 100 m, r = 3, 2 h): one-way, resolved TKE within 200 m of the upwind face at 50–150 m is 3.165 m²/s² with the generator against 2.278 without (+39 %). KE flux 0.786 W/m²; largest added mass flux 3.7e-17 of the mean-flow flux; child max|∇·u| 1.1e-16.
  • Two-way without the generator, against one-way: child KE ×1.04, parent ×0.995, overlap θ −0.002 K (passes).
  • Berlin smoke (12 steps of 2 s, noon), every variant: added mass flux 1.3e-18 to 2.7e-18 of the mean-flow flux; KE flux 0.0007–0.0017 W/m²; child heat ledger ≤ 7e-13 W/m², water ≤ 4.6e-16 kg/m².
  • Berlin flagship, 80 s of model time (coupling check, not a fetch measurement): the generator adds +3.9 % TKE at canopy top within 200 m of the upwind face at 03:00 and +2.5 % at 12:00; 163 against 161 ms/step.
  • Nested restart (nest_restart.jl, TKE parent, Lagrangian-dynamic child, r = 3, generator on, adaptive steps, checkpoint mid-window): bitwise equal to the uninterrupted run in every field, closure memory, open-boundary buffer, generator plane and clock, one-way, one-way with the parent running ahead, and two-way, at 1 and 12 threads. Restoring only the two simulations differs in 11, 16 and 28 of 30 items. Record 8.8–9.0 MiB, write 0.03–0.8 s, restore 0.03–1.1 s.
  • Berlin smoke resume (config_roundtrip.jl, every coupling on, 15 adaptive steps, checkpoint every 6, interrupted after step 9): state bitwise equal, parent included, and all 51 NetCDF variables equal. Flagship checkpoint 2754.5 MiB in 9.5 s (child alone 20.9 MiB, 0.02 s); a fresh process restored it and stayed bitwise for 30 child steps.

Two-way nesting with the generator: stable since #418 / PR #419. #268 measured it unstable on the idealised nest over 2 h, against one-way with the generator: child KE ×12.4 (×11.8 in a second realisation), parent ×2.6 (×2.4), overlap θ +0.26 K (+0.23 K). The cause was a loop through the two-way interface flux correction: it set each parent interface face to the child's flux, which included the synthetic perturbation, so the next window's boundary data carried the perturbation back and the parent statistics measured the grown eddies. PR #419 subtracts the synthetic normal velocity (a new contract generic synthetic_normal_velocity(data, side, t) in BoundaryConditions, implemented by InflowTurbulence) from the child's open faces for the correction and its ledger; runs without a synthetic part stay bit-identical. Measured, two-way against one-way with the generator, 2 h sheared CBL, r = 3: child KE ×0.937 / ×1.091, parent ×1.015 / ×1.020, overlap θ −0.0031 / −0.0085 K (seeds 1 / 2), within #268's 1.5× / 0.2 K criteria. The former @test_broken in inflow_nest_longrun.jl are real tests for both realisations (18/18 pass). Decaying refine-3 nest, 2 h: child ×0.985, parent ×0.999, θ +0.0009 K (×2.26, ×1.21, +0.31 K before). Composite θ ledger drift ≤ 3.5e-14 per parent step; interface imbalance before the correction 1.2e-16 (1.4e-2 before). Footprint-excluded parent statistics were measured and dropped (child ×1.76 alone, no gain on top of the fix). The coupling's two-way warning is removed; the Berlin two-way nest itself stays unvalidated (#331).

Bugs the tests caught:

  • A shallow copy in the structural walk restored the run's own plane arrays into the fresh build; nest_restart.jl caught it.
  • An example driver's types could not be deserialized: Serialization looks a type up by its module path, so driver modules are now bound in Main.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • The inflow smoke check expected exactly zero perturbation on outflow levels; the applied perturbation blends two knots in time, so a level just turned to outflow keeps the older knot's share. The check is now on the newest knot.
  • The config_berlin check for the anelastic core with checkpoints first hit the adaptive-timestepper incompatibility and never ran its own assertion; it now sets timestepper = "fixed".
  • At merge, the smoke matrix's anelastic run inherited [checkpoint] from berlin_summer.toml and was refused (correctly) by the config check; the smoke matrix now turns checkpoints off on a non-Boussinesq core.

Not built: the Berlin fetch measurement (hours of model time on the nested child; long runs are paused), deferred to the #270 24 h validation, which should replace the 140 m analysis_buffer default. Checkpoints of the anelastic core; resume of child_boundaries runs (refused with a message).

Phase 31 — Adaptive mesh refinement

src/AdaptiveMesh.jl. Block-structured AMR: Löhner gradient, vorticity and Richardson-extrapolation refinement criteria with union / intersection / majority combination; Berger-Rigoutsos signature-based clustering; conservative regridding; proper-nesting enforcement by flag erosion; and recursive subcycled level advance with refluxing. Validated: criteria flag 896 cells at a tanh front and 0 in the smooth region (vorticity: 512 at a shear layer, 0 for uniform flow); Berger-Rigoutsos turns 344 flagged cells into 27 blocks at 0.787 efficiency; mass conserved to 1.13e-16 across two regrid events (vs 4.49e-3 with refluxing disabled); and AMR reaches near-fine-grid accuracy at 56% of the cell count (L1 error 1.206e-2 on 2,296 cells vs 1.247e-2 on 4,096 uniform-fine cells, against 2.019e-2 coarse).

Numerical foundations — known limitations carried forward

Recorded honestly rather than papered over; each is a candidate follow-up phase:

  • Solver/geometry coupling. The FFT and cosine-FFT pressure solvers are aperture-unaware, so cut-cell runs must re-apply the velocity BC after the legacy projection. Wiring cutcell_laplacian! into the Phase-29 multigrid (which already supports variable coefficients) closes this.
  • Stretched-grid operator coverage. Advection, momentum advection, Coriolis and the Smagorinsky/eddy-viscosity kernels are not yet retrofitted for StretchedGrid — they MethodError rather than silently using the wrong metric, which is the safe failure mode.
  • Cut-cell geometry is sampling-quantised; marching-cubes exact intersection is the refinement.
  • IMEX SGS term is a co-located approximation of the Smagorinsky stress divergence, and ν_eff is lagged at uⁿ (standard).
  • Nested pressure projection is single-level per grid; a multi-level MAC sync projection is out of scope, so refluxing leaves O(mismatch) divergence in the parent shell.
  • Sound-proof cores reuse the Boussinesq viscous/SGS kernels rather than density-weighted flux divergences, and do not carry moisture.
  • AMR block advance uses a prescribed velocity field with flux-form upwind; per-block momentum and pressure solves are future work.

Radiation

Status: all seven phases (32–38) and the shared contract are complete (Phases 34–37 landed together in ac38822; the radiation test set is 4,912 tests on master at 2895fed). src/RadiationCore.jl fixes the interface every component in the radiation stack speaks — SpectralGrid (RRTMG-layout bands with correlated-k g-points and weights), OpticalProperties (τ/ssa/g with the correct optical-depth- and scattering-weighted combine_optics! rule), RadiativeFluxes (level-based, bottom-up, net = dn - up positive downward), AtmosphericColumn, and the AbstractGasOptics / AbstractCloudOptics / AbstractAerosolOptics / AbstractRTSolver / AbstractSurfaceOptics interfaces. Validated: the shortwave band solar source sums to exactly 1361.0 W/m², and π·Σbands `planckband` recovers σT⁴ to 0.008% (the residual lying genuinely outside 10-3250 cm⁻¹).

Replace the prescribed-profile and Beer's-law approximations with a real radiative-transfer solver, including 3-D effects that matter in urban geometry.

Phase 32 — Correlated-k gas optics

src/GasOptics.jl. Full spectral treatment of H₂O, CO₂, O₃, CH₄, N₂O and O₂ across the 16 RRTMG longwave and 14 shortwave bands, on the model's own vertical grid, replacing the prescribed LW-down constant.

Design deviation from the original plan, and why. The plan said "bind RRTMGP.jl". That is not possible on this host: RRTMGP.jl pulls in NetCDF/HDF5 for its k-distribution files, and those binary dependencies do not load here (the same libmpifort.so failure that forced the Serialization+TOML I/O layer in Phases 1–20). Rather than ship a module that cannot be loaded, Phase 32 builds the k-distribution from first principles instead of reading it from a file:

  • A Malkmus statistical narrow-band model with embedded line parameters (mean line strength and mean line spacing per band per species) gives band transmission in closed form.
  • Domoto (1974) inversion converts that band transmission into a cumulative k-distribution, from which per-g-point absorption coefficients follow — the standard route from a band model to correlated-k when line-by-line data is unavailable.
  • Pressure and temperature scaling of the absorber amount follows the usual Curtis-Godson-style path scaling.

This is less accurate than reading real RRTMGP k-tables — it is a band-model reconstruction, not a line-by-line fit — but it is self-contained, loads everywhere, and lands the broadband fluxes within a few percent of observations (below). A future phase can add an optional RRTMGP.jl backend behind the same AbstractGasOptics interface on hosts where it loads.

Also implemented: Rayleigh scattering optics, Planck-weighted longwave layer and surface sources, and us_standard_column for a reference atmosphere. erf_series/erfc_series are hand-written because SpecialFunctions does not load either.

Measured validation (US Standard Atmosphere, 40 layers):

QuantityAeolusReference
Clear-sky OLR240.2 W/m²~240 (global mean)
Surface LW downwelling306.7 W/m²288 (Brutsaert, this column)
Surface LW upwelling389.2 W/m²—
Greenhouse effect Δ149.0 W/m²~150
LW cooling rate (mean)−0.92 K/day−1 to −2
CO₂ doubling band-4 response+52.6 % absorption—
Window transmission, dry column0.81window stays open when dry

(These are the numbers after the window recalibration described below. Before it they were OLR 229.1, surface downwelling 345.8 and greenhouse effect 160.9 — the first and third visibly too low and too high respectively, and the second too high by ~58 W/m² against the Brutsaert empirical relation for a column of this humidity, though it looked plausible against the global-mean 333 W/m² that belongs to a much moister atmosphere.)

Bug found and fixed during validation: at large absorber amounts the band transmission underflows to exactly 0, so log(T) returned -Inf and the optical depth became NaN. Added malkmus_log_transmission, which computes log T directly without ever forming T — a genuine robustness fix, not a test workaround.

Test expectation corrected: an early test asserted that transmission rises with pressure. That is backwards — pressure broadening widens lines and fills the gaps between them, so higher pressure means more absorption and lower transmission. The module was right and the test was wrong.

Accuracy bug found later by the DYCOMS-II RF01 case, and fixed. The version of Phase 32 that shipped first had two compensating errors in the 8-12 μm atmospheric window: water-vapour line strengths roughly 50× too large, and a water-vapour continuum roughly 15× too weak. Those two cancel almost exactly for one particular column, so the US Standard Atmosphere broadband fluxes in the table above looked fine — which is precisely why a single broadband number is not an adequate test of a band model.

They do not cancel in general, because the two terms scale differently: line absorption grows as $\sqrt{ku}$ in the strong-line limit, while the continuum grows as $u\,(e + \gamma p_{dry})$ — linear in amount and in water partial pressure. Applied to the dry 4.2 kg/m² column above the DYCOMS RF01 cloud top, the window emissivity came out at 0.38 where it should be about 0.12, adding ~27 W/m² of spurious downwelling and cutting the cloud-top radiative cooling — the single term that drives the entire RF01 boundary layer — nearly in half.

The fix replaces the ad-hoc continuum with the Roberts, Selby & Biberman (1976) parameterisation, which is calibrated against measurement and has the correct self/foreign split (γ = 0.002, so the self-continuum dominates wherever there is appreciable water), and rebalances the window line strengths against it. See examples/validation/dycoms_rf01_radiation.jl.

The lesson is recorded here because it generalises: a band model must be validated across columns that differ in absorber amount and pressure, not against a single atmosphere. Compensating errors are invisible to the latter and obvious to the former.

Phase 33 — Two-stream solver with cloud optics

src/TwoStream.jl. Three two-stream closures behind one interface — delta-Eddington, PIFM (Zdunkowski) and Quadrature — using the Meador & Weaver (1980) layer reflectance/transmittance coefficients, an adding/doubling column solver, maximum-random cloud overlap, and parameterised Mie cloud optics in terms of liquid/ice water path and effective radius. Full cloud-radiative feedback on the thermodynamics via radiative_heating!.

Numerical care that mattered. Meador & Weaver eq. 14 as written contains e^{kτ} factors that overflow for optically thick layers. The implementation divides numerator and denominator through by e^{kτ} analytically, so the expression is evaluated entirely in terms of e^{-kτ} ≤ 1 and nothing overflows even at τ = 10⁴.

The longwave layer source is linear in τ rather than constant. Measured against a sub-layered reference of 180.66 W/m², the linear form gives an error of 0.55 where the constant form gives 3.30 — a 6× accuracy gain for no extra cost.

Measured validation:

CheckResult
Conservative scattering, white surface: (out/in)0.99999999997
Cloud τ at LWP 100 g/m², r_eff 10 μm15.0 (textbook 15)
…its single-scattering albedo0.99999
…its asymmetry parameter0.86
Cloud albedo at LWP 10/50/200/1000 g/m²0.110 / 0.407 / … monotone, < 1
LW heating rate (min/mean/max)−2.48 / −1.00 / −0.089 K/day

Two real implementation bugs were found by the tests — worth recording because both produced plausible-looking output:

  1. A spurious trailing * k on the direct-beam reflectance and transmittance. Conservative scattering returned 1.5 W/m² instead of 800, and a thick cloud had an albedo of 0.022 instead of > 0.5. Diagnosed by re-deriving against Meador & Weaver eq. 14 term by term.
  2. S_up and S_dn swapped, with the wrong integral, in the linear-in-τ longwave source. This made the "improved" linear source worse than the constant one (error 6.03 vs 3.28), which is what gave it away. Fixed by deriving the moment integral properly: ∫₀^x s e^{-s} ds = 1 − e^{-x}(1+x).

Test expectations corrected (implementation was right): a reflectance floor of 1e-6 at τ = 10⁴ with ω = 0.999 ignores that the diffusion regime falls off only as 1/τ; and a 96 K/day heating rate came from an artificial grey optical depth, not from the solver — it drops below 30 K/day with real gas optics.

DYCOMS-II RF01 intercomparison: now implemented, in examples/validation/dycoms_rf01_radiation.jl. RF01 is nocturnal marine stratocumulus in which longwave cloud-top cooling is the only term driving the boundary layer, which makes it the sharpest available single-column test of a longwave scheme. The intercomparison prescribes the answer analytically — a net upward flux of F₀ = 70 W/m² at cloud top and F₁ = 22 W/m² at cloud base — so it is a genuine external check.

QuantityAeolusDYCOMS spec
Net upward LW at cloud base24.8 W/m²22.2
Net upward LW at cloud top70.3 W/m²70.1
Jump across cloud45.6 W/m²47.9 (−4.8 %)
Peak cooling rate−296 K/day, 2.5 m below cloud top—
Mean cooling, top 50 m−83.9 K/dayΔF/(ρc_pΔz) ⇒ −85

The sounding itself reproduces the specification: cloud top lands exactly on the 840 m inversion and peak liquid water is 0.472 g/kg against the RF01 reference of ≈0.45.

Running this case is what exposed the Phase 32 window-opacity bug recorded above — the first version of it disagreed with the prescribed cloud-top flux by 54 %.

Phase 34 — 3-D Monte-Carlo radiative transfer

Photon-tracing solver that handles the genuinely 3-D radiative environment of an urban canyon or a broken cloud field: side illumination, inter-reflection, and shadowing without the one-dimensional plane-parallel assumption. Runs as a periodic correction to the two-stream solution. Validated by: I3RC intercomparison cases; comparison of canyon absorption against the two-stream + SVF approximation.

Built (src/MonteCarloRT.jl): Amanatides–Woo voxel traversal, Henyey–Greenstein and Rayleigh phase sampling with closed-form inverse CDFs, implicit capture, Russian roulette and next-event estimation; every routine takes an rng, so runs are reproducible. Measured: against delta-Eddington over 16 cases the albedo differs by a median 0.0059 (worst 0.0223 at τ = 1, g = 0.85, μ₀ = 0.5, where the Eddington closure is weakest; tolerances from the computed standard error); the Beer–Lambert direct beam is exact to < 1e-12; energy closes to < 1e-10 per realisation with buildings; SE(N)/SE(4N) = 2.09. A cubic cloud absorbs 11.5 % less than the independent-column answer at overhead sun (side leakage) and 70.6 % more at μ₀ = 0.5 (illuminated side). Not done: the I3RC cases were not run.

Phase 35 — Full urban radiosity

Replace the sky-view-factor approximation with a view-factor matrix between every pair of urban surface patches (roofs, walls, ground), solved by radiosity iteration for both shortwave inter-reflection and longwave exchange. Includes specular treatment for glass. Validated by: energy-balance closure; comparison against measured façade temperatures from an urban field campaign (e.g. Basel BUBBLE).

Built (src/Radiosity.jl): facet decomposition, occlusion-tested view factors, direct and iterative radiosity solves for shortwave and longwave. Measured: view factors against closed forms 9.2e-15 (parallel) and 4.4e-15 (perpendicular); reciprocity 5.1e-17, closure 4.4e-16; canyon sky-view factor within 0.04–1.4 % of √((H/W)²+1) − H/W. Radiative trapping: material albedo 0.2 gives an effective canyon albedo of 0.152 / 0.127 / 0.106 / 0.095 at H/W = 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 4, and the canyon floor's longwave loss falls to 0.63 / 0.42 / 0.25 / 0.14 of a flat plate. The façade-temperature comparison against BUBBLE / CAPITOUL was done in Phase 49 (#30). Glass is Lambertian here; the angle-dependent glazing is Phase 49 (#27).

Phase 36 — Spectral surface albedo and BRDF

Wavelength-dependent albedo per material with a bidirectional reflectance distribution function, so low-sun-angle reflection off asphalt, glass, and vegetation is correct. Validated by: comparison against spectral-albedo measurements for common urban materials.

Built (src/SurfaceOptics.jl): 16 materials as reflectance curves integrated per band, four BRDF models, Cox–Munk water, Wiscombe–Warren snow. Measured: MODIS Ross-thick white-sky albedo 0.189186 against the published 0.189184, Li-sparse-reciprocal −1.377660 against −1.377622; reciprocity 8.3e-13 over 17 models × 2000 geometries; Lambertian black- and white-sky albedo = ρ to 1e-12; water albedo 0.030 overhead, 0.294 at 80°. Single source of truth (same commit): the three copies of the urban class materials (SurfaceOptics, Radiosity, the urban example) became one derived mosaic; the derived class albedos differ from the old hand-entered ones (street 0.126 vs 0.10, wall 0.227 vs 0.18). Expectations corrected: non-water classes are not zenith-independent (vegetation brightens 1.53× toward grazing), and the old magic-number assertions were replaced by tests of the derivation.

Phase 37 — Aerosol and gas radiative feedback

Couple the aerosol and chemistry state into the optics: aerosol direct effect, absorption by black carbon, and the radiative impact of ozone. Validated by: aerosol-optical-depth-to-radiative-forcing comparison against AERONET-constrained cases.

Built (src/AerosolOptics.jl): full Bohren–Huffman Mie series with Wiscombe downward recursion, log-normal size integration, κ-Köhler growth, OPAC mixtures. Measured: Mie Q_ext 2.881999 at x = 10, m = 1.5 (Wiscombe 2.8820) and 8.033538e-6 at x = 0.101, m = 0.75 (8.033542e-6); black carbon ω 0.209 and MAC 7.33 m² g⁻¹ (Bond & Bergstrom 0.2–0.3, 7.5 ± 1.2); Ångström exponent urban 1.44, desert dust 0.069; critical surface albedo 0.1338 against 0.1398 from the Chylek–Coakley formula. Not done: the AERONET forcing comparison, and coupling to a prognostic chemistry state (the Berlin column radiation has no aerosol until #142).

Phase 38 — Solar-position and cloud-shadow accuracy

src/SolarGeometry.jl. Apparent solar position from the standard astronomical series (Meeus ch. 25/22/12) including the equation of time, nutation in longitude, apparent obliquity, aberration and topocentric parallax; Bennett refraction scaled by station pressure and temperature; Kasten-Young air mass; Bird clear-sky irradiance; Erbs and Reindl direct/diffuse partitioning; and true 3-D cloud-shadow ray marching with combined cloud-plus-building attenuation in a single march. Validated: against the published NREL SPA reference case (2003-10-17, Golden CO) to Δzenith 0.0083°, Δazimuth 0.0036°; declination at both solstices and the March equinox to < 0.02°; equation of time spanning −14.2 to +16.4 min with the correct zero crossings; refraction 0.564° at the horizon and 1.0 arcmin at 45°; air mass exactly 1.0 at the zenith, 2.0 at 30° and 37.9 at the horizon (finite, where the plane-parallel 1/μ₀ diverges); TOA irradiance 1316.7 W/m² at aphelion and 1408.0 at perihelion; Berlin summer-solstice maximum elevation within 0.5° of the analytic 90 − φ + ε and solar noon at the correct UTC hour. The earlier sinusoidal declination it replaces is in error by up to 0.79° near the equinoxes. Accuracy is stated honestly: this is Meeus-grade (~0.01°), not full NREL SPA (±0.0003°). The full SPA needs ≈ 350 published VSOP87 coefficients; transcribing those from memory would risk silent errors, which is worse than a documented 0.01°. AbstractSolarPosition is the extension point for a table-carrying SPAPosition. For radiative transfer 0.01° is not limiting — the solar disc itself subtends 0.53°.

Clouds and microphysics

Move from the Kessler/two-moment scaffold to schemes that reproduce observed cloud microstructure and precipitation.

Status: all seven phases (39–45) are complete. Phases 39, 41 and 42 landed before the issue tracker (622184e, 63fc020); Phases 40 and 43–45 are issues #1–#14 (PRs #72–#89), plus the contract fix #82 (PR #91) and the P3 size-limit deduplication (PR #87). Milestone 4 has 15 closed issues and none open. The microphysics test set is 47,392 tests on master at 2895fed; about 29,850 of them are the 14,880-state enthalpy sweep of #91. Shared contract: src/MoistThermodynamics.jl, which every cloud scheme speaks and which also owns Köhler theory (after three drifted copies were merged, see Phase 42) and, since #91, the one enthalpy-conserving process adjustment.

Phase 39 — Production saturation adjustment and thermodynamics

Exact moist thermodynamics: liquid-water potential temperature as a prognostic, iterative saturation adjustment with the full latent-heat temperature dependence, and consistent treatment of moist buoyancy including condensate loading. Validated by: moist-adiabatic ascent against analytic parcel theory; conservation of moist static energy.

✅ SHIPPED (src/MoistThermodynamics.jl, 622184e; enthalpy adjustment #82 / PR #91). Temperature-dependent L(T) by Kirchhoff, es as the analytic Clausius–Clapeyron integral of that same L(T), cpm with vapour and condensate, exponential θli, density temperature, Bolton θe/θw, LCL, CAPE/CIN. Measured: Clausius–Clapeyron residual 5.2e-12 (liquid), 4.2e-12 (ice); es 611.21 Pa at 273.15 K, 2336.9 at 293.15 (ref. 2339), ice 103.28 at 253.15 (103.3); liquid–ice gap 27.00 Pa at 261.5 K; θli conserved to 9.9e-11 K over a 6 km saturated ascent; moist lapse rate 3.78 K/km at 300 K, 9.53 at 220 K. The boiling point comes out at 99,754 Pa (−1.55 %, the constant-c assumption, recorded rather than tuned). #91 added `isobaricsaturationadjustmentandtemperaturefrommoistenthalpy: over 14,880 states |Δh|/h ≤ 4.4e-16; the P3 heating ratio c_pmΔT/(L_vΔq_c) went 0.99848 / 1.0121 / 1.06442 → 1.00082 / 1.00085 / 1.00097 at 1000 / 900 / 600 hPa. **Bugs the tests caught**:saturationspecifichumidityreturned values > 1 past boiling (15.19 at 400 K), which made θ_e non-monotone and broke the wet-bulb bisection; and (#82, found by the P3 tests of #81) the contract's adjustment conserves θ_li, not enthalpy, adding 1.2 % latent heating at 900 hPa and 6.4 % at 600 hPa to every process that used it. **Expectations that were wrong**: a 2nd-order finite difference was measuring its own truncation (1.2e-8), not the physics; moist static energy is not an invariant of isobaric condensation (2.8 % drift over 6 km, 24 % of L·Δq per #85); #85's two@testbroken` compared fog with the θli diagnosis, which should not conserve enthalpy; and the first #91 assertion had the sign of the diagnosis/process gap wrong (the diagnosis condenses 1.75 % less).

Phase 40 — P3 microphysics

Implement the Morrison-Milbrandt P3 scheme: predicted particle properties, a single prognostic ice category with predicted rime mass and density, four-moment rain. Validated by: comparison against P3 reference implementation on idealised cases; radar-reflectivity comparison for a squall line.

✅ SHIPPED (src/P3Microphysics.jl, #1–#4 / PRs #72, #76, #81, #79, dedup PR #87). In-package Lanczos Γ and regularised incomplete Γ (gamma_inc_pq, renamed to avoid a SpecialFunctions collision); four-regime m(D)/A(D) with Dth, Dgr, Dcr solved in closed form from Frim and ρrim; Mitchell–Heymsfield fall speeds; KK2000 warm rain, ventilated deposition/evaporation, Cober–List rime density, melting and shedding; per-moment flux-form sedimentation with sub-stepping; rain and ice reflectivity. Measured: Γ against MPFR 2.0e-14 (x ∈ (0, 30)), incomplete Γ against MPFR 1.0e-13 (1 ≤ a ≤ 100); closed-form ρg against a 256-bit solve 5.1e-16; mass continuous across the regime thresholds (4.9e-12); total water over 3 regimes × 120 steps 7.8e-16; V(1 mm rain) 3.983 m/s (Gunn–Kinzer 4.03); Marshall–Palmer DSD at 5 mm/h 33.93 dBZ against MP Z = 200R^1.6 34.19 dBZ; sedimentation mass error 5.7e-16 at Courant 162. Rain mean-size limits are P3 v5.5.2's 10 μm–2 mm (#87), held in one place. Bugs the tests caught: gamma_psd_partial_moment went negative in narrow intervals (262 of 33,600 cases); underflow of the small-L series at Frim ≤ 1e-200; the MH05 turbulence correction applied to every particle (graupel peaked at 11 m/s, then 0); Nc set with the pre-adjustment density; a 0/0 in the limiter; sublimation and rain evaporation limited separately (qv/qsi overshot to 1.193); rain riming number not cut with its mass; two-moment sedimentation left the leading edge with mass and no number (Dm 4e4 m) until the λr limiter was added; and (#87) two copies of the rain size limits that disagreed (0.8 mm, P3's old value, vs 5 mm). Expectations that were wrong: P(a, 1e-6a) < 1e-5 is false for a < 1; the issue's 35–45 dBZ at 5 mm/h is too high (MP gives 34.2); a 1 % enthalpy expectation for the θ_li adjustment (the cause of #82); a hand-computed median-volume constant wrong in the 7th digit. Not done: a comparison against the P3 reference implementation and a squall-line radar comparison.

Phase 41 — Superdroplet Lagrangian microphysics

Monte-Carlo superdroplet method as an alternative to bulk schemes: individual particle attributes, stochastic collision-coalescence, explicit condensation growth. The reference against which bulk schemes are evaluated. Validated by: Golovin analytic collision-coalescence solution; comparison against bin-microphysics reference for a warm cumulus.

✅ SHIPPED (src/Superdroplet.jl, 63fc020). Shima et al. (2009) all-or-nothing stochastic coalescence, Golovin / hydrodynamic / gravitational kernels, implicit diffusional growth with Kelvin and Raoult terms, Eulerian coupling. Measured: against Golovin's solution (n₀ = 2²³ m⁻³, r̄ = 30.531 μm, 2¹⁵ superdroplets) L¹ error 0.037 at 1200 s, 0.080 at 2400 s, 0.118 at 3600 s; mass conserved to 5e-15; the error falls with Nsd. Bugs the tests caught: a growing pure-water droplet collapsed to the radius floor in one step (the backward-Euler residual is U-shaped, and the bracket was chosen at the wrong end; 5 μm at 13 % supersaturation went to 1e-10 m) — and the evaporating branch of the same solver had the same collapse, found only in Phase 45 (#13: Var(r²) was 61× the analytic value). Not done: the bin-microphysics warm-cumulus comparison. A finite-Nsd coalescence bias of ~2.5 % at N_sd = 2¹² remains (README limitations).

Phase 42 — Aerosol-cloud interaction

Prognostic aerosol with size distribution, Köhler-theory activation to cloud droplets, in-cloud processing, and regeneration on evaporation. The first and second indirect effects done properly. Validated by: activation-spectrum comparison against parcel-model reference; Twomey-effect sensitivity against observations.

✅ SHIPPED (src/AerosolCloud.jl, 63fc020). Abdul-Razzak & Ghan activation, an explicit adiabatic parcel reference, prognostic aerosol with activation / regeneration / in-cloud processing, Twomey and Albrecht diagnostics. Measured: over w = 0.1–5 m/s and Na = 100–1000 cm⁻³ ARG under-predicts Smax by 8–24 % (its known bias) and the activated fraction agrees with the parcel to 1–25 % (mean 7 %). One Köhler: AerosolOptics, Superdroplet and AerosolCloud each carried their own Köhler theory; they had drifted (σ = 0.0728 N/m at 298 K, where 0.0720 is measured, 1.2 % in scrit) and now delegate to MoistThermodynamics (agreement 1.6e-12). Bugs the tests caught: the parcel model reported zero activation always (it tested growth past rcrit at the instant of Smax instead of scrit < S_max); the Köhler critical-point bracket inverted for nearly insoluble particles. Not done: the Twomey-sensitivity comparison against observations.

Phase 43 — Ice nucleation

Heterogeneous ice nucleation with prognostic ice-nucleating-particle species (dust, biological), immersion and deposition freezing, secondary ice production (Hallett-Mossop). Validated by: comparison against cloud-chamber measurements; mixed-phase-cloud persistence in an Arctic stratus case.

✅ SHIPPED (src/IceNucleation.jl, #5–#7 / PRs #73, #77, #88). Prognostic dust and biological INP (available / consumed, closed budget, Stokes–Cunningham settling); Niemand 2012, Ullrich 2017 (immersion, deposition), DeMott 2015 and Tobo 2013 nucleation; Hallett– Mossop splintering in P3 (350 splinters per mg of rime, 10 μm splinters, P3's rimer conditions); nucleation coupled into the P3 ice category; a 1-D ISDAC-like mixed-phase stratus column. Measured: INP budget closure 9.0e-16 over 1500 random steps; settling 3.49e-3 / 0.304 cm/s at 1 / 10 μm (Seinfeld & Pandis 3.5e-3 / 0.31); Niemand ns 2.347e8 / 4.13e10 m⁻² at −20 / −30 °C; Ullrich/Niemand = e^0.424 at every T; Hallett–Mossop raises Ni ×115 in a 20 min −5 °C riming parcel; stratus initial LWP 14.38 g/m² (Paukert & Hoose ~14); the cloud persists 8 h with ×1 and ×10 INP and glaciates at 2.17 h with ×100 (after #91 the 8 h LWP is 35.59 g/m² with ×1 INP). Bugs the tests caught: singular nucleation moved 16,117 m⁻³ particles with zero mass; dust took ρp = 1770 from the ARG default instead of 2600; below Ticenuc deposition ran on INP immersed in drops (2.75e5 m⁻³ spurious crystals); splinter mass was 2 μm instead of P3's 10 μm (125×); column ice number exceeded the INP consumed by 3e-4 (density mismatch). Expectations that were wrong: a reviewer's 85–90 % largest-first mass fraction (94.0 %); the 25 μm drop restriction cutting splinters below 0.1 % (0.59 %, broad spectrum); a 1e-14 tolerance below the round-off of 253.15 − 273.15. Not done: cloud-chamber comparison; the stratus IWP was not compared with the ISDAC LES (paper not accessible), and the stratus longwave constants are not sourced.

Phase 44 — Precipitation, fog and visibility

Sedimentation with a proper terminal-velocity spectrum, sub-grid precipitation fraction, radiation fog with the correct droplet- radiative feedback, and a visibility diagnostic for transport applications. Validated by: fog onset/dissipation timing against a field campaign; drop-size-distribution comparison against disdrometer data.

✅ SHIPPED (#8–#11 / PRs #86, #89, #75, #85). Kessler rain now falls at the mass-weighted speed of its Marshall–Palmer spectrum through the conservative P3 sedimentation; sub-grid precipitation fraction with maximum-random overlap and four in-cell regions (src/p3/precip_fraction.jl); src/Visibility.jl (Koschmieder over Mie droplet, κ-Köhler haze and Rayleigh extinction); src/RadiationFog.jl (enthalpy-conserving fog adjustment, implicit dew into the surface budget, a 1-D FogColumn with droplet-radiative feedback through the radiation stack). Measured: Kessler fall speed within 0.1 % of Klemp & Wilhelmson at 1–3 g m⁻³; the old urban rain kernel created mass (×1.000678 in 20 s), the new one conserves exactly; fog visibility 171 / 108 / 83 m at LWC 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3 g m⁻³ (Kunkel 205 / 111 / 78); clear air 307 km (~340 from the Bucholtz tables: the GasOptics λ⁻⁴ fit is ~10 % high); ParisFog 2011: max LWP 92 g/m² (obs ~70), fog reaches the ground at 05:20 (obs 02:30), clears at 08:00 (obs ~10:00), CLW 30–35 g m⁻² h⁻¹ (Waersted 40–70); with the droplet feedback off the fog never reaches the ground. The old fog scheme heated a box model from 288 to 363 K in 24 h; the new one holds 288.0000 K. Bugs the tests caught: the vapour heat-capacity term counted twice in the isenthalpic solve; `fogequilibriumreturned the bracket midpoint when Newton hit g = 0 exactly (82 of 200,000 states); gamma-spectrum Mie extinction biased high for broad spectra (Q_eff 6.567 where ~2.12 is physical); the gamma quadrature aliased Mie resonances; the per-region limiter applied to summed rates (rain on 10 % of a cell removed 64.5 % of q_c); the fog column's default dt = 10 s was not converged (onset moved by 55 min). **Expectations that were wrong**: conserving moist static energy (24 % error;moist_enthalpy` added instead); |Q − 2| < 1e-3 at r = 5 mm; a pointwise x^(−2/3) Mie slope; the bare f^0.32 autoconversion factor; random overlap of a contiguous deck; the issue premise that the old fog scheme blew up the urban run (it drained qv; the blow-up came from the unstable moisture advection, replaced by WENO-Z5). Not done: disdrometer DSD comparison; onset is 2.8 h late and dissipation ~2 h early against ParisFog.

Phase 45 — Turbulence-microphysics interaction

Sub-grid supersaturation fluctuations and their effect on droplet growth and activation — the mechanism by which entrainment broadens droplet spectra. Validated by: spectral-broadening comparison against DNS and superdroplet reference.

✅ SHIPPED (src/TurbulentMicrophysics.jl, #12–#14 / PRs #74, #78, #84). Sub-grid saturation-deficit PDF (Gaussian and Larson double Gaussian) from the local-equilibrium SGS variance; activation averaged over that PDF; per-superdroplet Ornstein–Uhlenbeck supersaturation fluctuations; a bulk spectral-moment scheme validated against the superdroplet reference. Measured: σs → 0 reproduces the contract adjustment exactly (==); condensate +0.16 / +0.48 / +1.58 % against 2-D quadrature of the exact adjustment at σqt = 0.1 / 0.3 / 1 g/kg; Nd at w = 0.5 m/s 182.31 → 171.36 cm⁻³ as σS goes 0 → 0.1 %; Var(r²) / analytic OU 0.991–1.017; bulk vs superdroplet ε within 1 % for σS ≤ 0.1 % and 3.5–4.1 % low at 0.2 % (the gamma closure); width growth σr² ∝ t^0.5142 (bulk) and t^0.5155 (superdroplet) against the OU 0.5142 and the DNS t^½ law. Bugs the tests caught: Mellor's a used the bare L/cpm (≈1 % bias that did not vanish as σ → 0); the evaporating superdroplet collapse of Phase 41; per-cell stepping aged every OU fluctuation once per cell (τ became τ/K); the double Gaussian returned NaN for |Sk| ≳ 1e8. Expectations that were wrong: cloud fraction rising monotonically with skewness; O(Sk) rather than O(Sk^{1/3}) convergence to the Gaussian; NCCN = N at S = 10 %; ε narrowing for 100 s without fluctuations (S̄ is used up); a guessed ε ratio of 1.5 (measured 1.33); five more in #84, none of them code. Not done: digitised DNS data (the DNS target is the analytic OU broadening law).

Land, urban and surface exchange

Make the surface as good as the atmosphere. Currently the biggest source of error in the urban runs.

Status: all eight phases (46–53) and the shared contract are complete — issues #15–#43 and #92, PRs #93–#122. Milestone 5 has 30 closed issues and none open. The surface test set is 19,326 tests (6 @test_broken) on master at 2895fed. Shared contract (src/SurfaceExchange.jl, #92 / PR #93): material constants that read latent heats, cp and g from MoistThermodynamics rather than restating them; Monin–Obukhov similarity as a dispatch hierarchy (Businger–Dyer / Paulson unstable, Beljaars–Holtslag stable) solved for ζ by a bracketed Illinois iteration; and the AbstractSurface interface (`surfacetemperature,roughnesslengths,stepsurface!, the RadiationCore albedo and emissivity generics) that every land and urban surface implements. Measured: neutral C_D 2.7e-16 from (κ/ln(z/z0))²; ψ against Paulson and Beljaars– Holtslag to 6 digits; returned L against the Obukhov length of its own fluxes 9.1e-15; a very stable case (ΔT = −10 K, U = 2 m/s) converges in 8 iterations. Expectation that was wrong: Ri_B = ζ C_D/C_H (the identity is ζΦ_h/Φ_m²). Since the milestone closed, the Berlin case runs this contract in every column (MOST +SoilThermalColumn`, #129 / PR #158) — which found a src bug recorded under Phase 46 — and the urban facet system got a matrix-free GMRES solve (#138 / PR #163, Phase 49).

Phase 46 — Multi-layer soil and hydrology

CLM-style soil column: 10+ layers with Richards-equation water transport, temperature-dependent hydraulic conductivity, freeze-thaw, and a water table. Validated by: soil-temperature and moisture profiles against FLUXNET site observations.

✅ SHIPPED (src/SoilColumn.jl, #15–#18 / PRs #98, #109, #107, #117). CLM5 / CLM-exponential layering; Clapp–Hornberger, van Genuchten–Mualem (with the Vogel/Ippisch air entry) and Gardner hydraulics; mixed-form Richards solved by modified Picard with water conserved by construction; temperature-dependent K via water viscosity; Johansen thermal properties, enthalpy-form heat with freeze–thaw and ice impedance; SoilThermalColumn <: AbstractSurface; a SIMTOP water table / unconfined aquifer; the production column under the urban example (replacing its three-layer soil). Measured: exact Gardner infiltration 1.36e-4 → 3.86e-5 (50 → 100 layers, order 1.8); 30-day mixed budget 1.3e-15 m per step; Neumann freezing front −1.15e-3 relative at 10 mm (first order); diurnal damping-depth amplitude 0.3693 vs 0.3703; energy residual over 40 freeze–thaw days ≤ 6.0e-8 J m⁻²; water-table recession 1 step vs 2000 steps identical to 1e-12; soil + aquifer drift 4.9e-14 m over 6 years; in the 24 h Berlin run the production soil lowered street and roof Ts extremes by 0.3–0.6 K (heat island 18.4 → 17.9 K). Bugs the tests caught: Picard chatter near saturation (4,136 sub-steps per 5 min; fixed with air entry, HYDRUS criteria and under-relaxation, now 5); the arithmetic interface mean is first order (logarithmic now default); a 3-term viscosity correlation 1.2 % off at 80 °C; example SoilGrid type piracy once SoilGrid was exported. Found later, in the Berlin integration (#129): SoilThermalColumn.step_surface! returned the evaporation the skin balance charged even when the soil delivered less (≈ 70 of 184 W m⁻² of street LE was water the soil never lost); fixed in src. Expectations that were wrong: the Parlange sorptivity integral stopping at the air-entry potential (12 % off); "Parlange within 1–2 %" (8.5 % for constant D); an upward flux beyond Gardner's maximum capillary rise; hydrostatic equilibrium of a dry column in 2 years (6 needed); sine forcing from t = 0 biasing a harmonic projection. Not done: soil temperature / moisture profiles against FLUXNET (PLUMBER2 carries no soil water; see Phase 47 for the flux comparison).

Phase 47 — Vegetation photosynthesis and stomatal conductance

Farquhar photosynthesis coupled to Ball-Berry / Medlyn stomatal conductance, so transpiration responds to light, CO₂, humidity and soil water stress rather than a fixed availability factor. Validated by: latent- and sensible-heat-flux comparison against FLUXNET eddy-covariance towers across several biomes.

✅ SHIPPED (src/Photosynthesis.jl, src/LandSurface.jl, #19–#21 / PRs #95, #105, #116). Farquhar C3 / Collatz C4 with Bernacchi and Kattge–Knorr temperature responses and the CLM4.5 PFT table; Ball–Berry and Medlyn stomata solved exactly for the leaf-surface humidity, coupled with An in one bracketed residual; a two-big-leaf vegetated column over SoilThermalColumn with Zeng root fractions and CLM water stress. Measured: Kc/Ko/Γ* temperature shape against Bernacchi 1.3e-4; An = gc(ca − ci) over 3,456 cases 2.4e-11, all converged; C3 ci/ca mean 0.719; against five FLUXNET2015 towers (PLUMBER2, no calibration) hourly LE r = 0.83–0.94 and Rn r ≥ 0.98, LE bias +7.9 (GF-Guy) to +87 W m⁻² (DE-Gri); the column does not beat the PLUMBER "1lin" benchmark in RMSE at any site (as for the LSMs of Best et al. 2015). Bugs the tests caught: the Medlyn deficit bound on air rather than leaf-surface deficit ran away to gs ≈ 10³ mol m⁻² s⁻¹ (now max(Ds, Dmin)); βt > 1 by one ulp; Newton cycling on the dew switch (leaf residuals up to 40 W m⁻²); an oscillating outer canopy iteration; NaN at LAI = 0; Kn transcribed as 0.11 instead of CLM4.5's 0.30 (daytime EF at DE-Hai 0.862 → 0.786). Expectations that were wrong: peaked Vcmax agreeing with Bernacchi within 2 % (the Bonan-2011 deactivation removes 13 % at 25 °C); TPU limitation with CLM defaults (impossible: Jmax/4 < 3Tp); C4 ci/ca stable when ca doubles; a 0.01 K outer-iteration bound set from one site. Not done: GPP and soil moisture against observations; the FR-Pue drought response has the right sign but the midday Bowen ratio is 3–5× too low.

Resolved-canopy drag with a leaf-area-density profile, implicit treatment so it is unconditionally stable, radiation attenuation through the canopy, and leaf-energy-balance for leaf temperature. Phase 49 — Production urban canopy model

Full urban-canopy model matching or exceeding PALM's USM: per-facet (roof / wall / ground) multi-layer wall conduction, window transmission, anthropogenic heat with a real building-energy model, and street-canyon-resolved radiation coupled to the radiation stack's radiosity. Validated by: façade and surface temperatures against BUBBLE / CAPITOUL urban field-campaign data.

✅ SHIPPED (src/UrbanSurface.jl + BuildingEnergy coupling, #26–#30 / PRs #94, #102, #103, #121, #122; GMRES #138 / PR #163). A cited 16-material thermal table (optics derived from the SurfaceOptics library, not restated); a θ-scheme conduction core; UrbanFacet <: AbstractSurface with the skin balance linearised into a Robin condition; Fresnel / Beer–Lambert glazing, multi-pane units with ISO 15099 gaps, WindowFacet; implicit facet–facet longwave through the radiosity response J; building heat release from the Phase 50 model replacing the prescribed anthropogenic heat; TEB canyon wind and Rowley convection for the validation driver. Measured: ISO 6946 composite wall to 1e-10; asphalt admittance 1204.04 (Heisler & Brazel 1204); 3 mm clear float T/R/A 0.833/0.076/0.091 and SHGC 0.857 (NFRC 0.83/0.08/ 0.09, 0.86); 6-13-6 U 2.707 / 2.547 W m⁻² K⁻¹ (air / argon; Vitro 2.669 / 2.555); implicit longwave coupling conserves to 7e-15 W/m² where the lagged one leaks 14.7 W/m²; CAPITOUL road and wall mean temperature +0.06 / −0.03 K over 2,304 hourly samples (TEB RMSE 3.09 / 1.22); BUBBLE Q* 170.0 vs 179 W m⁻² (TEB 170); CAPITOUL winter building energy use 65.7 W m⁻² vs the inventory's 58.6 (+12 %; TEB-BEM −15 %). GMRES (#138): 1.4e-12 K against dense LU in 6–7 iterations; the Berlin scene costs 0.31 s per step at 5,810 facets (LU 4.54 s). Each iteration is O(n²): from 5,810 to 7,524 facets the solve time grew 0.112 → 0.196 s, a ratio of 1.75 against (7524/5810)² = 1.68, at 7 iterations each (an implied exponent of ln 1.75 / ln 1.295 ≈ 2.2 from these two sizes). Bugs the tests caught: θ < ½ accepted (Ts ran away to 1e8 K); the h ≥ 0 check bypassed by the default constructor; a singular inter-pane system at grazing sun; the example heated glass with the light passing through it; the example's prescribed anthropogenic heat never reached the air — it cooled the surfaces by up to 40 W/m². Expectations that were wrong: 8 cm EPS carrying > 80 % of the ΔT (76.9 %); ISO 13786 within 1 % at 2 cm cells (spatial truncation, second order); the first step returning fluxes at the initial skin; a 6 mm pane held to the 3 mm angular tolerance; Fresnel reflectance monotone from 0°; a linearised NFRC reference (+3.2 % → +1.4 %). Incropera's asphalt k = 0.062 was rejected as a source. Failures recorded as @test_broken, not loosened: CAPITOUL roof +3.93 K (TEB +1.40; the observed roof is pitched and its construction unknown); BUBBLE S↑ misses TEB's tolerance by 0.3 W m⁻² after the forcing was corrected; BUBBLE daytime L↑ +39 W m⁻² (no vegetation in the source area). Not done: per-sample RMSE (campaign series not public), per-wall CAPITOUL values (figures only), a canyon-air model.

Phase 50 — Building energy model coupling

Two-way coupled building interior: indoor air temperature, HVAC demand and its exhaust heat, thermal mass, and occupancy schedules. The AC-feedback loop that amplifies urban heat islands. Validated by: measured building energy demand vs simulated for an instrumented building.

✅ SHIPPED (src/BuildingEnergy.jl, #31–#33 / PRs #104, #111, #118). One thermal zone with a prognostic air node solved implicitly with every ConductionColumn / UrbanFacet of its envelope; the ISO 13790 5R1C reference; ideal-loads HVAC with the EnergyPlus DX biquadratic COP and capacity curves, condenser exhaust (air-cooled, evaporative, boiler, heat pump), dehumidification; DOE medium-office schedules and internal gains. Measured: concrete-slab first mode τ₁ 42.43 h against the plane-wall 42.42 h; zone energy over 10 days 3.3e-14; AC–canyon feedback amplification 1/(1 − g) = 1.3621 exactly; ANSI/ASHRAE 140-2020 (BESTEST, Denver TMY3): incident solar 5/5 inside the reference range with Perez (2/5 isotropic), annual loads 19 of 20 inside the six-program range (Case 395 heating 0.6 % low), peaks 16 of

  1. Bugs the tests caught: a driver albedo cap that made walls absorb

50 % of the sun; a COP check dividing by the wrong quantity. Expectations that were wrong: a 40-day spin-up for a light box (slowest mode τ ≈ 18 d); monotone DX capacity over the whole fitted range (the packaged curve peaks at 19.6 °C); BESTEST peak count ≥ 36 of 40 (there are 20); the Standard's combined exterior coefficients being adequate (they put 6 of 10 heating loads below range). Not met: the phase target — no measured demand of an instrumented building was available, and BESTEST is program-to-program.

Phase 51 — Snow, ice and frozen surfaces

Multi-layer snowpack with compaction, metamorphism, albedo ageing, liquid-water percolation and refreezing; lake and sea-ice thermodynamics. Validated by: snow-depth and albedo evolution against SnowMIP reference sites.

✅ SHIPPED (src/Snowpack.jl, src/IceThermodynamics.jl, #34–#37 / PRs #108, #114, #115, #99). Layered snowpack with Anderson / Vionnet compaction and four conductivity laws, mass/depth/enthalpy-conserving relayering, AbstractSurface; Flanner–Zender dry ageing from CLM's own SNICAR table (read with the in-package NetCDF reader), Brun wet ageing, albedo through SurfaceOptics' Wiscombe–Warren snow; Coléou–Lesaffre retention, bucket and Colbeck kinematic-wave percolation, enthalpy-exact refreezing; Bitz–Lipscomb (1999) brine-pocket sea and lake ice. Measured: 12-day storm/melt run mass 1.6e-13 kg m⁻², enthalpy 1.4e-7 J m⁻²; seasonal bulk density within 25 % of Sturm et al. (2010) alpine climatology (−17 / −19 % by late March); grain growth within 0.1 % of a literal port of CTSM SnowAge_grain; Summit SSA after 37 d 23.8 m² kg⁻¹ (observed 23 ± 3, assumed temperature range); kinematic front arrival 7.35 h vs 7.37 h analytic; Stefan ice growth 0.035 %; MU71 sea ice first annual cycle within 0.3 cm of the BL99 reference code, 100-yr equilibrium 264 cm vs BL99 Table 1's 281 (the 6 % offset traced to one boundary stencil in the reference). Bugs the tests caught: a layer merge at T_f lost enthalpy; a pack sublimating away returned more vapour than it held (0.29 vs 0.20 kg m⁻²); trilinear interpolation of the jointly fitted SNICAR parameters (484 μm where the nodes give 123–181); gravity drainage counting the first refreeze twice; 0/0 brine heat capacity for fresh ice at 0 °C; an albedo jump inside the surface Newton. Expectations that were wrong: fresh-snow depth exactly the fall depth (it settles 0.75 % within the step); a hand-computed wet-growth radius; the Neumann small-St expansion (1 − St/6, not /12); a basal-melt rate formula that holds only for accretion. Not done: SnowMIP sites; albedo of old melting snow (needs impurity deposition).

Phase 52 — Water surfaces and wave coupling

Lake and coastal-sea surface with a mixed-layer model, wave-dependent roughness (Charnock and beyond), sea-spray heat flux, and a diurnal warm-layer scheme. Validated by: sea-surface-temperature diurnal cycle against buoy observations; air-sea flux comparison against COARE.

✅ SHIPPED (src/OceanMixedLayer.jl, src/AirSeaFlux.jl, #38–#40 / PRs #97, #100, #112). Layered water column with EOS-80, Jerlov shortwave, Kraus–Turner entrainment, Fairall cool skin and Fairall / Zeng–Beljaars warm layers; Charnock, COARE 3.0/3.5, wave-age and sea-state roughness with COARE stability and gustiness; Andreas (2015) sea spray with the microphysics delegated to MoistThermodynamics and Superdroplet; rain heat flux; the full COARE loop. Measured: cool skin against the COARE 3.6 code on its ship data 9.2e-5 K (653 records); fresh-water density maximum 3.9817 °C; Kato–Phillips deepening −0.017 %; COARE 3.0 CD10N at 20 m/s 2.071 × 10⁻³ (Fairall 2003: 2.06); against the NOAA COARE 3.5 reference file u* 0.27 %, LE 0.25 %, H 1.7 % (the moist cpm; 0.23 % with dry cp); full algorithm LE within 1.51 W m⁻²; TOGA COARE peak afternoon warming (Fairall 1996a, 42 days) r = 0.953, RMSE 0.246 K with the Fairall scheme, 0.50 K with Zeng–Beljaars. Bugs the tests caught: the u* bracket of `neutraltransfercoefficients` (negative COARE 3.5 α below 2.9 m/s, a second unstable Oost root); a 0/0 in the gustiness convergence check. Expectations that were wrong: COARE's own shipped output file (it disagrees with COARE's code by 0.24 K); a 1e-12 energy tolerance against a 4e10 J m⁻² store; free convection at U = 0.5 m/s (not ≪ wg); spray tolerances against digitised curves. Open: the default fully developed sea makes the spray latent flux 1.76× Andreas's figure at 25 m/s — pass a SeaState.

Phase 53 — Surface heterogeneity and blending

Sub-grid tile approach with a blending-height formulation so a single grid cell can contain multiple surface types and still produce the right aggregate flux and the right internal boundary layers. Validated by: heterogeneous-surface LES against homogeneous-tile aggregation; observations from patchy terrain campaigns.

✅ SHIPPED (src/SurfaceTiles.jl, #41–#43 / PRs #101, #110, #120). SurfaceMosaic (flux aggregation over any AbstractSurface tiles, nestable), parameter aggregation for comparison, blending-height effective roughness with Mason / Claussen / Bou-Zeid / IBL closures and BlendedMosaic; a patchy-strip LES driver. Measured: a single tile is bit-identical to the bare surface; parameter aggregation gets the sign of H wrong for a stable + unstable pair (+43.6 vs −24.1 W m⁻²); effective z0 against the Bou-Zeid et al. (2007) LES within 15 % in all 8 cases (rms ln error 0.074; Mason 0.052; log mean 0.193); a priori, tiles with patch-mean air are within 2.5 % of the resolved strip-LES flux and parameter aggregation 22–37 % low; a posteriori the tiled LES is +4.2 % / −8.0 % (calm) and +1.4 % (Ug = 5 m/s). Bugs the tests caught: an export collision with SurfaceOptics' TiledSurface (renamed SurfaceMosaic); a signed zero breaking bit-identity; the interrupted blended_forcing draft's closure overwriting its own residual (every Jacobian column zero). Expectations that were wrong: fractions summing exactly to 1; Claussen blending higher than Bou-Zeid; single-tile split exact under stability; the domain-mean wind vector as grid-cell forcing (+16–34 % in free convection, uniform surface included). Not done: field observations (LITFASS); the published strip LES was compared only qualitatively.

Turbulence closure

The SGS model is the heart of LES. Make it defensible.

Status: all six phases (54–59) are merged. That is the 29 issues #167–#195, merged as PRs #203–#231 on 2026-10-04/05, plus six follow-ups from the Technical-debt milestone (#232–#237 / PRs #240–#244, #271). Milestone 6 is still open: its six phase tracking issues #44–#49 have no open work left, but agents do not close issues, so closing them and the milestone is left to the maintainer. The turbulence-closure work adds five src modules: TurbulenceStability, WallModels, LargeScaleForcing, PrognosticSGS and Transition. The closures themselves live in Operators. The groundwork from before it (#132 / PR #160) moved the local Germano–Lilly scaffold into Operators after its Mij sign was found wrong (Cs = 0 everywhere).

Berlin defaults after the milestone. The :sgs slot runs the Deardorff TKE closure (:tke), with the stress-form divergence, the diagonal scalar diffusivity, and qv and every tracer diffused with κt. Every eddy-viscosity provider is wrapped in StabilityCorrected (:brown94). TKE handles stratification itself, so it is not wrapped, by design (#233). The ground wall model takes a time-filtered input. The facade wall model is on, and TKE shear production at the facades matches the facade stress (#232). The :geostrophic coupling uses the geostrophic pressure gradient, with the sponge retargeted at Ug. turbulence_diag writes N², Rig, fm, γ, the supercritical fraction and the relaminarised fraction. The surface exchange evaluates the maximum sustainable heat flux in every column. The following are options, not defaults: LASD (the default of the :dynamic provider), AMD, Vreman, σ, the Reynolds-stress closure, the isentropic tensor, the TBLE wall model and the dynamic z0.

Open after the milestone (Technical debt, not the Turbulence closure milestone): #238, Berlin nights are implausible (37 °C at 03:00, heat island 27.7 K, u* ≈ 0.001 m/s); #239, N² ignores condensate loading and saturated stability.

Phase 54 — Production dynamic and scale-dependent SGS models

Lagrangian-averaged scale-dependent dynamic Smagorinsky (Bou-Zeid, Meneveau & Parlange), fully validated, replacing the local Germano-Lilly scaffold. Includes the correct near-wall behaviour without ad-hoc damping. Validated by: log-law recovery with no wall-damping function; resolved-scale spectra against DNS.

✅ SHIPPED (src/operators/sgs_closures.jl and the Lagrangian model in Operators, #167, #168, #173, #174, #186 / PRs #207, #205, #212, #216, #224). What was built:

  • SGS closure contract. AbstractSGSClosure, a κ_t field and θ-aware closures. A closure commits once per accepted step on every core (Euler, RK3, IMEX, the anelastic core, and adaptive stepping with rollback on a rejected try).
  • LES toolbox. Box, Gaussian and spectral filters, the isotropic energy spectrum, a solenoidal initialiser and the Comte-Bellot & Corrsin (CBC) benchmark.
  • Lagrangian dynamic Smagorinsky (Meneveau, Lund & Cabot 1996), aware of the solid mask.
  • Scale-dependent LASD (Bou-Zeid et al. 2005), with a 5-cell second filter and a coefficient cadence.
  • Channel and half-channel case builders, with Φ_m, total-stress and E11 diagnostics.

Measured:

  • The contract reproduces the old Smagorinsky paths bit for bit over 50 steps of three steppers, at 0.9 % overhead.
  • CBC at 32³, dynamic: K98/K42 +13.2 % and K171/K42 −3.6 %. At 64³: −4.9 % and −17.6 %.
  • In isotropic turbulence the Lagrangian C_s is 0.1500, against the plane-averaged 0.1539, at t = 3.
  • Half channel with Smagorinsky 0.17: the near-wall Φ_m overshoot peaks at 1.629 (1.45–1.65 over 12 seeds).
  • LASD at 24³ with the 2Δ input filter, without wall damping: Φm stays in 0.934–1.151 over 0.05–0.3 H (0.961–1.094 at 48³), and ⟨τw⟩ is 1.028 u*². Smagorinsky 0.1 at 32³ gives 0.965–1.589.
  • Berlin :dynamic with LASD at cadence 4: C_s 0.095 in the canopy and 0.089 in the free stream (LASI: 0.037 / 0.033), at 25–33 ms per step on the 64×64×32 mesh.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • The spectrum measured the centre average of the staggered velocities. As a result, fresh CBC fields carried 22.0 % too much kinetic energy (13.7 % at 64³).
  • The masked Germano filter allocated 125 MB per call.
  • The 5-cell LASD filter reached into the mirrored halo at a free-slip wall. That gave a spurious β ≈ 1 at the second level.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • The 32³ CBC pass of K98 within ±10 % depended on the spectrum defect. The measured value is now +13.2 %, kept as @test_broken.
  • β was tested as an arithmetic mean. The right statistics are the geometric mean (1.03–1.06) and the median.
  • "LASD Cs within 10 % of LASI" fails on the domain mean (+10.5 to +14.2 %), because C²Δ is convex in a noisy φ.
  • "β → 1 above z/H ≈ 0.2": β crosses 1 about 10 cells from the wall, wherever that falls in z/H.
  • Exactly zero ν_t in laminar shear for the Lagrangian model: it decays geometrically, to 1.4e-8 of the start.

Not met:

  • Resolved spectra were compared only with CBC, which is an experiment, not DNS.
  • The k⁻⁵ᐟ³ range of E11 is not reproduced at 32³–48³ (fitted slopes −4.27 and −3.48).
  • K171 within ±10 % fails for every closure but AMD at 32³.
  • The −17.6 % K171 deficit at 64³ is unexplained.
  • LASD is the default of a non-default provider.

Phase 55 — Anisotropic and tensorial eddy viscosity

Anisotropic minimum-dissipation (AMD) and Vreman models, plus a full tensorial eddy-diffusivity formulation for strongly stratified flow where vertical and horizontal mixing differ by orders of magnitude. Validated by: stably stratified channel against DNS; GABLS1 intercomparison.

✅ SHIPPED (Operators, WallModels channel cases, examples/sbl/driver.jl; #175, #181, #176, #187, #191 / PRs #214, #218, #215, #225, #228). What was built:

  • Stress-form SGS divergence ∂j(2νij Sij) with separate (νh, νv), a generic `addstressdivergence!, ansgsstress` keyword on every core, the IMEX split, and the stress-form diffusive CFL limit.
  • Tensorial scalar diffusion: a diagonal (κh, κv) operator, a general symmetric K_ij in triad form, and a rotated isentropic tensor with a slope taper.
  • AMD (with its scalar and buoyancy terms) and Vreman closures.
