  • StabilityCorrected, which wraps any eddy-viscosity closure with Rig stability functions from TurbulenceStability, anisotropic length scales and Prt(Ri).
  • A shared stable-boundary-layer (SBL) driver, used by GABLS1 and the stratified channel. BusingerDyerLogLinear moved into SurfaceExchange so that there is one copy.

Measured:

  • Stress form: solid-body rotation gives zero tendency (the Laplacian form gives 2.33), and the discrete dissipation matches to 2.8e-16.
  • The stress form's spectral radius is 8νΣΔ⁻², twice the Laplacian's. The corrected CFL limit costs one extra step in 1,647 over a Berlin hour.
  • The isentropic tensor spreads a blob at 0.9977 / 1.0067 of 2κt along / across the isentropes.
  • AMD is exactly zero in laminar shear and reaches zero at the critical N²*. In CBC, AMD gives +9.9 % / −4.7 %, the first closure to meet ±10 % on K171. Vreman gives +4.9 % / −12.7 %.
  • StabilityCorrected:
    • Neutral case bit for bit with the base closure; analytic stability functions to 6.1e-14.
    • Internal wave: ω within 0.46 % of N kx/|k|, with νv ≡ 0.
    • Breaking wave: 23.4 % of the energy lost in 3 periods, against 5.5 % resolved only.
    • Berlin: νv in the capping inversion is 2.3e-5 m²/s, against 8.1e-3 uncorrected. The flagship night mean κv/κ_h is 0.016. Cost: +8 % per step, +5 % over the 21 h run.
  • GABLS1 at 64³, hours 8–9, Brown 1994:
    • h 196.4 m (reference about 200).
    • u* 0.284 m/s, 0.4 % above the range's upper end 0.283.
    • w'θ'₀ −0.0134 K m/s, inside −0.0148 to −0.0094.
    • Low-level jet 9.49 m/s at 191 m.
    • At 32³, u* is 4–7 % above the range.
  • Stably stratified channel at Re_τ = 180, 32×32×48:
    • Ub⁺ within 4.4 % of García-Villalba & del Álamo neutral, and within 1.4 % / 2.7 % at Riτ = 18.
    • Nu is over-predicted by 15 % and 9 % / 14 %.
    • Nu(18)/Nu(0) is 0.63 / 0.67, against the DNS 0.667.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • Vreman's B_β written as a sum of minors left 1e-10 m²/s on a two-component shear. It is now in Cauchy–Binet form.
  • IMEX froze θ's κ_t for qv under AMD.
  • step! diffused θ with κh only while `stepimex!` used (κh, κv), a relative error of 1.0.
  • The Laplacian-form CFL limit was 2× too permissive for the unprojected stress form.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • "AMD ν_t falls monotonically with N²" holds only where ∂w/∂z < 0.
  • "Breaking wave loses more than 30 %": measured 23.4 %.
  • "νv = νh where Ri ≤ 0" ignored the anisotropic length scales.
  • "The corrected closure gives a lower GABLS1 BL": it is deeper, 196.4 m against 191.1 m.
  • "The corrected channel is closer to the DNS": it is not, at CI resolution.
  • A Berlin dt_diff check used the Laplacian-form limit for the stress form.

Not met:

  • At Ri_τ = 60 the channel relaminarises (Nu 1.23, against the DNS 2.80). We attribute this to resolution.
  • The DNS at Re_τ = 550 is unaffordable wall-resolved.
  • AMD's CBC K98 is asserted to ±13 % (the reference spectrum is uncertain by about 3 %).

Phase 56 — Prognostic SGS energy and stress

Deardorff prognostic TKE and, beyond it, a full differential Reynolds-stress SGS model for the cases where eddy viscosity fails (rapid rotation, strong stratification, flow around sharp obstacles). Validated by: SGS-stress comparison against filtered DNS fields.

✅ SHIPPED (src/PrognosticSGS.jl, the a-priori harness in Operators; #177, #182, #178, #188, #192 / PRs #213, #219, #208, #223, #227; follow-ups #232 / PR #242, #235 / PR #244). What was built:

  • SGS diffusion of qv and of every Berlin tracer with κ_t, aware of the solid mask. Before this, that physics was missing.
  • Deardorff / Moeng TKE closure, plus wall_distance. It is the Berlin :sgs default.
  • A-priori harness: exact subfilter stress; Smagorinsky, Bardina and gradient model stresses; metrics. It has a 96³ DNS fixture in the suite and an offline 256³ record.
  • Differential SGS Reynolds-stress closure (Deardorff 1973; LRR-IP; Schumann realizability), as an option.
  • Validation against filtered DNS (isotropic, rotating and stratified decay) and a surface-mounted cube.

Measured:

  • qv diffusion matches the discrete factor to 1.8e-15. The old code gave a relative error of 2.3.
  • Berlin with tracer diffusion: ground-level pollutant maximum −27 %, cost +4.5 %.
  • TKE closure:
    • Neutral equilibrium Cs is 0.194413 = ck^{3/4} c_ε^{-1/4}.
    • Ri_c is 0.23313.
    • Energy exchange closes to 2e-16.
    • 64³ CBL: σw² peaks at 0.459 w*² at 0.351 zi, against Lenschow et al.'s 0.466 at 0.313 (RMS difference 0.035).
  • A priori at 256³ (Reλ 78.6): Smagorinsky ρΠ(4h) 0.832, matched Cs 0.099, Bardina ρΠ 0.956, backscatter 21.1 %.
  • RSM: the shear equilibrium is exact to 1e-10, and the rotation invariants hold to 1e-13.
  • Against filtered DNS (48³ DNS, 12³ LES):
    • Rotating decay, KE error: RSM +6.4 %, Smagorinsky −11.9 %. At 96³ the RSM error is 1.4 %.
    • Stratified decay, τ₃₃/τ₁₁: DNS 0.700, RSM 0.762, TKE 1.002.
  • Surface-mounted cube, x_R/H: Smagorinsky 1.82, TKE 1.80, RSM 1.97, against 1.612 (Martinuzzi & Tropea 1993).
  • In Berlin, RSM costs about 2× TKE, so TKE stays the default.
  • With the facade fix (#232): canopy shear production +71 %, near-facade ν_t +5.3 %.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • step! and step_rk3! never diffused qv, and moist step_rk3! advanced qv with three Euler steps.
  • A backward-Euler RSM source flipped the sign of growing modes. The realizability projection then added 3.96e4 m⁵/s² of energy.
  • The literal 2e0 in the constructor made every RSM closure built with e0 start at e = 1 m²/s².
  • germano_lilly_cs raised a DomainError.
  • A Berlin sgs_dissipation shadowed an export.
  • TKE production at wall-modelled facades booked a stencil flux the step does not apply: 27.3 % of canopy production (#232).
  • The 256³ record failed Parseval (0.9967) because of the pre-#205 spectrum (#235).

Expectations that were wrong:

  • "ρΠ < 0.85 at 48³": Reλ 32 is too low.
  • "ρΠ falls monotonically with Reλ": dropped on the maintainer's decision.
  • "TKE and RSM isotropic KE within 10 %": measured −11.6 % / −11.7 %.
  • "TKE e within 25 % of ½τ_kk": measured 2.8×.
  • "RSM heat flux closer to the DNS than TKE's": the reverse holds.
  • "ρ(τ₃₃/τ₁₁) > 0.5": holds only up to T/4.
  • "Σ P_s V = −stencil sink with the facade model on" is physically wrong.

Not met:

  • TKE over-dissipates the CBC decay (−18.5 % / −34.4 %).
  • The cube's x_F is far too short (0.21–0.51, against 1.04).
  • RSM cannot run under IMEX.
  • RSM facade production has the inconsistency #232 fixed for TKE.

Phase 57 — Wall models for high Reynolds number

Equilibrium and non-equilibrium wall models, dynamic wall models, and a proper treatment of the log-layer mismatch problem. Required for wall-modelled LES of realistic urban flow. Validated by: Kim-Moin-Moser channel at several Reynolds numbers; comparison against wall-resolved reference.

✅ SHIPPED (src/WallModels.jl, #169, #183, #179, #189, #190 / PRs #204, #220, #211, #221, #222). What was built:

  • Equilibrium wall laws (Spalding, rough log law, MOST), delegating to SurfaceExchange, and an implicit apply_wall_stress! (local, Schumann, Moeng). The Berlin ground stress is evaluated every step.
  • Matching height, time filter and 2Δ input filter (MatchingInput). The Berlin ground takes the time-filtered input.
  • Wall model on IBM and cut-cell walls. Berlin wall_model_facades is on by default.
  • TBLE non-equilibrium wall model with dynamic κ, as an option.
  • Anderson–Meneveau dynamic z0, as an option.

Measured:

  • Spalding round trip 8.7e-15. Momentum is conserved to 1.3e-15, and the operator is stable at 100× the explicit limit.

  • Log-layer mismatch against Lee & Moser, U_b⁺ error (first-cell matching → matching height plus filters):

    Re_τ18254410005186
    U_b⁺ error+5.58 → −1.24 %−0.50 → −1.19 %−0.59 → −1.40 %−1.41 → −1.45 %

    Φm(Δz) goes from 1.70 to 1.47 at Reτ 182 and from 1.24 to 0.93 at

  • IBM walls match z-walls to 3e-15, at U_b⁺ 19.18 (−4.0 % against Lee & Moser). Without the model it is 13.86.

  • The 30° cut-cell channel gives ⟨τ_w⟩ 0.990.

  • Berlin facades carry 9.3 % of the surface drag. The stencil drag they replace was 33× the modelled drag.

  • TBLE:

    • Stokes I converges at second order.
    • After a sudden tripling of dp/dx, τw gets half-way in 5.965 δ/uτ, against the resolved reference 5.960 (equilibrium: 6.255).
    • In Berlin, the street stresses differ from the equilibrium ones by more than 30 % in 77 % of columns, at +9.4 % stage cost.

  • Dynamic z0: u* is grid-invariant to within 4.5 % from 32² to 16², against +9 % to +10.6 % with a fixed α.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • With wind given, the wall-stress operator scaled the stress by U1/U_in. The #169 test had encoded the wrong formula.
  • Matching the dynamic κ on the stress is degenerate.
  • TBLE :full with centred convection was unstable.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • "|u⁺ − log law| < 1e-3 at y⁺ = 1e4": Spalding itself differs by 2.506e-3 there.
  • "2× lower Ub⁺ error at Reτ 1000": first-cell matching is already within 1 %.
  • "The matching height lowers the rough-wall Φ_m peak": the peak is the Smagorinsky overshoot, which the wall model does not control.
  • "TBLE within 1 % of Spalding at h⁺ = 30": −3.0 %, because the buffer layer differs.
  • "Stencil drag more than 10× the modelled drag": measured 3.8×.

Not met:

  • The 2× reduction at Re_τ 1000.
  • Dynamic z0 of a cube array is 5.8× Macdonald (1998).
  • Berlin keeps first-cell matching, because 3 cells is in the canyon.
  • The cut-cell wall model is not in Berlin.

Phase 58 — Stable-boundary-layer and intermittent turbulence

The hardest regime in boundary-layer meteorology: very stable stratification, intermittent turbulence, gravity-wave-turbulence interaction, and the correct behaviour as the flow relaminarises. Validated by: GABLS2, GABLS3, GABLS4 intercomparison cases; CASES-99 observations.

✅ SHIPPED (src/TurbulenceStability.jl, src/LargeScaleForcing.jl, the MSHF in SurfaceExchange, examples/sbl/; #170, #171, #193, #194 / PRs #203, #210, #229, #231; follow-up #237 / PR #243). What was built:

  • Stability functions of Ri_g (Lilly, Brown et al. 1994, short and long tail, MOST-consistent) and N² / Ri_g fields. The fog column and both cores delegate to them.
  • LargeScaleForcing: geostrophic pressure gradient, nudging, advective tendencies and a T_s series. It replaces both example copies.
  • Maximum sustainable heat flux (Van de Wiel et al. 2012) and a LES collapse test.
  • GABLS2, GABLS3, GABLS4 and CASES-99 on the SBL driver.

Measured:

  • MOST round trip of K_m 5.4e-11. The log-linear critical Ri is 0.199996.
  • Ekman veer 44.74°. Inertial oscillation 6.5e-8 of the ageostrophic speed.
  • MSHF: the closed form matches to 1e-8.
  • Collapse LES:
    • At H = 2 H_max, the lowest third keeps 2.8 % of the neutral TKE, with γ = 0.
    • At H = 0.5 H_max it keeps 83.2 %.
    • The first-level wind is 1.253–1.257 U₀, against the predicted 2^{1/3} = 1.260.
  • GABLS3:
    • CI grid (25 m): jet 9.07 m/s at 94.9 m. Envelope 7.8–9.3 m/s at 100–130 m.
    • 6.25 m: jet 7.70 m/s at 126.8 m; U(10 m) 2.62 m/s, inside 2.41–2.83.
    • 3.125 m: profiles on the LES median.
  • GABLS4 exp. 3 at 2 m, against the six 1 m LES:
    • u* 0.092 m/s and H −11.0 W/m² are inside.
    • h 30.7 m is outside (14.5–19.5).
    • L 4.6 m is outside (6.5–9.9).
    • θ* 0.125 K is outside (0.07–0.12).
  • GABLS2 at 30 h, corrected: H −23.0 W/m², u* 0.192 m/s and h 100 m are inside the SCM ranges. Uncorrected, H and h are outside.
  • CASES-99 hockey stick, at 30 m only: V_s 7.56 m/s and slope 0.17, against 6.5 m/s and about 0.25 (Sun et al. 2012).
  • Berlin at 03:00: every stable column is beyond the MSHF point. The night has no jet and u* 0.001–0.002 m/s (the very stable regime 1).

Bugs the tests caught:

  • Random initial perturbations were not divergence-free, which gave θ swings of ±20 K.
  • Under the pressure gradient, the Berlin sponge drained the mean wind to 0.12 m/s. Nudging had hidden this.
  • The GABLS4 reference counted CSIRO's 3.9 / 1.17 m run as 1 m (#237).
  • Couvreux et al. (2020) Table 5 is shifted by one hour.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • "N² within 2× of the dry value": the vapour term takes about 2/3 off.
  • "A nocturnal low-level jet in Berlin": the 200 m domain is too shallow.
  • "One H_max separates both regimes": the bulk value is 0.032 × the wall value.
  • "Whole-domain TKE below 5 % at 20 H/u": it gets there at 40 H/u.
  • "GABLS3 jet height at CI resolution": 5–7 m low, kept as @test_broken.
  • The GABLS4 h range of 14.5–25 m.

Not met:

  • GABLS4 h, L and θ*.
  • The CASES-99 shape at 10 m and 50 m.
  • GABLS4 was not run at 1 m, and GABLS3 not on the 800 m cube.
  • All the runs are dry.
  • Calm soil columns deliver more than H_max (linearised skin).

Phase 59 — Transition and laminar-turbulent coexistence

Handle flows that are partly laminar: nocturnal boundary layers, low-Reynolds regions in a cut-cell wake, and the transition process itself. Validated by: transition location in a flat-plate boundary layer; relaminarisation in a strongly stable case.

✅ SHIPPED (src/Transition.jl, NoSlipWall in BoundaryConditions, the σ-model in Operators; #172, #184, #180, #185, #195 / PRs #209, #217, #206, #226, #230; follow-up #236 / PR #271). What was built:

  • No-slip and moving walls, with IMEX support.
  • σ-model (Nicoud et al. 2011), with closed-form singular values, as the Berlin :sigma option.
  • Transition diagnostics: γ, intermittency, Rif, Reb, VTKE and growth-rate fits, in the Berlin `turbulencediag`.
  • Flat-plate boundary layer: a fringe that shares add_sponge!'s profile, Blasius inflow and free-stream disturbances.
  • Relaminarisation: criteria, the laminar_consistent trait, and the Berlin relaminarised fraction.

Measured:

  • Couette 2.7e-15. Poiseuille converges at order 2.0000.
  • σ-model:
    • Exactly zero for shear, rotation and 2-D flow. Near-wall slope 2.955 (z³).
    • The Kelvin–Helmholtz growth rate with σ equals the no-model 0.1820.
    • CBC: +6.6 % / −11.1 %.
  • Kelvin–Helmholtz growth rate 0.1864, against Michalke's 0.1897 (−1.76 %). At Miles–Howard J = 0.15 it grows (σ 0.0391); at J = 0.30 it decays.
  • T3A-like plate at 576×64×96:
    • Downstream c_f 1.057–1.135 × the turbulent correlation (target: within 20 %).
    • Onset at Re_θ 198 (Mayle 190, Abu-Ghannam & Shaw 200).
    • A 3-D control at Tu 0.1 % stays laminar.
  • Relaminarisation:
    • Laminar flow: the σ closure stays exactly zero.
    • Cooled open channel at Re_τ 360: survives at h/L 0.45 and collapses at 0.50, on 48³ and on 96³. The critical value is about 0.474, against Nieuwstadt's 0.492 in the κ-including convention (−4 %).
  • Berlin winter, before dawn: 4.0 % of stable cells relaminarised (4.1 % with σ). The background ν = 2 m²/s dominates this.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • growth_rate windows took in the seed transient and the start of saturation (0.1859).
  • Mixed z boundary pairs were built, then failed in fill_halos!.
  • The example's critical-h/L bracket always returned NaN.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • "Smagorinsky is nonzero for solid-body rotation".
  • "σ gives exactly zero in Berlin's free stream", which is weakly 3-D.
  • The original factor-3 miss against Nieuwstadt: Aeolus puts κ in L, so his 1.23 is 0.492 in our convention.
  • The coarse T3A grid (1.26–1.39) was the grid, not the code.
  • A single-seed L11 assertion.

Not met:

  • At Re_τ 180, survival at h/L 0.3 fails (critical 0.12).
  • Flores & Riley's L⁺ ≈ 100 cannot be reconciled with Nieuwstadt.
  • h/L 0.45 was not re-run on 96³.
  • σ is off by default.

Turbulence closure — known limitations carried forward

  • The Berlin flagship night is not physical (#238): 37 °C at 03:00, a 27.7 K heat island, u* ≈ 0.001 m/s. Its constant ν = 2 m²/s is about 100× the night-time SGS κ, so it hides most of the closure differences measured above.
  • N² uses dry or vapour-only θ_v. It has no condensate loading and no moist-adiabatic stability (#239). AMD's buoyancy term uses dry θ.
  • The SBL validation runs are dry. The Boussinesq step_rk3! does not advance qv.
  • Resolution-limited misses: the K171 CBC deficit, the stratified channel at Riτ = 60, collapse at Reτ 180, and GABLS4 at 2 m.
  • Option-only closures wait on a maintainer decision to become defaults, measured at their cost: LASD, AMD, Vreman, σ, RSM, TBLE and the dynamic z0.

Model infrastructure

The model around the physics: how a run is configured, driven, logged and tested. It has no phase numbers; the work was pulled forward by the Berlin nesting epic and the Technical-debt milestone. Its GitHub milestone is "Model infrastructure".

Run configuration and input drivers (epic #311)

✅ SHIPPED (src/Config.jl, src/config/, src/InputDrivers.jl, src/input_drivers/; CFG-a to CFG-i, #356–#364 / PRs #366, #372, #376, #375, #374, #380, #389, #393, #396; docs/src/design/run_configuration.md). What was built:

  • Aeolus.Config: a declarative schema with strict validation (every problem in one ConfigError, with suggestions), extends chains (at most 8 links), dotted overrides, a canonical resolved run.toml with config_hash and input hashes, and load_run_config to replay it.
  • One entry point: julia -m Aeolus run | check | resolve | options | preprocess, a run-driver contract and drivers for bare simulations, Berlin, SBL, DNS, channels, columns, CBC decay, patchy surfaces and DNS decay; [sweep] tables; every example script runs a shipped config.
  • Static driver (NetCDF-4): terrain, buildings, surface and ground classes, LAI and polygons, written by the OSM/GeoJSON preprocessor; Berlin reads its geometry from one committed file.
  • Dynamic driver (NetCDF-4): initial state, boundary planes and forcing, from sounding, Aeolus-parent and ERA5 (GRIB2 or NetCDF) preprocessors; the Berlin offline nest. New dependency GRIBDatasets.
  • Docs and coverage: generated option and input-format references and a round-trip suite over every shipped config.

Measured:

  • 400 random configs (NaN, ±Inf, −0.0, control characters, emoji) are a canonical fixed point; Float64 bits survive TOML → config → TOML.
  • 25 of 25 shipped configs replay bitwise from their own run.toml (4190 assertions); a resumed run is bitwise equal to an uninterrupted one for the simulation and SBL drivers.
  • Berlin: the four TOML scenarios equal the Julia presets field for field, and the 22 state digests after 12 steps are bit-identical; geometry from the static driver is bit-identical to the OSM path.
  • ERA5 preprocessor on an analytic atmosphere: level heights 2.2e-16, θ 1.9e-16, geostrophic wind 1.2e-13 relative; GRIB2 and NetCDF paths bitwise identical. Real ERA5 against the Lindenberg sounding (2019-06-29 00 UTC, 200–3000 m): θ RMSE 0.787 K, qv RMSE 0.718 g/kg (bound 1.5 each).

Bugs the tests caught:

  • sbl_surface! read stale periodic halos at the end of an RK3 step, so the surface layer of the last row and column used an old wind (GABLS1 32³: max|Δu| 1.9e-4 m/s; the reduced GABLS2 inversion moves 856.0 → 851.7 m). Caught by CFG-d's bit-identity digests.
  • The case-section check compared value origins, so a run.toml (which records every default) did not replay (CFG-e).

Expectations that were wrong:

  • The DYCOMS RF01 prescribed flux jump "between 50 and 75 W/m²": the measured 47.9 W/m² is the exact (F₀ − F₁)(1 − e^{−κ LWP}), now asserted to 1e-12.
  • "At least 20 CBC steps": the decay is 16 steps at 16³.
  • CFG-a's "a driver section may not share a common section's name" became a rule change in CFG-b (a same-named driver section extends the common one).
  • In the merge train, CFG-h's lindenberg_era5.toml needed a [test] table and an ERA5 dynamic driver made once at test time before CFG-i's round-trip suite could cover it.

Not met / not built: config_roundtrip.jl takes 154–176 s against the 120 s budget (first-call compilation); Berlin resume (#269, since built: PR #416); vegetation, soil and water type rasters in the static driver; terrain (zt is written as 0).

One model: core code, configuration and input drivers (epic #459)

The target is docs/src/design/single_model.md (PR #449, agreed with the maintainer on 2026-10-09): one model, a run is a TOML configuration plus its static and dynamic drivers, physics reaches a run as a component of a fixed stage pipeline, and examples are configuration. It supersedes epic #443 (EX-a … EX-e) and PRs #447/#448 (closed 2026-10-16). Shipped 2026-10-16 with NEST-p3 (#497): one model, one nest engine, one assembly path (aeolus run → SimulationDriver → Model); the urban site, its nest parent and the former validation run types are configurations of it.

Sizes, before → after: urban code 16,600 lines (src/UrbanCase.jl + src/urban/, before ONE-e) → 12,961 (src/components/urban.jl + src/components/urban/); src/sbl/ 2,277 → 0 (GABLS/CASES-99 are configurations); Aeolus.RunDrivers and the ParentCase/ParentConfig/ urban_parent parent gone; three assembly paths (the model, the urban case, the run types) → one; src/config/simulation_driver.jl 630 → 1,401 lines, the one builder (nesting, the urban site and its parent included). All of src/: 127,813 lines. Fast tier on master after the last merge: 149 jobs, 120,748 pass, 0 fail.

What was built, in the epic's order:

  • ONE-a (#450 / PR #462): Aeolus.Components: the component interface, the fixed stage pipeline (stages 6–8 inside the core through concretely typed hooks), Domain and Model; advection, SGS, Coriolis, surface θ flux and sponge as components. 28 config variants reproduce master's state digests bit for bit through Model.
  • ONE-b (#451 / PR #466): schema version 2: no run types for the model, one section per component, [[domain]] with per-domain overrides, slot/requires/incompatible validation, a bare config is a minimal dry LES, no extends.
  • ONE-c (#452 / PR #467): one budget system (Aeolus.BudgetSystem) and one output writer selecting from component catalogues.
  • ONE-d (#453 / PR #468): [checks], results.toml and exit code 3. Dry CBL at 16³: energy closure 4.5e-14 W m⁻², momentum 6.2e-25 Pa.
  • ONE-j (#460 / PR #464): every component on both cores, with checkpointing. Anelastic θ budget of the dry CBL, 32³, 30 min: −1.36e-3 → −2.5e-14 of the surface input.
  • ONE-h (#457 / PR #463): preprocessing moved to AeolusPrep.jl; it regenerates all 11 committed drivers bit for bit in their data.
  • ONE-e part 1 (#454 / PR #469): every equation out of the urban glue into its module (54-run smoke matrix, 1245 digests, 0 differ).
  • ONE-e part 2 (#454 / PR #472): the urban couplings are components of an urban site of the model ([urban] enabled, one section per component); CaseConfig's physics/options, the coupling registry, the urban stage loop and the urban run type are gone; the Berlin configs are self-contained TOML in examples/urban/. The open faces' θ relaxation was booked twice in the air-heat budget (−119.015 J m⁻² as urban tendencies, +119.607 J m⁻² as transport over the 12-step smoke run); booked once since.
  • ONE-f (#455 / PR #470): nesting by [[domain]], two-way self-nesting by default; the blow-up guard checks every domain.
  • ONE-g (#456 / PR #471): GABLS1–4 and CASES-99 as configurations of the model; the column, channel and DNS a-priori cases dropped; the a-posteriori DNS-vs-LES example (dynamic Smagorinsky closest: max |ΔK|/K 0.079).
  • ONE-i (#458): examples are case directories, examples/<case>/<case>.toml + input/ + data/ + scripts that read output; test/examples_enforcement.jl fails on any .jl under examples/ that builds or steps a model, with no exemptions (the forbidden names are read from the package: 46 assertions, each rule also fired on synthetic files); the column and dns_decay legacy cases that lived in examples/ are gone (the Kessler box is a model configuration; sgs_decay loads the P56-c fixture itself) with Config.DRIVER_FILES and run_configs. The dry CBL of the README, 32³, 60 steps of 5 s: energy closure 3.4e-13 W m⁻² of 279.98 W m⁻² stored, momentum closure 2.4e-21 kg m⁻¹ s⁻².
  • ONE-k (#474 / PR #478): the urban site is a component of its domain (UrbanSite), stepped by step!(::Model) through the model's run loop; UrbanCase's run loop, CaseConfig, its NetCDF writer and restart are gone, and Aeolus.UrbanCase is Aeolus.Urban under src/components/urban/ (14,083 → 13,889 lines). 54 smoke runs, 1137 digests, unchanged.
  • ONE-l (#475 / PR #477): the seven Aeolus.RunDrivers run types ported to configurations or dropped (inflow_fetch, obstacle_channel: channel cases), Aeolus.RunDrivers deleted; the channel harness moved to test/fixtures/channel_cases.jl. KH, flat plate and CBL-TKE match the old scripts; scalar_advection_2d is a stated physics change (centred flux form on the staggered grid), mean θ constant to every digit over 1006 steps.
  • ONE-m (#476 / PR #479): one key per exclusive slot ([core] model, [timestep] scheme, [sgs] model, [surface] model, [microphysics] scheme, [advection] scheme); no enable/disable pairs.
  • NEST-p2 (#482 / PR #485): one nest engine (ModelNest): windows, adaptive root steps with the smallest stable whole number of child substeps, a failed window retried from a snapshot, siblings in parallel. Adaptive windows 6.1× cheaper per simulated second than fixed ones on the 64×64×32 test nest (8 s windows, n = 3, 0.065 s).
  • NEST-p1 (#481 / PR #486): the Berlin parent's physics as components (tiled surface, prescribed flux, mean-column radiation, forcing); the 96×96×38 tiled parent steps in 0.0711 s through the pipeline against 0.0742 s before.
  • NEST-p3 (#483 / PR #497): complete per-domain configs (--set domain.<id>.<section>.<key>); the Berlin parent is a [[domain]] with its own static driver (AeolusPrep.jl writes its tiles, the site's classes under the site's footprint); the site is a child of ModelNest; ParentCase, ParentConfig, urban_parent and NestedRun's window API deleted (src/components/urban 14,204 → 12,950 lines). Berlin smoke, 48 s on one thread: 2,175 → 1,947 s wall per simulated hour, 0.97 → 0.79 s per site step; a nest with an urban site no longer snapshots its windows (3.5–4.4 s per 1.5 s window measured).
  • PREP-fix (#487 / PR #488): AeolusPrep.jl's suite green against Aeolus again (132/17/10 → 243/0/0), and bin/test-against <checkout> in AeolusPrep runs it against any Aeolus branch.

Expectations that were wrong (recorded in the PRs): ONE-g's GABLS3 jet-height bound was narrower than the scatter between realisations (five seeds: 89.5–99.3 m, σ = 0.5 Δz), widened to 1.5 Δz; a contract-test tolerance of 1e-9 was below the round-off of the measured change (1.7e-7); ONE-i's cloud_spinup test expected a diagnostics row at t = 0, which the run monitor never writes (rows at 60 … 480 s). Bugs the tests caught: a nested run's guard snapshot held the root at t = 4 s and the child at t = 0 (ONE-f); a mean writer's open averaging intervals were not checkpointed (ONE-g: u* 0.5027 vs 0.5083 after a resume). Later: [sgs] model = "tke" could not be built on a stretched grid (NEST-p1); an earlier NEST-p3 commit silently dropped the site's footprint from the parent's tiles (restored as driver data); the Berlin smoke digests depended on the BLAS thread count, not on load (#489: the radiosity LU). Expectations that proved wrong in NEST-p3: the smoke checks' Bird tolerance at μ₀ = 0.148 (−15.2 %, outside the calibrated μ₀ ≥ 0.27) and the stability check on a profile with no capping inversion (#490).

Left for #498: the run-driver registry and [run] legacy hold one entry (the model) and go; the core's step keywords (body_force!, post_predictor!, sgs) become private once the wall-model channel harness, the flat plate, the immersed-boundary path and the urban time stepper pass the pipeline's hooks. #484 (attributed budget exchange across nests) and #461 (external LES validation) are separate.

Structured logging, run folders, diagnostics and provenance (epic #293)

✅ SHIPPED (src/logging/RunLog.jl, src/io/run_folder.jl; LOG-a to LOG-g, #333–#338 and #347 / PRs #340, #344, #346, #370, #373, #388, #390; docs/src/api/logging.md). What was built: structured events through stdlib Logging (the library never prints; a quiet test logger); one run folder per run with run.toml provenance (commit, versions, Manifest hash, FFTW policy, seeds, one [[session]] per restart) and a dirty-tree policy; a diagnostics registry writing diagnostics.csv and diagnostics.nc (NetCDF-4, per-step rows and costly rows with profiles); a progress line; a blowup guard that diagnoses, writes a crash checkpoint and stops; a StepRecord filled by every step core (dt and its limiter, CFL, solver iterations and residuals, wall time per stage) with the run monitor attached to the cores; the verbose keywords removed; the Berlin console output through the logger.

Measured: one six-key record to run.log 7.9 µs with the 5 s flush policy; the default monitor's parts 0.084 ms per step, 1.56 % of a 64×64×32 step (budget 2 %, asserted by the benchmark); step! still allocates 0 B with a hook installed; a run folder costs 11.6 ms per run.

Bugs the tests caught: latest_run compared Float64 timestamps as strings, so resume = :latest could restart the wrong run; the diagnostics.nc provenance of a restart that changed its config recorded the run's first config.

Budget restored (#514 / PR #515, 2026-11-06). By November the same parts were 2.27 % of a 3.71 ms step and the benchmark failed: the step had become 30 % faster (#493), the monitor had not, and nothing reran the benchmark. Without checking less often:

  • the attached guard repeats nothing step_adaptive! has done: it sweeps only νt, κt (and a dry core's qv) and keeps the step's rollback snapshot as the last good state instead of copying the state;
  • the remaining full checks are one fused, threaded sweep;
  • one pressure value is read between checks;
  • the costly diagnostics.nc rows go in the 5 s block write (each one was 13.5 ms written at once, 0.085 ms of it the profiles).

Measured on 64×64×32:

  • the default (attached) monitor's parts are 0.57 % of a step on 1 thread and 0.46–0.55 % on 4–8; a due check costs 0.068 ms against 0.44 ms before;
  • timed step by step, what a run pays is +1.1 to +1.5 % on 1 thread (master +2.8 to +3.1 %), about 2.5× the parts (cache effects). The benchmark now prints both and asserts the parts.

Against master, 13 blowup scenarios caught the same blowups at the same step in the same fields; under an attached monitor the crash checkpoint is one step older, and fixed-step runs are stopped 3–7 steps earlier. les_tke (32³, fixed FFTW planner) was bit-for-bit identical in field output, diagnostics.nc and results.toml.

Not met / loose ends:

  • The benchmark was not run by anything, so the overrun went unnoticed for a month; it is still a manual benchmark.
  • The fused finiteness test in save_state! was rejected: it was slower serially (0.54 against 0.45 ms).
  • The per-step CSV row costs ~23 µs in a run against ~2 µs timed alone (the largest remaining in-run cost).
  • bitwise_reproducible is still recorded in the provenance, although #493 dropped bit-identity as a goal.
  • Asynchronous field-output and checkpoint writes are #516 (Performance milestone).

Expectation that was wrong: test/logging_diagnostics_nc.jl asserted that a costly row is on disk at once. That was the old policy, which #514 changed on purpose, so the code was right and the expectation was stale.

Tests, CI and documentation deployment (#316, #317, #320, #354)

✅ SHIPPED (#316 / PR #325, #354 / PR #355, #320 / PR #387, #317 / PR #395; docs/src/testing.md). What was built:

  • Runner: test/run.jl runs the files in parallel. Update 2026-11-05: the fast/full tiers were replaced by two suites, the tests (the PR gate, cut to their crucial checks) and the validation runs (validation/, not a gate); see docs/src/testing.md and docs/src/validation.md.
  • CI (GitHub Actions): the tests on every PR and push to master, the validation runs weekly and on demand; Julia 1.13. The Documenter workflow builds the docs on every PR and deploys dev from master and stable from tags to gh-pages.
  • Berlin test suite removed (#320): the 13 test/berlin_*.jl files (4162 assertions, 1260 s of job time) and the coverage lists are gone. Every src physics check moved into a module test with a synthetic setup; the example's end-to-end runs are a by-hand script, examples/urban/berlin_checks.jl (897 assertions).
  • Pruning (#317): large_scale_forcing.jl 78.3 → 12.6 s (a turbulent box instead of 100 Berlin smoke steps); one shared k^(−5/3) fixture for five SGS files.

Shipped with these blocks: FLAG-a (#392 / PR #398): the default flagship failed near step 12 because a timing script forced 2 s steps past the stable dt (0.47 → 0.098 s within 9 steps; |v| 7.8 → 49 m/s, θ 229–360 K at step 15); step_case!(case, dt) now refuses a dt above the adaptive stepper's stable_dt, and the default case runs 20 steps without a retry (max CFL 0.46). The issue's premise proved wrong: it read as an instability of the default case, but the case is stable when stepped at its own dt; the failure was the forced step.

The guard's snapshot of the tiled surface (#526, 2026-11-06). Profiling a 24 h Berlin run showed where the time went. The model domain is 100 m, 200 × 200 × 60, with land-use tiles and ERA5 open faces, on 24 threads. The run loop took 1.75 s per step against 0.26 s for the model step alone.

87 % of the main thread was the blow-up guard. Twice every 10 steps it snapshots the components, and the tiled surface's checkpoint_state was the generic structural walk: 4,538,305 objects and 346 MB, 7.2 s. Most of those bytes were solver scratch (35 workspace vectors per soil tile) and constants.

Built: a packed checkpoint state.

  • SurfaceExchange.pack_state! / unpack_state! are implemented by SoilWaterColumn, SoilThermalColumn, SlabLand and SurfaceMosaic.
  • TiledSurfaceState holds every tile's state in one vector, packed over the columns in parallel. The walk stays for other surfaces and for old checkpoints.

Measured on that configuration (the real run loop, (240 s run − 40 s run) / 100 steps, 24 threads):

surface checkpoint_stateserializedrun loop
before6.8–7.9 s523 MB1.753 s/step
after0.043 s50.6 MB0.357 s/step

That is 4.9× faster, a 24 h run in about 4.3 h instead of 21 h.

The numbers vary between sessions. Two more sessions of the new code gave 0.430 and 0.361 s/step. A free snapshot (the ceiling) gave 0.294, 0.306, 0.325 and 0.345 s/step. Measured back to back, the new code was 0.361 against 0.345: 0.016 s/step for the snapshot. Most of that is garbage collection (0.034 against 0.014 s/step), from the snapshot's 50 MB allocations.

The real checkpoint/resume path was also checked: run 240 s, resume from the 120 s checkpoint, compare the 240 s states. The old and the new code give the same continuation to within run-to-run round-off (2.6e-9 after the resume, 2.9e-9 between two uninterrupted runs).

That check also showed a resume of an open-box domain does not continue the uninterrupted run. The first step after it differs by up to 0.012 m/s at the east open face, the same with the old and the new surface state. That is pre-existing and not part of this change; its numbers are in the description of #526's PR, for an issue of its own.

Tests: a column (with an aquifer), a mosaic and the whole component are restored into another history with the scratch poisoned (NaN), and must continue as the original (rtol 1e-12). A test expectation that proved too weak: the first component test poisoned the tiles through unpack_state! itself, so a dropped T_skin restore went unnoticed (identical histories). It now gives the surface a different history (three extra tile steps) and checks that a dropped T_skin fails it.

Open after these blocks: #397 (FFTW MEASURE segfault); #238 (implausible Berlin nights).

Speed over bit-identity (epic #493)

Results need not be bit-identical across thread counts, machines, restarts or refactors; tests assert physics within stated tolerances (maintainer decision, 2026-10-16). See docs/src/testing.md.

FFTW threads in every model (#524, 2026-11-06). Since KA-h (#298) init_threads! puts FFTW on Threads.nthreads(), but only the urban site's build_case called it: every other model domain, nested or not, kept FFTW at its start-up count of 1, so the pressure solver's FFTs ran on one core while the kernels ran on all of them. Config.build now calls init_threads! for every run before any plan is made; an explicit count ([fftw] threads, AEOLUS_FFTW_THREADS, set_fftw_policy!) is kept. Measured on a 200 × 200 × 60 open-box domain (Berlin, 100 m, 40 steps, 24 Julia threads on 12 cores): the pressure solve went from 3.48 s (1 FFTW thread) to 0.89 s (12 threads), 8 % → 2 % of the step; the step's total did not change, so the remaining time is elsewhere (being profiled). Test: config_model.jl builds a non-urban run from a policy at 3 threads and expects Threads.nthreads(), and an explicit 2 kept.

The open faces' time blend reads the slabs directly (#529, 2026-11-06). A dynamic driver's boundary record is a SlabArray, a storage-indexed view whose getindex searched the field's slabs and tested three ranges at every point. The blend of two records into each open plane read two such points per output point. Most planes are below the threading threshold (26 112 points against 16 384 per task; 18 of the 20 run serially), so this was a serial loop paying the search at every point. blend_window! now asks blend_source(A, ranges) for the dense array and offset behind the plane's box. A SlabArray returns the one slab holding the whole box, found once per plane, and a box no single slab holds keeps the point-by-point view.

Measured on the Berlin 100 m domain (200 × 200 × 60, ERA5 open faces, 24 threads), all 20 planes and 548 352 points:

  • one boundary_values! takes 2.69 ms before and 0.42 ms after (2.55 and 0.36 ms in a second pair of runs);
  • per step, counted inside step!: 3.0 calls, 8.1 ms before and 1.8 ms after, out of a 0.26 s step. That is a 2.4 % gain.

A deviation from the plan: the issue estimated the blend at 12 % of the step, from its share (95 of 785 samples) of the main thread's profile. The direct timing gives 3 %. The real run loop can't resolve 2.4 %: its s/step varied between 0.35 and 0.59 between sessions, for both versions.

Test: input_dynamic.jl records a driver whose values differ at every point, field and time. Every plane takes the slab path, reads exactly what getindex reads (copied data) and blends to the same values; a box spanning more than one slab keeps the view. Shifting the slab offset by one point makes the test fail (43 errors).

A checkpoint of an open box continues the run (#528, 2026-11-06). Resuming the Berlin 100 m run (ERA5 open faces) did not continue it. One step after --resume, u differed by up to 0.012 m/s at the east open face; after 60 steps, by 0.14 m/s in u and 0.10 K in θ. Two uninterrupted runs agree to 3e-9.

The open boundaries belong to the simulation's boundary conditions, not to a component, and the checkpoint stored only the fields and the components. A restart rebuilt each OpenBoundaryState with initialized = false. Its first step refilled every plane buffer from the boundary data, so the values carried over were lost:

  • the radiated values at outflow points;
  • the Runge–Kutta stage combination of the buffers;
  • the mass-flux correction.

The Orlanski history was lost as well.

The checkpoint now stores open_boundary_checkpoint(sim): every buffer, initialized, the correction's ledger and the radiation planes' history. restore_checkpoint! copies these back. A checkpoint from before this change restores as before, with a warning.

A bug the experiment caught in the first version of the fix: an initialized state skips the first update, which is where θ is bound (by identity) to its planes. The unbound θ got the mirror halo for one step, so the restored run still diverged (2e-4 m/s in u, 1.6e-2 K in θ after one step, with Dirichlet faces). Restoring now binds the fields. Finding this took a walk over the whole object graph: the restored and the continuous simulation were identical except for scratch, and stepping a fresh build once before restoring made it exact.

Measured on a 16 × 12 × 8 open box (RK3, dt 0.5 s, time-varying data, checkpoint after 20 steps, compared with the uninterrupted run over the next 10 steps):

outflowrestored with the open-boundary stateold restart, after 10 steps
dirichlet09.9e-5 m/s, 1.6e-3 K
convective00.42 m/s, 1.5 K
orlanski00.41 m/s, 1.5 K

Test (open_boundaries.jl), Orlanski outflow:

  • a restored fresh build continues to 1e-12;
  • an old payload warns and diverges by more than 1e-2;
  • a differently built open box is refused.

The real run, through the CLI. This is the Berlin 100 m configuration (200 × 200 × 60, ERA5 open faces), at 24 Julia threads, dt 2 s, with the estimate FFTW planner (a deterministic plan, and no #397 crash):

  1. run for 240 s, with checkpoints every 60 steps;
  2. set the 240 s checkpoint aside;
  3. --resume, which restores the 120 s checkpoint and runs to 240 s.

The resumed state at 240 s equals the uninterrupted one in every field (u, v, w, θ, qv, p, νt, κt): max|Δ| = 0. Before the fix the same check gave 0.14 m/s in u after 60 steps.

FFTW's threads on its own pool: the MEASURE planner no longer crashes (#397, 2026-11-06). Planning a threaded transform under MEASURE crashed the process (signal 11 in spawn_apply) at 10–14 threads.

FFTW.jl registers spawnloop as FFTW's thread callback. It spawns one Julia task per job, over a job array on the C stack of the FFTW call waiting in @sync, and that caller may yield while the jobs run. This is upstream JuliaMath/FFTW.jl#306, and the fix there (#344) is unmerged. FFTW.jl 1.10.0, the latest release, with FFTW_jll 3.3.12, still has it.

Since #525 every model plans threaded, so this hit every run with an unlucky thread count. The FFTW policy (_fftw_policy!, the one place every plan's flags come from) now clears the callback when it first resolves. FFTW then runs its threaded transforms on its own threads.

Measured on a Ryzen AI 9 HX 370 (12 cores), at 12 Julia and 12 FFTW threads, under MEASURE:

  • planning a 256³ irfft crashed 4 of 4 processes before, 0 of 4 after;
  • the Berlin r2r plus the 256³ irfft crashed 8 of 8 before, 0 of 8 after.

The cost: the 200 × 200 × 60 r2r under ESTIMATE takes 1.6–1.7 ms on Julia tasks against 1.9–2.3 ms on FFTW's threads at -t 12, and both run 1.7–2.5 ms at -t 24. That is about 2 ms of a 0.24 s Berlin step.

Test: fftw_threads.jl (exclusive) plans the 256³ irfft under MEASURE in a -t 12 subprocess and checks that the round trip recovers the input to 1e-12. With the fix disabled, the subprocess crashes and the test fails. Remove the workaround once FFTW.jl ships the fix.

The budgets cost 13 passes per step instead of 50, and can be turned off (#539, 2026-11-07). The measured budgets (budget_energy, budget_water) were the largest item of memory traffic in the Berlin 100 m step (#531): 50 full-field level sums per step at 16 B per point (the field and a Float64 air mask), 1.92 GB, 22 % of the step's bytes.

Inventory first. Every measurement of one Berlin step (master a69f9751, step 4, energy and water) was logged with its context and whether the change it closed was exactly zero:

  • 2 at the step's begin and 2 at its end (zero: nothing between steps, nothing outside the contexts);
  • 8 top-level closes before the core: clock/large_scale_forcing (both quantities), surface_flux/sgs (both) and the water close of surface_flux/mean_column_radiation measured exactly zero; only tiled_surface (both) and the radiation's energy close changed anything;
  • 36 in the core: 3 RK3 substages × 3 entries (tendencies/coriolis, body_force/large_scale_forcing, post_predictor/tiled_surface) × 2 quantities × open and close. Every one measured exactly zero: these entries write the momentum only. 6 opens were also exact repeats of the previous close of the same array;
  • 2 closes of the dynamics (non-zero).

So 41 of the 50 measured a component that cannot change the quantity; 9 carried information.

What was built.

  • Declared writes. Components.budget_writes(c, Val(stage)) names the quantities an entry can change (default: all, so an undeclared component is measured as before). An entry is measured only for those and skipped for the others (budget_skip!). Declared: Coriolis, the sponge, the fringe, the kinematic flow, the tiles' and the urban drags, the IBM and the facades' stress after the predictor (momentum); the surface θ flux, the surface layer's tendency, the column radiation's heating (energy); the tiles' and the prescribed surface flux (energy, water); SGS, towers, parent statistics, the radiation stage of the urban radiation components, the shortwave modifiers, the moist buoyancy (tw only) and the site's clock (nothing); the large-scale forcing and the urban geostrophic forcing (momentum when only the geostrophic pressure gradient, else all).
  • Nothing can hide. At the top level a skip marks the budget stale, and the next measuring context (or the end of the step) measures afresh and books any change since the last measurement as (:core, :unattributed). An undeclared write is then neither hidden nor attributed to its neighbour. This costs Berlin 4 of its 13 passes (the fresh measurements before tiled_surface, the radiation and, for water, the dynamics); trusting the declarations would make it 9. Inside the core a skip is free, and an undeclared write stays in the dynamics' share: the closure holds, but the term is the dynamics'. This is a deviation from the issue, which expected the end-of-step comparison to catch it: a tendency written inside the core is applied by the predictor, so only a measurement of the tendency around the entry could separate it, which is the pass being saved.
  • The verify mode ([budgets] verify_writes = true, with_budgets(…; verify = true)) measures every entry and stops with an error at a change a declaration left out. The Berlin run (30 steps, one surface update) and the urban synthetic city run it without error.
  • The meter's mask. The air mask is a Bool array (9 B per point where there are solid cells) and is not read at all in a domain without solid cells (8 B per point). The sums are the same values in the same canonical plane order.
  • The switch. [budgets] enabled = false builds no DomainBudgets in model domains and urban sites (the site also skips its per-step water snapshot). The requested budget_* outputs and budget checks are dropped with one warning, and results.toml records [budgets] enabled = false.

Measured. Berlin 100 m (200 × 200 × 60), master a69f9751 against this branch, estimate planner, FFTW threads = Julia threads, 31 warm-up steps then 60 timed steps; the median step, minimum over 2 processes (the mean includes the surface and radiation steps):

passes/stepbytes/step6 threads12 threads
before, budgets on501.92 GB239.9 ms258.4 ms
after, budgets on130.25 GB217.8 ms222.3 ms
after, enabled = false00220.9 ms220.0 ms
before, budget outputs removed00220.8 ms221.4 ms

The budgets cost 19 ms (6 t) and 37 ms (12 t) before; after, the difference to budgets off is below the run-to-run spread (±10 ms between processes). Passes counted with StageBudget.nmeasure; bytes are passes × 2.4 M points × bytes per point.

The dry convective boundary layer (examples/dry_convective_bl, at 128 × 128 × 64; energy and momentum closure checks; Euler): 21 → 15 passes per step (each remaining entry writes its quantity), 16 → 8 B per point. Median step 26.3 → 23.0 ms at 6 threads (off: 21.1 ms) and 22.8 → 19.4 ms at 12 (off: 17.4 ms).

The urban synthetic city (test/data/urban/small_city.toml, 16³ with buildings, energy ledger): 31.7 → 18.1 passes per step (the 14 in-core measurements per step and the clock, radiation-stage, shortwave and pollutant entries skipped), 16 → 9 B per point.

Rejected. Fusing the level sums into the kernels that stream the field (the scalar update, the tendency kernels): with 13 passes left, 4 of them the price of the guarantee, the remaining budget cost is below what the run resolves, and it would tie the meter into the kernels (#533 step 7 can revisit it). One launch for energy and water: it saves launches, not bytes (4 launches per step of ~20 µs). Reusing a close's measurement for the next open: after the skips no such pair is left in the runs measured.

Tests (budgets_output.jl): the declared run agrees with the verify run term by term and in storage within 1e-12 of the quantity's content (the same sums; the skipped contexts measured exactly zero), closes to the same, with fewer passes (water: 4 per step); an undeclared write at the top level is booked as (:core, :unattributed) (rtol 1e-10) while the next entry keeps its exact term, inside the core the closure holds; the verify mode stops at each false declaration (top level, tendency, post-predictor); the switch builds nothing, warns once, drops the outputs and checks and records it, on is unchanged. urban_run.jl: the synthetic city's budget-checked configuration runs with the budgets off, and in the verify mode with its budgets closed. A test expectation that proved wrong in the first draft: the closure tolerance was scaled by the largest term, which is 2e-15 for a budget that barely changes; round-off is relative to the quantity's content.

Suites: test/run.jl 4425 of 4426 passed in 115 files; the one failure was config_model.jl's list of run-level sections, which [budgets] joins by design (expectation updated; 49/49 after). Validation urban_checks.jl: 33/33.

Chemistry and aerosol

From four toy species to a mechanism a chemist would accept.

Status: not started (milestone 7: seven issues, all open). The Berlin epic (#135 / PR #162) moved a reduced NOx–O₃–VOC mechanism with an exact NO–NO₂–O₃ triad into src/Chemistry.jl (triad against RK4 1e-9, NOx and Ox conserved to 1e-14, noon Leighton ratio 1.0013 in the flagship), an MCM clear-sky J(NO₂), Wesely-type ra + rb + rc dry deposition and HNO₃ rain scavenging. That is groundwork, not Phases 60, 61 or 66: no KPP mechanism, no online actinic flux, rc is order-of-magnitude, there is no pollen and nothing is validated against flux data.

Phase 60 — KPP-generated gas-phase mechanism

Real KPP code generation with a full mechanism (MOZART-T1, CB05 or MCM subset), analytic Jacobian, and a Rosenbrock stiff solver. Replaces the three-reaction toy scheme. Validated by: box-model comparison against the reference KPP implementation; ozone-production comparison for a photochemical smog case.

Phase 61 — Photolysis rates

Online photolysis using the computed actinic flux from the radiation solver, including the effect of clouds and aerosol on photolysis rates. Validated by: J-value comparison against TUV reference model.

Phase 62 — Aqueous and heterogeneous chemistry

In-cloud aqueous-phase chemistry (sulphate production), heterogeneous reactions on aerosol and building surfaces, and the urban-specific chemistry of NO₂ on surfaces. Validated by: sulphate-production comparison against cloud-chamber data.

Phase 63 — Modal and sectional aerosol microphysics

Full aerosol dynamics: nucleation, condensational growth, coagulation, and a thermodynamic-equilibrium module (ISORROPIA-style) for inorganic partitioning, with a modal and a sectional option. Validated by: size-distribution evolution against chamber experiments; comparison against reference aerosol-model output.

Phase 64 — Secondary organic aerosol

Volatility-basis-set treatment of SOA formation from biogenic and anthropogenic VOCs, including the urban-relevant cooking and traffic sources. Validated by: organic-aerosol mass and O:C ratio against AMS measurements.

Phase 65 — Real emission inventories

Ingest gridded emission inventories (TNO-MACC, EDGAR, CAMS), traffic emission models keyed to OSM road geometry and diurnal traffic counts, biogenic emissions from MEGAN driven by the resolved vegetation and temperature, and point sources from facility registries. Validated by: mass-balance audit against inventory totals; concentration comparison against urban monitoring stations.

Phase 66 — Deposition and pollen

Resistance-network dry deposition (Wesely / Zhang) with real surface properties, size-resolved wet deposition, and a pollen model for allergy applications. Validated by: deposition-velocity comparison against flux measurements; pollen-concentration comparison against counting stations.

Real-world data, forcing and validation

A model is only as good as what drives it and what it is checked against.

Phase 67 — ERA5 and reanalysis driver

Read ERA5 / MERRA-2 to build initial and boundary conditions: vertical profiles, geostrophic forcing, advective tendencies, surface conditions, and their time evolution over a multi-day run. Validated by: reproduction of an observed boundary-layer evolution for several real dates.

Phase 67a, shipped (CFG-h, #363 / PR #393): an ERA5 reader (GRIB2 and NetCDF) writes the dynamic driver, compared with the Lindenberg sounding for one date (θ RMSE 0.787 K, qv RMSE 0.718 g/kg). The reader now lives in AeolusPrep.jl (#457). Phase 67b, open (#57): MERRA-2, multi-day runs and the validation across several dates.

Initial soil profiles, shipped (#521; AeolusPrep#6 / its PR #7 writes them).

  • The driver. It reads PALM's init_soil_t / init_soil_m over zsoil, as a profile or a volume (alt_dims, a new field of DriverVariable).
  • Where they go. The urban site's ground ([ground] soil_init: auto, uniform, driver, annual_wave) and the nest parent's soil tiles start from them. The base is held at the deepest level.
  • The fallback without data. It is the analytic annual wave (SoilColumn.annual_soil_temperature, soil_damping_depth).

Measured:

  • The wave start removes the slow mode. On a 15 m loam column under the annual surface wave, 30 d of hourly steps from day 120: started on the wave it stays within 0.0045 K of the analytic solution over the top 3 m; started uniform at the mean it is 1.51 K off (d = 2.84 m).

  • FLUXNET land column, uniform start vs annual wave (a script, not a validation case: the climatology is assumed, MAT 8.3 °C / 7.8 °C, A = 9 K, maximum on day 200). Bias of G against the observed Qg:

    sitedays 1–5days 6–35
    DE-Hai−21.6 → −2.8 W m⁻²−6.1 → +2.7 W m⁻²
    DE-Gri−13.6 → +5.4 W m⁻²−3.5 → +5.9 W m⁻²

    RMSE: days 1–5 31.0 → 25.6 (DE-Hai) and 24.9 → 27.3 (DE-Gri); days 6–35 26.5 → 27.5 and 23.7 → 26.7 W m⁻². The 1 m soil temperatures of the two starts still differ by 6.0 K and 5.3 K after 35 d. The start is not a detail at the daily scale, and an assumed climatology is no substitute for ERA5's profile.

Test expectations that were wrong:

  • Two CLM nodes below ERA5's deepest level (1.945 m): there is one, because the 9th node is at 1.73 m.
  • The water clipped after the interpolation: the code clips the driver's levels before, which is what it should do.

A soil volume read without a mesh is no longer an error. The sounding reader opens the same file without one; the soil's consumer reads it with the mesh and checks x and y.

Deviations:

  • "auto" without soil warns only when a driver exists; an idealised case logs info.
  • A nested child does not yet take the parent's mean profile (PALM's rule).
  • Per-column texture from data (SoilGrids) is the extension noted on #521.

Phase 68 — Mesoscale model nesting

Offline and online nesting into WRF, ICON and IFS output, with the interface handling different vertical coordinates, different microphysics assumptions, and the spin-up of turbulence from smooth mesoscale fields (synthetic-turbulence inflow generation). Validated by: nested-LES comparison against the driving mesoscale model's own boundary layer; turbulence spin-up distance diagnostics.

Phase 69 — Production OSM and geospatial ingestion

Full OSM support: multipolygon relations, building parts, inner courtyards, roof shapes, material and level tags, street widths, tree registries, and land-use polygons. Plus DEM terrain (SRTM / Copernicus), CORINE land cover, and lidar-derived building and canopy heights. Validated by: geometric comparison against reference city models (CityGML) for several European cities.

Phase 70 — Observation operators and evaluation framework

Forward operators mapping model state to what instruments measure: sonic anemometer, sodar, wind lidar, ceilometer, radar reflectivity, radiometer brightness temperature, air-quality monitor, thermal infrared imagery. Plus a statistical-evaluation framework. Validated by: end-to-end comparison for an instrumented site.

Phase 71 — Canonical benchmark library, part 1 (dry)

Automated, versioned, reference-data-backed implementations of: neutral channel (Kim-Moin-Moser, Hoyas-Jiménez), dry CBL (Sullivan-Patton), GABLS1-4, Wangara, CASES-99, flow past a cube (Silsoe), backward-facing step, urban canyon (Uehara wind-tunnel), Michelstadt and MUST urban dispersion campaigns. Deliverable: a CI-runnable suite that reports skill scores per case.

Phase 72 — Canonical benchmark library, part 2 (moist and reactive)

BOMEX, ATEX, RICO, DYCOMS-II RF01/RF02, ARM SGP diurnal cumulus, ASTEX transition, plus photochemical-smog and urban-dispersion reactive cases. Deliverable: the same CI suite extended, with the intercomparison ensembles as reference envelopes.

Phase 73 — Reproducibility and provenance

Full run provenance: input hashes, library versions, compiler flags, a deterministic-mode guarantee, and archived reference output so that any result in the docs can be regenerated years later. Deliverable: a provenance.toml per run and a reproduce command.

Partly built early (LOG-b #334, CFG-a/b #356–#357, the KA port #291): every run folder's run.toml records the commit, versions, Manifest hash, FFTW policy, seeds and input hashes; julia -m Aeolus run run.toml replays it (within round-off: since #493 results need not be bit-identical). Archived reference output and compiler-flag provenance remain.

Spin-up (#519)

Surface spin-up, shipped (#519, part B of the issue): PALM's spinup_time as a run-level [spinup] section. See Run configuration.

  • How it runs. The urban site's surfaces, radiation and building advance alone at negative times. The air above the surfaces is forced by PALM's solar sinusoid or by the dynamic driver's sfc_temperature. The soil water is frozen by default. Afterwards the atmosphere, the clock and the ledgers are exactly as at the start.
  • The initial envelope. The city-mean building now starts in steady conduction between outdoor air and room (steady_conduction!, steady_envelope!).
  • Measured. These runs are on the synthetic small city (radiation every 600 s, amplitude 4 K). The figure is the maximum over surface classes of the day-over-day drift of the daily-mean surface temperature:
    • 2 d: roof 5.26 K/d with dt = 300, 2.91 K/d with dt = 60;
    • 3 d: roof 0.144 / 0.026 K/d, street 0.25 / 0.21 K/d;
    • 4 d (dt = 300): roof −0.007 K/d, street 0.13 K/d;
    • water stays at 2–2.7 K/d, because its slab (C = 8 × 10⁶ J m⁻³ K⁻¹) is slow;
    • cost: 4 d at dt = 300 is 1152 steps in 31 s; 3 d at dt = 60 is 4320 steps in 67 s.
  • Time scales. These are the slowest eigenmodes: brick wall 9.3 h, roof 26 h, ground stack 19 d, soil (3.43 m) 30 d. Deep soil is for #521 (profiles from data), not for the spin-up.
  • Validation. validation/run.jl --only urban_checks.jl: 33 pass, 0 fail (292 s).
  • Test expectations that were wrong:
    • "the roof concrete is within 1 K of the room" was wrong: the analytic drop is 1.07 K. The test now asserts the analytic q·(Rsi + Rplaster + R_concrete).
    • The XPS assertion is the exact q·(d − Δz)/k.
    • config_model.jl's inventory of run-level sections gains spinup by design.
  • Deviations from the issue's plan:
    • The forcing is θ only. Wind and humidity stay at the initial state, as in PALM, rather than coming from the driver.
    • The solar forcing uses the case date's sun on every day.
    • There is no separate surface_time / surface_dt: the section is [spinup] time / dt.
  • Open:
    • part A ([time] spinup: output, statistics and checks skipping the turbulence spin-up);
    • saving and reusing the spun-up state (PALM's read_spinup_data);
    • moving the validation cases' own spin-up loops (land_surface_fluxnet, anthropogenic_heat, building_energy) onto it.

Temperature outputs (#536)

Absolute air, 2 m and surface (skin) temperatures, shipped (#536). See Output and the parent's surface.

  • What was built.
    • The core's temperature (3-D), temperature_profile, temperature_mean: $T = θ Π̄_k$ (K, CF standard_name = air_temperature; OutputVariable gained a standard_name).
    • SurfaceLayer ([surface] model = "most"): the old surface_temperature (the prescribed θs) is renamed **`surfacetheta**, without deprecation;surfacetemperature` is now θs Π̄s and `temperature2mθ_2m Π̄(2 m). The sbl examples,examples/sbl/runs.jl(keyθs) and the gabls scripts use the new name. Selectingsurfacetemperaturewhere no surface publishes it is refused with a message namingsurface_theta`.
    • TiledUrbanSurface ("tiles"): the maps surface_temperature (area-weighted skin T), surface_temperature_<street|roof|park|water|wall> (NaN where the fraction is 0), surface_theta, theta_2m, temperature_2m (PALM's calc_2m_temperature per tile: Monin–Obukhov from the tile's own surface layer when its forcing level is above 2 m, else interpolation; area-weighted).
  • The reference (one source of truth). Components.thermodynamic_reference(d): the domain's drivers.reference, else the anelastic base state. The model's config now builds the hydrostatic reference (Urban.domain_reference, from the initial θ and [dynamics] p_surface, else the driver's, else 1013.25 hPa: PALM's hyp) for every Boussinesq domain whose initial θ is positive, not only those with a tiled surface or the column radiation (a buoyancy-free run at θ = 0, the Taylor–Green vortex, has none, and selecting a temperature there is a configuration error that says why); an urban site's domain carries its own CaseReference. Urban.air_reference delegates to it. Components.reference_exner(ref, zc, z; g) reads Π̄ between level centres and, below the first, uses the hydrostatic dry adiabat $Π̄_1 + g(z_1 − z)/(c_p θ̄_1)$ (no second Exner or hydrostatic integration). A bare Boussinesq core made in Julia has no reference: its catalogue leaves the temperature* variables out, and the surfaces' absolute values throw an ArgumentError saying why. Chosen over omitting the variables in configured runs, because the reference costs nothing and every configured domain then has the outputs.
  • One solve. SurfaceExchange.surface_layer_solution is the solve of turbulent_fluxes, factored out, with default_stability_functions(); the tiles' 2 m value calls it, so it uses the same inputs and stability functions as their fluxes.
  • Measured (tests): T = θ Π̄ to 4 ε; Π̄ of a constant θ against the analytic $Π_s − g z/(c_p θ)$: ≤ 1e-12 on a stretched grid (RK4 of the continuous equation), and 2.1e-7, 8.4e-7, 1.4e-5 at Δz = 25, 50, 200 m on a uniform grid (the discrete anelastic balance, O(Δz²)); the 2 m value against a hand assembly of the same solve to 1e-12.
  • End-to-end, Berlin 100 m (the 24 Aug 2025 config, 00 LT, 200 × 200 × 60, 120 s at dt = 2 s, the new variables selected; 95 s wall time on 12 threads). After 120 s:
    • first level (25 m): plane-mean T = 286.39 K against θ = 285.64 K, so T/θ = Π̄₁ = 1.00264 (about 1009 hPa); the mean T profile falls 0.0040 K m⁻¹ over 25–475 m, which is Π̄ ∂θ/∂z − g/c_p under the night inversion;
    • skin temperatures (min/mean/max): street 282.69/282.86/283.10 K, roof 284.98/285.02/285.07, park 282.71/282.79/282.85, water 290.0, wall 282.69/282.87/283.10; area-weighted 282.76/283.34/290.0 K;
    • temperature_2m 284.19/285.35/287.26 K, theta_2m 283.22/284.41/286.29 K;
    • each per-class map is NaN exactly where that class's fraction is 0 (street 2,767 columns of 40,000, park 22,437, water 37,121), and finite elsewhere;
    • the 2 m value lies between the area-mean skin and first-level T in 39,541 of 40,000 columns, and in all 2,147 pure-park columns. The 459 others all hold water: a warm, unstable water tile next to stable land tiles, where the area means of nonlinear profiles need not nest. The largest excursion is 0.59 K.
  • A bug the tests caught. Building the reference for every Boussinesq domain at first included the buoyancy-free runs that start at θ = 0 (theta0 = 0: the Taylor–Green vortex). Π̄ then came out as (-Inf)^κ, and 5 files of the full suite failed (8 failures, 6 errors: config_simulation_driver, config_cli, config_examples, checks, dns_vs_les). Now the reference is built only when the initial θ is positive at every level and g > 0, or when a component needs one.
  • Test expectations that were wrong:
    • The hand-assembled 2 m value first left out the park tile's surface humidity (β = 0.6): qs enters the stability through θv. The code was right.
    • "Both tiles are unstable": the park (299 K under 300 K air) is stable; the test now asserts that each tile's θ_2m lies between its skin's and the air's.
  • Deviations from the issue's plan:
    • A tile's surface is at its displacement height $z_{s,i}$ (0 for park and water, the column's $d$ for built tiles), so its θs is ``Ts/Π̄(zs)$and its 2 m level is$zs + 2$m. The issue says Π̄(surface); this matches the tile's own surface layer, whose forcing height is measured from$d``.
    • The 2 m value is computed from the current state (a fresh solve per output), not from scales stored at the last surface step. No checkpoint state is needed, and a restarted run writes the same values.
    • The old name is rejected only where no surface publishes surface_temperature. With "most" or "tiles" the name is valid, with its new meaning.
    • The tiles of a blended mosaic are evaluated at their own forcing levels, not at the blending height.

Canopy drag of the tiled surface (#538)

A threaded, order-independent drag that does not wrap across open faces (#538, from the kernel-by-kernel measurement of #531). See the parent's surface coupling.

  • What was built. The :post_predictor stage of TiledUrbanSurface, $u ← u/(1 + Δt a/U)$ at the u and v faces of the canopy levels, was a serial k, j, i loop on the main thread that damped u in place and then read it for the v face (Gauss–Seidel, loop-order dependent), with a periodic wrap (im = i == 1 ? Nx : i - 1) at every lateral face. It is now:

    • two launches over the canopy points: the u and v face denominators from the pre-drag velocities (Jacobi) into a scratch array of the component (drag_den), then the division; each point writes only its own two faces, so the result does not depend on the loop order or the thread count;
    • the neighbour column of a west/south boundary face from the boundary conditions, through the existing helpers: periodic, the opposite edge (as before); a face whose normal velocity the boundary fixes (pinned_face_indices: an open face), no drag, since that value is the boundary's and the open-boundary update overwrites it; any other non-periodic face (no-flux), the drag of the column it bounds (neighbor_index, one-sided). The east/north boundary faces lie in the halo and were never touched;
    • the halos of u and v filled first (the old loop read the halo values left from before the predictor);
    • the speed as $\sqrt{u^2 + \bar v^2}$ instead of hypot (3× cheaper in this kernel), and the denominators launched with a work weight (DRAG_POINT_WEIGHT = 8, ten loads, two roots and two divisions per point): Berlin's canopy is one level, 40,000 points, which unweighted ran on two tasks at any thread count (the launch's 16,384 points per task).

  • Discretisation reference. PALM's canopy drag (plant_canopy_model_mod.f90, pcm_tendency, u component): the speed at a u face with v averaged from its four neighbours, and the u loop starting at nxlu, which skips the boundary face of a non-cyclic inflow or radiation boundary (init_pegrid.f90). The open-face rule here is the same. PALM takes the cell's LAD at a face, rather than averaging (sharp canopy edges); the face mean of the two columns is kept, as it was.

  • Measured (Berlin 100 m config, 200 × 200 × 60, open ERA5 faces, warm state after 6 steps, FFTW estimate, under the shared bench lock; the stage per call, median of 200 calls, the old code on the same commit in a separate worktree, interleaved; 3 calls per step):

    threads12612
    drag stage before (ms/call)2.102.122.142.12
    after (ms/call, incl. the u, v halo fill)0.360.240.240.21
    of which the halo fill (ms)0.130.110.070.07
    per step, before → after (ms)6.3 → 1.16.4 → 0.76.4 → 0.76.4 → 0.6
    Berlin step median before / after (ms, 20 steps)488.3–500.5 / 485.4–493.6330.3 / 327.7239.3 / 240.3243.7 / 238.6

    The step saving (0.4–5 ms by the stage numbers) is not resolved at any thread count: at 1 thread three interleaved runs of the old code gave 488.3, 492.4 and 500.5 ms and three of the final code 493.6, 492.6 and 485.4 ms; at 2, 6 and 12 threads the differences (−2.6, +1.0, −5.1 ms) are inside the run-to-run spread of about ±5 ms. Two runs of the intermediate hypot code at 1 thread were elevated (578.2 and 533.0 ms, the latter interleaved with 488.3 ms of the old code) although its drag stage took only 1.1 ms; the final code did not reproduce this. The stage numbers carry the result. The stage allocates about 30 KB per call when threaded (the halo fill and the two launches' tasks; 0 B at 1 thread), about 90 KB per step against 7.6 MB per step for the whole Berlin step at 12 threads. Kernel micro-benchmark (200 × 200 × 1, the two launches, without the fill): hypot unweighted 0.75 / 0.43 / 0.77 / 0.82 ms at 1/2/6/12 threads; sqrt with weight 8: 0.24 / 0.14 / 0.08 / 0.07 ms.

  • Against the old loop, on the warm Berlin state, one call at Δt = 2 s: interior faces (2…N) differ by at most 3.7e-5 m/s in u and 2.9e-4 m/s in v, against a drag change of up to 8.1e-2 (u) and 3.1e-2 m/s (v) in that call: the Gauss–Seidel ordering's second-order term. The west u and south v boundary faces (open) were damped by up to 1.1e-2 and 1.2e-2 m/s by the drag of the opposite edge; now 0 (and the open-boundary update overwrites them in both versions).

  • Tests (test/components_parent.jl, three new testsets):

    • a uniform wind with a drag recomputed from it, $a = cU^2$ ($du/dt = -c|U|u$): the implicit step is $1/u ← 1/u + cΔt$, exact, so 100 steps agree with $u_0/(1 + cu_0t)$ to 1e-12; a uniform (u, v) keeps its direction to 1e-14; a held a ($du/dt = -a$) converges at first order: errors 0.143, 0.076, 0.039 m/s at Δt = 4, 2, 1 s (ratios 1.88, 1.94);
    • a random 40 × 30 field against an explicit Jacobi reference applied in reverse loop order, with the serial and the threaded backend (with_backend, threshold 1): 1e-14; the threaded backend is threaded only when the suite runs with more than one thread (passes at test/run.jl --threads 4; at the default of 1 thread both run serially); against the former loop: 2.6e-3 (u) and 3.7e-3 m/s (v), below 0.1 × the drag change (0.197 m/s) and ε² max|U|;
    • periodic, open and no-flux boxes with a strong drag in the last column and row: the periodic box damps the west/south boundary faces (wrap), the open box leaves them at the boundary values, the no-flux box applies the bounding column's drag (zero there, and the full a₁, not (aNx + a₁)/2, when only column 1 drags); the interior faces next to the strong column agree with the periodic box to 1e-14. With a drag in column 1 and row 1 as well, the open box still keeps the boundary values while the no-flux box damps the faces (u < 2, v < 0.5 m/s): this subtest fails (2 of 126) with the `pinnedface_indices` check removed, which the first version of the test did not (review finding);
    • the thread-invariance harness has a new section canopy (one drag on a random 96 × 80 wind, two canopy levels, threaded launches): AEOLUS_THREAD_TESTS=1 AEOLUS_THREAD_SECTIONS=canopy AEOLUS_THREAD_COUNTS=1,4,12, 10 pass, 0 fail. It is its own section because columns currently stops at its aqchem emissions case (the Berlin smoke config is rejected: `parentdriver = "aeolus"` without a parent domain), which predates this issue.
    • Targeted runs: components_parent, config_parent_domain, urban_run, urban_checks, plant_canopy, naming_conventions 230 pass, 0 fail; validation urban_checks.jl 33 pass, 0 fail (313 s, and 315 s after the review fixes). Full suite after the review fixes: 4482 pass, 0 fail (116 jobs, 514 s).

  • Test expectation that was wrong. The first open-box test expected the pinned west face to keep the interior's 5 m/s; it held 0, because the box's boundary data had not been loaded (update_open_boundaries!) and the halo fill writes the data into that face. The code was right; the test now loads the data first, as a run does.

  • Deviations from the issue's plan:

    • The drag is a launch over (i, j, k) in two passes, not one: a single pass cannot read the pre-drag neighbours that other work items write. The scratch is a component field, built with an outer constructor of the old arity so build is unchanged.
    • At an open face the boundary-normal face is skipped (PALM's choice) rather than given a one-sided drag; one-sided averaging is used at a no-flux face, which has no boundary data.

  • Open: PlantCanopy.ResolvedCanopyDrag averages its LAD to the faces with the same periodic wrap at construction ("as the LES is horizontally periodic"); a vegetated column at an open edge would see the opposite edge's leaves. Not in the Berlin run, and left for its own issue. With #533 this pointwise drag becomes a :post_predictor term of the fused sweep.

Speedup integration: the measured speedups merged together (#566)

Merged as one integration (branch issue/566-speedup-integration, 2026-11-14). It brings in the speedup PRs that the HPC benchmark (#566, run 1 on Fritz, 2026-11-10) measured, in the merge order of the measured build bench/all-minus-D plus today's master:

  • C: loop fusion (#533: #545, #550 with #546–#549, #551–#553). It contains #543.
  • E: the kernel fixes, cherry-picked from the group D branches. E1 and E2 come from #555, E3 and E4 from #564, E5 from #562 and E6 from #565.
  • B: the budget passes, #541.
  • A: the tile update, #544. The canopy drag, #543, came with C.
  • #542: documentation of #535.

Measured (run 1, Berlin 100 m and 50 m, median s/step, if_possible, pinned): this build against master is 1.42× (100 m, 16 threads, 0.291 against 0.413 s), 1.42× (100 m, 72 threads, 0.249 against 0.353 s), 1.39× (50 m, 16, 1.132 against 1.572 s) and 1.53× (50 m, 72, 0.874 against 1.338 s). At 1 thread, where pinning plays no part, bench/all was 1.35–1.37× faster than master; this build was not run at 1 thread. The groups' gains overlap: C is the only one that still counts with the others in (1.06–1.17×).

Left out (maintainer decision, 2026-11-14), with branches, numbers and how to revive them in Built but not merged:

  • group D, the cache-blocked passes of the TKE, the tracer diffusion and the Reynolds stresses (#555 part 2, #561, #562, #565). With the rest in, it gains 0.98–1.10×, and it adds a second code path for each closure.
  • group F, the split in j (#563): never measured.
  • the launch trace of #566.

Conflicts with master (#567, #568, #572): unions only (the roadmap, the tiled surface's budget_writes next to its run_results, the imports of simulation_driver.jl). The result matches bench/all in those files, minus D.

Model setup time at large grids (#567)

Shipped (#567). The Berlin 20 m case of the #566 scaling test (150 M points) spent about 43 min in Config.build on a Fritz node before its first step. Profiled on the Berlin 100 m and 50 m cases (local, 24 cores), two causes, both linear in the number of points:

  • The initial profiles (SimulationDriver._set_profile!) evaluated profile_value(p, z) at every grid point, and its table branch converted the configuration's z and values to new vectors at every call: about 17 kB of allocation per point (41 GB for the 100 m build) and the garbage collection that goes with it. profile_function(p) now reads a profile once into a function of z; profile_value delegates to it.
  • IBM.wall_distance (the TKE closure's wall cap) looked up each of the 26 neighbours of every cell, in the seed and in every sweep, through neighbor_index on boundary conditions built from run-time symbols: a dynamic dispatch with a keyword argument that allocated, serial. Now three index tables built once, the seed and sweeps behind a function barrier, and with no solid cell the height above the floor (below the lid) returned directly. Identical to the old implementation on 36 random masks (max relative difference 0); 200 × 200 × 60: 4.64 s → 0.44 s with a block, 4.75 s → 0.003 s floor only.

Measured (one build, fftw.planner = "estimate", local 24-core machine; "warm" = a second build in the same process, without compilation):

casepointsbeforeafter
Berlin 100 m, -t 12, warm2.4 M12.1 s, 41 GB alloc1.3 s, 2.5 GB
Berlin 50 m, -t 12, warm9.6 M55.2 s, 164 GB alloc, GC 21 s4.1 s, 9.0 GB, GC 1.2 s
Berlin 50 m, -t 1, warm9.6 M56.7 s4.8 s
Berlin 20 m, -t 24, first build150 M890 s, 3487 GB alloc, GC 233 s88 s (23 s of it compilation), 99 GB

Peak memory of the 20 m build is unchanged (68.8 GB before, 70.4 GB after). What remains at 50 m (3.6 s): the TKE initial equilibrium (threaded), the tiled surface's soil columns (build_parent_surface → soil_surface, about 2 s, serial: one soil column per tile and column, about 4 µs each), and the field allocations. The roughly 20 s of compilation of a first build does not grow with the grid.

Second pass: the serial rest, and a race. Profiled again on the 20 m build itself (compiled, estimate planner: 26.8 s), the main thread was in:

  • field allocation: Backends.allocate was zeros, so one thread zeroed (first-touched) every field. On a multi-socket node that also puts every page on the main thread's NUMA domain, whichever thread later works on it. It now zeroes a 3-D array larger than the serial threshold through a launch! over its indices, so each thread first touches about its own slab. The Poisson solvers' work arrays go through it, and the cosine solver's 1/λ table is a launch!. The NUMA effect on Fritz has not been measured.
  • the tiled surface: one soil column per tile and column, serial, 7.6 s at 20 m. Now Threads.@threads :dynamic over the columns, since each iteration writes only its own column; the one shared write, GroundSoil.T_env (a min/max for the smoke check), is under a lock. On the 50 m case (160 000 columns), -t 1 and -t 12 give identical classes, displacement heights and forcing levels per column.
  • a data race in the initial noise (bug found here): the θ perturbation and the uniform velocity perturbation were set! closures calling rand(rng) on one Xoshiro. set! runs threaded above 32 768 points, so in every production-size run the threads shared one RNG state: the noise was not reproducible from [run] seed, and threads could repeat draws. The test suite runs one thread per job and never took that path. The draws are now one array from the RNG on the calling task, for the levels below the depth; the 50 m θ field is identical at -t 1 and -t 12. The θ0 initial state also no longer reads the configuration at every point.

After the second pass, the 20 m build at -t 24 (compiled) takes 12.6 s with estimate, down from 26.8 s.
