One build including compilation takes 62 s with the default measure planner and 33 s with estimate, against 890 s on master. With measure, FFTW planning of the cosine transforms is about 28 s at 150 M points; [fftw] wisdom saves it after the first run. Peak memory is unchanged (70 GB).

Tests: test/tke.jl (no test called wall_distance before, although its docstring cited one): the floor and floor + lid distances, and a 4 × 4 × 2 cube on a 2:2:1 grid against the exact Euclidean distance to cube and floor (< 1e-12). test/config_simulation_driver.jl: every profile kind, the log and linear values below a table, and no allocation per evaluation of a 301-level table; the θ noise and the tapered uniform velocity noise equal the documented draws of Xoshiro([run] seed) (< 1e-12).

Urban parent surface: forcing height above the roughness (#568)

Shipped (#568). The Berlin 20 m case of the #566 scaling test (1000 × 1000 × 150 cells) built, then failed in its first step: solve_surface_layer: need z_m > z0m > 0.

  • Cause, measured on the land-use tables (read_parent_landuse, parent_morphometry with window 8, macdonald_roughness, the default class roughness, level centres at Δz/2, 3Δz/2, …):
    • 20 m: one built tile of 1 379 716 fails. Cell (30, 615), roof class: λp = 1.0 over its 160 m window, λf = 0.047, h = 9.96 m, so Macdonald's d = h (9.961 m) and z0 = 0; the class floor gives z0m = 0.05 m. The first centre at or above h is 10.0 m, so z_ref = 0.039 m < z0m.
    • 50 m and 100 m: no failing tile; the smallest z_ref/z0m is 3.92 and 4.44.
    • Both of the issue's hypotheses hold at once in that tile: d → h as λp → 1, and the class floor, which Macdonald's $h − d > z_0$ does not cover. The old docstring's claim "above h, z − d ≥ h − d > z0 for every λ" was wrong at λp = 1 and for the floor.
  • What was built. parent_forcing_level(z_centres, h; d, z0) returns the lowest centre at or above h with $z_k − d ≥ 2 z_0$. build_parent_surface calls it per built tile with that tile's d and $\max(z_{0m}, z_{0h})$. The margin is PALM's check of the first level against the roughness (land_surface_model_mod.f90: $z_0 ≥ z_{mo}/2$ stops the run, or with allow_roughness_limitation lowers $z_0$ to $z_{mo}/2$). Here the forcing level is raised and the roughness kept, so a tile's surface parameters do not depend on the grid.
  • Effect. On the 20 m table exactly that one tile moves, from the 10 m level to 30 m (zref 20.04 m); afterwards the smallest zref/z0m is 2.03. No tile of the 50 m or 100 m tables changes, so those runs are unchanged.
  • Test (components_parent.jl): the unit cases (the old rule with d = z0 = 0; raised and unchanged levels; no level at all), and a synthetic 12 × 4 block of 20 m cells (r = 2): closed roof at h = 9.96 m next to half-built cells. Every built tile is forced at $z_k − d ≥ 2\max(z_{0m}, z_{0h})$; the roof tile is raised to level 2 and the half-built ones stay at level 1; the old height is refused by the surface-layer solve, and the column now steps with finite fluxes.
  • Follow-up: stop by default, raise on request, report (maintainer decision after #569 was merged: raising the level silently hid that such a column may be better resolved than parametrised; PALM stops by default too). build_parent_surface(...; raise_forcing_level = false) now refuses at build, before the first step, with the number of tiles and the nearest one (column, class, λp, λf, h, d, z0m, z0h, the first level and its height above d) and the option to set. [surface] raise_forcing_level = true (the "tiles" provider) raises those tiles; the build then warns, ParentSurface.raised lists them, and results.toml gets tiled_surface_<domain>: forcing_levels_raised (0 when none) and the nearest tile's old and new heights above d. Test: the synthetic closed-roof block is refused by default ("20 built tile(s) in 20 column(s)", the 5 × 4 columns with λp = 1); with the option exactly those 20 roof tiles go from level 1 to 2, the component warns and reports 20, the worst zref 0.04 m → 20.04 m; a surface with nothing raised reports 0. The Berlin 20 m benchmark config needs `raiseforcing_level = true` to run.
  • Not done here. The 20 m case has not been rerun past its first step (setup takes about 43 min on Fritz, #567), so it is not shown that nothing else fails later.

Pressure solve: tridiagonal in z instead of a DCT along z (#572)

Shipped (#572). The #566 rerun of Berlin 20 m (1000 × 1000 × 150, open lateral faces) built in about a minute after #567, then did not finish 12 steps in 55 min: one thread busy, 82% kernel time, 656 k context switches per second, every stack sample in the pressure solve's 3-D DCT.

  • Cause, measured (benchmarks/hpc/REPORT_fft_20m.md § Findings on issue/566-hpc-benchmark, development machine, 24 threads):

    • With FFTW_ESTIMATE and 4 or more FFTW threads, FFTW planned the z part of the DCT at Nz = 150 as a serial loop over the 10⁶ columns, each 150-point transform threaded on its own. 118 s per transform against 1.73 s on one thread; 243 s per pressure solve, about 12 min per step.
    • Nz = 60 (the 100 m and 50 m cases) and 1–3 threads give a plan that splits the columns between the threads. The cost per point at Nz = 150 was the same (~780 ns) from 100 × 100 up, so it depends on Nz, not on the size. The channel's z-only DCT collapsed the same way (790 against 4 ns per point).
    • FFTW's own pool (Aeolus, #397) and FFTW.jl's Julia-task callback both collapse; the callback is 2× worse. measure picks the column split (0.47 s per transform, 15 s of planning per plan).
    • The 72-thread plan of the Fritz run, printed offline (estimate is deterministic), has the same structure.

  • What was built. CosineFFTPoissonSolver keeps its interface (every caller, the urban cosine_fft provider and nesting included, is unchanged). With walls in z it now holds a ColumnTridiagonalSolver with the uniform Δz (field impl): transforms in x and y, the Thomas algorithm per horizontal wavenumber in z. That is PALM's poisfft method (checked in PALM 25.10.1, which uses it on non-cyclic domains too, with a DCT-I in x and y). Periodic z keeps the transforms along all three axes (_CosineTransformSolver, the former code). A new ColumnTridiagonalSolver(T, N, (dx, dy), dzc, dzf) builds the conservative z coefficients; StretchedPoissonSolver now delegates to it, so the uniform and stretched solvers share them.

  • Measured (minimum of 2–5 solves, 24 Julia threads; "old" is the former transforms along z):

    grid, caseFFTW threads, plannerold solvenew solve
    1000 × 1000 × 150, box24, estimate243 s0.58 s
    1000 × 1000 × 150, box24, measure0.67 s (build 47.8 s)0.53 s (build 3.2 s)
    1000 × 1000 × 150, channel24, measure0.73 s (build 28.6 s)0.49 s (build 2.1 s)
    1000 × 1000 × 150, box1, estimate5.80 s4.18 s
    400 × 400 × 60, box (50 m)24, estimate0.049 s0.036 s
    400 × 400 × 60, channel24, measure0.045 s (build 7.3 s)0.032 s (build 0.7 s)
    200 × 200 × 60, box (100 m)24, estimate0.010 s0.009 s
    200 × 200 × 60, channel1, estimate0.045 s0.047 s

    The new solve is faster or equal everywhere; the one row where it is slower (the 100 m channel on one thread) is 3%. With measure, setup drops from up to 48 s of planning to 2–3 s. Memory: four Nx·Ny·Nz arrays (two buffers and the Thomas factors) against three for the box (two buffers and 1/λ; +1.2 GB at 20 m) and about 3.5 for the channel (two buffers, the complex half-spectrum and a half-size 1/λ).

  • Agreement. The two solve the same 7-point Laplacian with the same mirror closure: 1.9e-12 relative at 1000 × 1000 × 150 on a random source, < 1e-11 in the test at 24 × 20 × 16. Results of every run with walls in z change at round-off.

  • Tests (test/pressure_column.jl): the implementation follows bc_z; agreement with the DCT along z for the channel, the box and mixed lateral axes (< 1e-11 relative, round-off); the zero-mean solution; an incompatible source leaves the uniform residual −s̄ (< 1e-10); the source-buffer path; the coefficient builder. The thread-invariance worker already covers all eight axis combinations.

  • Suite and validation. test/run.jl: 117 jobs, 4662 tests passed (4639 before, + 23 new). validation/run.jl --only gabls1.jl,neutral_abl.jl,wall_models_channel.jl,nest_boundary.jl,outflow_conditions.jl,urban_checks.jl: all six passed, 75 passed, 2 broken (the two existing @test_broken of wall_models_channel.jl).

  • Docstrings corrected. set_fftw_policy!, init_threads! and apply_transform! said FFTW's threaded plans hand whole 1-D transforms to the threads; the 20 m plan threads inside one. ka_port.md has the same correction.

  • Not done here. The #566 20 m single-node test on Fritz at -t 72,0 has to report its step time with this change.

FFTW single-threaded: serial plans on Julia's threads (#575)

Shipped (#575, the fix chosen on the issue: PALM's method). On Fritz, with JULIA_EXCLUSIVE=1, the Berlin 20 m step took 28.4 s pinned against 5.9–6.2 s unpinned at 72 threads (#566). Julia thread 1 was pinned to core 0 and waited 194 s in its run queue over 295 s of stepping; the 99 threads created after start-up, FFTW's pool among them, inherited thread 1's affinity (Linux pthread_create) and waited 15 124 s in total.

  • What was built.

    • FFTW's thread count is fixed at 1. [fftw] threads, AEOLUS_FFTW_THREADS, set_fftw_policy!(threads = n) and init_threads!(; fftw_threads = n) are deprecated: a value other than 1 is reported once per source and ignored; a value below 1 is still an error. _fftw_own_threads! (#397) is gone, since there is no FFTW thread pool.
    • Operators.BlockTransform cuts a 3-D transform into blocks along an axis it does not transform, levels for the x-y transforms and rows for those along z, one block per Julia thread (one when the backend runs serially). It makes one serial FFTW plan per distinct block size and memory alignment, at most four. A level of an Nx × Ny Float64 array is 16-byte aligned only when (k-1)·Nx·Ny is even, and FFTW plans depend on the alignment. The blocks run through launch_weighted!.
    • serial_r2r_plan, serial_rfft_plan, serial_brfft_plan and serial_fft_plan make a block's plan for the block's own output strides, with FFTW.jl's guru constructors. FFTW.jl's plan_* assume a fresh contiguous output, and a block of rows is not one.
    • Every spectral pressure solver uses it:
      • ColumnTridiagonalSolver (walls in z; behind CosineFFTPoissonSolver, StretchedPoissonSolver and DirectWeightedPoissonSolver): one 2-D plan per block of levels.
      • FFTPoissonSolver: r2c in x-y by levels, then a complex DFT along z in place by rows. The inverse is unnormalised; its 1/(Nx Ny Nz) is folded into 1/λ.
      • _CosineTransformSolver (periodic z): the DCTs along the Neumann axes by levels (x-y) and rows (z), then the r2c and the complex DFT along z. The DFT normalisation joins the DCT factor in the write-back.
    • The diagnostics' FFTs (spectra, filters, the streamwise spectrum, the inflow statistics) are not on the step path. They keep their whole-array plans and are now serial; the spectral filter of the dynamic roughness wall model works on a 2-D surface field.

  • Measured: the pressure solve. Development machine (12 cores, 24 threads), minimum of 3 solves, random source. "Before" is master 4f61b8a6 with FFTW on Threads.nthreads() threads; "after" is this branch before the merge of #576. The machine was shared with other sessions' runs, so differences under about 10% are inside the noise.

    grid, solverthreads, plannerbeforeafterOS threads before → after
    1000 × 1000 × 150, walls in z24, measure0.813 s0.766 s103 → 60
    1000 × 1000 × 150, walls in z24, estimate0.927 s0.848 s103 → 60
    1000 × 1000 × 150, periodic z24, measure0.928 s (build 10.6 s)0.944 s (build 0.9 s)104 → 60
    1000 × 1000 × 150, periodic z24, estimate1.154 s1.029 s103 → 60
    1000 × 1000 × 150, triply periodic24, measure0.564 s (build 25.1 s)0.663 s (build 3.1 s)104 → 60

    1000 × 1000 × 150, walls in z1, measure2.229 s2.278 s14 → 14
    1000 × 1000 × 150, triply periodic1, estimate2.088 s2.049 s14 → 14
    256 × 256 × 64, walls in z24, measure0.0176 s0.0170 s103 → 60
    256 × 256 × 64, triply periodic24, measure0.0140 s0.0131 s103 → 60
    • At 24 threads the solve is within −11% to +18% of FFTW's own threads. The one slower row is the triply periodic solve under MEASURE (+18%), where FFTW's threaded 3-D plan is better than serial plans per block. Under ESTIMATE, the planner the Fritz runs use, every solver is 8–11% faster.
    • At one thread the plans are the same (one block): equal within 2%.
    • Planning under MEASURE is 3–8× faster (25.1 → 3.1 s for the triply periodic solver), because serial plans are timed on one block, not on the whole array with every thread count.
    • 43 fewer OS threads at 24 threads (103 → 60): those FFTW created for its threaded plans. Which of them belong to its pool and which are its helpers was not separated.
    • Fritz (#566, solver_compare.jl, 72 threads): the 2-D transform pair took 0.326 s as serial plans over levels against 0.353 s on FFTW's threads.

  • Measured: pinned against unpinned, Berlin example (examples/urban/berlin_summer.toml, the nest, adaptive steps, -t 4,0, benchmarks/hpc/bench_run.jl time). Development machine, s per adaptive step, median of 20 steps per process with loop fusion on (if_possible), minimum of 3 processes. "Before" is master c7603464 (FFTW on 4 threads), "after" this branch merged with it. The machine was shared.

    unpinnedJULIA_EXCLUSIVE=1pinned / unpinned
    before (FFTW's pool, 4 threads)0.8920.927+3.9%
    after (serial plans on Julia's threads)0.8890.853−4.0%
    • Unpinned the step is unchanged (0.889 against 0.892 s).
    • Pinned it is 8% faster, and pinning goes from costing 3.9% to saving 4.0%. At 4 threads FFTW's pool adds only 3 threads to core 0, so the effect is small here; on Fritz at 72 threads it was 4.7× (#566).
    • One unpinned "after" process took 3.9 s per step while the machine was loaded by other runs; it is excluded by the minimum.
    • Loop fusion off (never): unpinned 0.953 / 0.960 s, pinned 0.986 / 0.905 s (before / after).

  • Agreement. A blocked solve is the same arithmetic as the one-block solve, with each block's plan chosen for its size. In test/fftw_threads.jl (subprocess, -t 8, MEASURE) every spectral solver and axis-BC combination agreed with its one-block build to 0.0 (identical bits) at 32 × 24 × 16 and 27 × 21 × 13. BlockTransform against FFTW's whole-array transforms (r2r, r2c, c2c and c2r at 15 × 13 × 9, several alignment classes) agrees to < 1e-13 relative. The residual of every solver and axis-BC combination at 24 × 20 × 16 and 15 × 13 × 9, blocked on 4 threads, was ≤ 7.5e-15.

  • Tests.

    • test/fftw_threads.jl replaces the #397 crash test, which exercised the FFTW thread pool that no longer exists. It checks the agreement above and that planning and solving create no OS thread (/proc/self/task: 29 → 29). The same script on master went 29 → 42 after init_threads! put FFTW on 8 threads, so the test tells the two apart.
    • test/pressure_column.jl: BlockTransform against the whole-array transforms.
    • test/backends.jl and test/config_model.jl: FFTW stays at 1 thread; a deprecated count warns once and is ignored; 0 is an error.

  • A test expectation that was wrong (while writing the test, not in the code). The first BlockTransform round trip failed: the test made the inverse plans after transforming. A MEASURE plan overwrites its arrays while it times candidates, so the test now makes every plan before any data. The solvers already did: they plan on freshly zeroed buffers.

  • Suite and validation. Merged with master c7603464 (#576):

    • test/run.jl: 127 jobs, 18 262 tests passed; after also merging master 03780fb2 (#577), 127 jobs, 18 270 tests passed.
    • validation/run.jl --only gabls1.jl,neutral_abl.jl,wall_models_channel.jl,nest_boundary.jl,outflow_conditions.jl,urban_checks.jl: all six jobs passed, 75 passed, 2 broken (the two existing @test_broken of wall_models_channel.jl).

  • Not done here. The #566 20 m step on Fritz at -t 72,0, pinned (JULIA_EXCLUSIVE=1) and unpinned, has to be measured with this change. The GC threads were also pinned to core 0 on Fritz but used no CPU while stepping (#575 "Not covered").

Stepping allocations: per-row dispatch in the row kernel and the open-boundary snapshot (#574)

Shipped (#574). The #566 memory run on Fritz (Berlin 20 m, 72 threads) allocated 1.22 GiB per step (0.15 GiB at 50 m). Not a leak (a full GC returns the live heap to its baseline), but at a 74 GiB live heap a collection costs 1.7 s, about 25 % of a step.

  • Reproduced locally on the Berlin 50 m case (400 × 400 × 60, open lateral faces, benchmarks/hpc/bench_run.jl memory): 0.151 GiB per step at 8 threads, as on Fritz. With Profile.Allocs attributed by the kernel's method instance: 107 MiB per step in the open-boundary blend (_blend_body!), 36 MiB in the stage combination (_map_body! with _StageCombine), 11 MiB in the buffer copies (range_map! with identity), the rest small.

  • Cause 1 (the 1.15 GiB): the row kernel was compiled for body!::Function. _rows3_kernel! only handed body! on to _row!. For an argument of type Function that a method only passes on, Julia's compileable signature widens it to Function (Core.Compiler.get_compileable_sig shows it), and that signature is used whenever the kernel is not inlined into KernelAbstractions' task. _row! was then dispatched at run time for every row, boxing the range, the index-offset tuples, the floats and _StageCombine (the types the Fritz profile listed). It hit the launches that are too small for every thread (their workgroup size is part of the kernel's type), which are the open-boundary buffers; the large interior kernels did not show it. Results were correct. The issue's suspects (more than 24 arguments, non-concrete args) were not the cause.

  • Fix 1. The body reaches the kernel in a tuple with its arguments, (body!, args); a tuple's element types are never widened, so the compileable signature keeps typeof(body!). _rows2_kernel!, _chunks1_kernel! and _planes_kernel! call their body themselves and were not affected.

  • Cause 2 (the 236 MiB at 20 m): a fresh snapshot of every open-boundary buffer each step (open_boundary_snapshot, [copy(p.data) for …]) in step_rk3!, step_adaptive!'s retry copy and the IMEX cores.

  • Fix 2. open_boundary_snapshot!(snap, sim) copies into an earlier snapshot (a pointwise kernel; a fresh one when the planes changed). StepWorkspaces keeps one per use (open_snapshot!(sim, slot): :rk3, :retry, :imex, :imex_intermediate, separate because the retry copy must outlive the RK3 one). The nesting rollback's own reuse helper (_snapshot_open!) now delegates to it, so there is one copy routine.

  • Measured (Berlin 50 m, development machine, memory mode, 5 steps after 3 of warm-up, one process per row; master 4f61b8a6 against this branch):

    threadsallocated per step, masterthis branchstep, masterthis branchlive heap growth over 5 steps, masterthis branch
    8151 MiB2 MiB1.13–1.26 s1.22–1.25 s0.76 GiB0.01 GiB
    24153 MiB3 MiB1.44–1.53 s1.10–1.37 s0.77 GiB0.01 GiB

    After merging master c7603464 (#576: loop fusion with the fused sweep, budgets, tile update, canopy drag), re-measured at 8 threads (5 steps; the machine was shared with other runs, so the step times are not comparable): master 151 MiB per step and 0.75 GiB of live-heap growth over 5 steps, this branch 1 MiB and 0.00 GiB. Attributed by kernel on the merged branch (Profile.Allocs, 1.29 MiB per step in all): no kernel allocates per row, the fused sweep's kernels (_b_sweep_edge! and the others) included; the largest share (1.19 MiB) has no kernel frame (task objects, bookkeeping).

    The remaining 1–3 MiB per step are KernelAbstractions' task objects (a few kB per threaded launch) and small bookkeeping (the list of open faces, a plane lookup by name). The step times are one run each: no change at 8 threads; 24 % faster at 24 threads (median 1.12 against 1.48 s), which a repeat should confirm. No collection fell into the 5 measured steps here; at 20 m the gain is mainly the collections that no longer happen (1.7 s each at 72 threads).

  • Tests. test/backends.jl: the row kernel's compileable signature keeps the body's type (no Function), and the launch still covers every point once. test/open_boundaries.jl: step_rk3! keeps the same snapshot object from step to step; a copy into it holds the buffers' values (exact: data copied); a copy allocates under a tenth of one fresh snapshot; a closed box has none. A direct allocation test of the per-row dispatch was not possible: in a small script the kernel is inlined into its task and does not allocate on master either (1 936 B per launch on both).

  • Suite and validation (on the branch merged with master c7603464). test/run.jl: 127 jobs, 18 257 tests passed. test/run.jl --only urban_run.jl,urban_checks.jl: 70 passed. validation/run.jl --only urban_checks.jl,outflow_conditions.jl,nest_boundary.jl,gabls1.jl: 59 passed.

  • Not done here. The 20 m step on Fritz: allocated bytes per step and the step time with collections.

Fused sweep split in j (#566 thread utilisation, port of #563)

Built (#579). This is item C1 of the #566 thread work. Run 1 at 20 m (1000 × 1000 × 150, 72 threads) measured _b_sweep_slab! at 21 % of the step with 37.6 of 72 threads busy. The split in j of PR #563 (#558) is ported to master's FiniteVolume.blocked_predictor!. It is not built on #563's group-D driver blocked_update! (not merged, D1 and F); there is still one driver.

  • Blocks. The interior is cut into ns slabs in k times nt row ranges in j, one launch item each (_b_sweep_block!).
  • Edge rows. A row range keeps its first and last ly = lag rows out of the in-place predictor. Their tendencies wait in 2ly extra ring rows, and a third launch (_b_sweep_yedge!) applies them.
  • Post terms. post_plan(post, lz, ly) starts the y-widths at the edge rows, so a post term runs inside a block only where its stencil stays among the block's inner rows; the rest runs after the sweep.
  • Budgets. A term tuple that books per-level sums keeps one row range, since the level sums must be summed by one task.
  • Interface. sweep_slabs became sweep_blocks(b, npoints, nk, nj; lz, ly) -> (ns, nt). sweep_buffers and SweepBuffers gained nt and ly.

Berlin's lz is 1. Every sweep term's footprint has z-radius ≤ 1, and the Berlin smoke tuple gives lag = lz = 1.

  • Master's split at 150 levels and 72 threads was therefore 72 slabs: 66 of 2 planes and 6 of 3.
  • Every plane of a 2-plane slab is an edge plane, so the slab launch only filled the edge buffer and _b_sweep_edge! did every predictor. The 3-plane slabs also take 1.5× as long. The busy-thread count has not been re-measured.

Rule (_split_blocks). It is #558's rule except for the slab bound: planes × row length ≤ 5000 points (_SLAB_POINTS) instead of 20 planes.

  • The 20-plane bound picked a slower split. On 400 × 400 × 150 at 24 threads it picked (8, 3), 32 % slower than master's (24, 1).
  • The scan there (24 threads, interleaved, min ms) gave 47.9–71.3 ms for slabs of 6–12 planes and 82.3–104.5 ms for 19–50 planes. At row length 200 the new bound gives #558's picks.
  • Picks for 1000 × 1000 × 150: (24, 1) at 24 threads, as master, and (36, 2) at 72 threads, against master's (72, 1). For 400 × 400 × 24 at 24 threads: (2, 12), against (12, 1).

Measured (development machine, 24 threads, under the benchmark lock, master c7603464, two interleaved runs each; median (min) in ms):

gridbuildsplitblocked_predictor!step!
400 × 400 × 24master(12, 1)21.56 (14.73), 22.10 (15.45)72.26 (70.79), 70.52 (63.04)
this branch(2, 12)16.31 (8.63), 17.02 (8.67)69.43 (66.00), 69.22 (64.04)
400 × 400 × 150master(24, 1)93.83 (87.77), 108.26 (94.03)417.19, 598.8

  • Where the cap binds, the sweep is 23–24 % faster at the median and 41–44 % at the min. The step gains 2–4 % (median).
  • At 150 levels the split is unchanged, and the two builds agree within the noise.
  • Not measured here: the 72-thread Fritz case. On this 12-core machine 12 blocks ran about as fast as 24, and the rule does not know the core count.

Tests.

  • test/fused_sweep.jl:
    • 1–5 slabs times 1–3 row ranges, in both block orders, against the separate passes.
    • The rule's bounds.
    • A -t 4 subprocess: 30 concurrent splits against the one-block sweep, tolerance 4 eps, measured 0. Also step_rk3! split (1, 4) against loop_fusion = "never", tolerance 1e-10 for round-off growth over 5 steps, measured 0.
  • test/fused_sweep_post.jl: every post operator at 1, 3 or 4 row ranges.
  • Mutation check. With the post y-widths ignoring the edge rows, 560 checks fail.
  • No bug was caught, and no expectation proved wrong.
  • Validation: gabls1.jl (8 checks) and urban_checks.jl (33 checks) pass.

Deviations from #563.

  • No SweepBlock, block_slot or edge_rows: the predictor's edge rows are its lag.
  • No reverse_slabs keyword: the tests reverse the blocks themselves.
  • The row-length slab bound (above).
  • PALM splits prognostic_equations.f90 (322–816) over i with whole columns and writes u_p, so it needs no edge rows; that is not reimplemented here.

Thread utilisation of the step: work-item shapes and serial passes (#581)

The Berlin 20 m trace at 72 threads (REPORT_threads_20m.md on issue/566-hpc-benchmark) showed passes that ran on one thread or a few. This PR fixes the ones in the launch layer and the serial passes next to them. The FFTW transforms (#575), the per-row dispatch and the open-boundary copies (#574, #577) and the fused sweep's split in j (#579) are separate.

Built

  • Boxes thin along i (B1, B2, B5). A 3-D launch with fewer than 8 points along i (_THIN_ROW) gives each work item one k-plane (_planes3_kernel!) instead of one 1–3-point i-row. This covers the open-boundary time blend (_blend_body!, 0.5 of 72 threads busy on Fritz), the stage combination (_map_body!, 3.7) and the west-face gradient (_b_∂x_cf!, 2.2). Like _rows3_kernel! after #577, the kernel takes the body in the tuple (body!, args).
  • The halo shell (B3). _shell_map! launches the whole halo planes below and above the interior over their own (i, j, k) points; the interior levels keep the row launch.
  • Radiative cooling (A3). apply_radiative_cooling! is one launch over the levels instead of a serial broadcast per level.
  • Open-boundary mass-flux correction (A4). The flux and the areas are one plane_reduce! per face (_face_sums), and the correction is one launch per face (_face_subtract!); _fold_faces is gone.

Measured (24-thread desktop, under the bench lock, master with #577 in both builds): the blend of one x-normal plane, 3 × 1006 × 156 = 470 808 points, took 3.88 ms on master and 3.68 ms here at 1 thread, and 1.05 ms on master and 0.52 ms here at 24 threads. A y-normal plane of the same size takes 0.13 ms at 1 thread, so a thin plane still costs about 30× more per point serially: that is the per-plane loop, not threads. Before #577 the same 24-thread blend took 5.35 ms on master and 0.63 ms here. The 72-thread Fritz step has not been re-measured.

Not merged: the threaded step-start copy (A2). Built (158a139b) and reverted (4326dbf6): at 1006 × 1006 × 156 a threaded launch copied in 61.5 ms against 61.5 ms for copyto! at 4 threads and 72.7 against 60.6 ms at 24, and a threaded zeroing took 41.9 ms against 17.5 ms for fill!. One thread already saturates this machine's memory bandwidth; a two-socket node needs its own measurement (not_merged.md).

Tests (test/thread_utilisation.jl, on a backend that threads every launch, threshold 0): the shell against the serial loop and the interior untouched; the cooling against the former loop, uniform and with solid cells; the mass-flux correction's Q, δ and residual on the imbalance case of open_boundaries.jl; thin boxes of 1, 3 and 7 points along i against the serial loop; in test/backends.jl, the k-plane kernel compiled for the body's own type. No bug was caught and no expectation proved wrong.

Left for measurement or later work: the halo fills at 50 m (_z_item!, 28 % of the step, busy 24.3; cause not found in the code), the nested launches (D2), the state copy on Fritz (A2), and PALM's methods that change the scheme: the boundary blend once per step and the state buffer swap instead of the copy.

Measured on Fritz after #582 (2026-11-15, jobs 4472316–4472319, REPORT_after_581.md on issue/566-hpc-benchmark): B1, B2, B3, A3 and A4 confirmed (the blend 2.41 → 0.034 s per 2 traced steps at 20 m, 72 threads). The step at 72 threads is 6.08 s, from 36 to 72 threads 1.21× (was 1.05×); 25 % of the traced step is still outside launches.

Second PR: the TKE step's serial zeroing and the strain's imbalance.

  • A7, whole-field fill! in advance_sgs! (~0.55 s per step on Fritz, 15 + 13 main-thread samples):

    • c.Pw was zeroed every step. Without immersed walls nothing writes it (Berlin: no profile samples in the wall path), so it is now zeroed when built (allocate) and when walls detach, and the wall path zeroes it itself.
    • c.tend was zeroed every step. With transport = false (Berlin's closures; a driver transports e) nothing writes it, so it stays zero. With transport, the advection writes it instead of adding (advect_positive!(…; accumulate = false)), so no fill is needed first.

  • C5, _dual_strain_row! (23 of 72 threads busy on Fritz). The cause is load imbalance, not the launch's shape:

    • KernelAbstractions splits the 150 000 one-row items statically into contiguous blocks of about two levels.
    • Every row of the bottom and top level takes the checked path. On one thread at 400 points per row: 9.05 µs against 1.93 µs for a bulk row (4.7×), and 20.1 µs for a j-boundary row (10.4×).
    • Replaying master's split at 400 × 400 × 48 on 24 threads, the thread with the top level takes 12.8 ms against a median of 6.5 ms (13 of 24 busy); at 400 × 400 × 150, 19 of 24.
    • The two levels now have launches of their own, weighted 5 Nx so that a level gets as many tasks as its work.
    • Measured in one process at 400 × 400 × 48, 24 threads: dual_strain! 13.5–13.7 ms against 13.2–14.4 ms for the one launch. On this desktop 24 threads saturate the memory bandwidth, which hides most of the imbalance; the 72-thread effect needs Fritz.

  • Not a serial pass: the report's item 3 (~0.4 s "around the transforms"). The main-thread samples at PressureSolvers.jl:388, 715, 729 sit under fftw_planning.jl:319, the transform launch itself: the main thread running its own block or waiting at the launch's end. It is the same cost as the launch's imbalance:

    • The x–y transform is split by whole levels, 150 levels into 72 blocks: 66 of 2 levels and 6 of 3.
    • So at most 72 × 2.08 / 3 ≈ 50 threads are busy; Fritz measured 49.3.
    • A finer split needs separate x and y passes, with more memory traffic. It is not done here.

  • Tests (test/tke.jl):

    • advect_positive! with accumulate = false against adding into zeros, with the halo untouched;
    • a step with NaN in tend against one with zeros;
    • with transport = false, tend and Pw still zero after steps, and detaching walls zeroes Pw;
    • the dual strain at Nz = 2 and 3 against the scatter reference, on a serial and a fully threaded backend.

    Mutation check: ignoring the level offset of the split fails 24 checks. No bug was caught and no expectation proved wrong.

The step-start state kept by swapping arrays, not copied (#584)

step_rk3! copied u, v, w, θ (and qv) into its workspace at the start of every step, on one thread. On Fritz at Berlin 20 m and 72 threads that was ~1.0 s of a 6.1 s step (#581 A2). This PR removes the copy when the first substage takes the fused sweep. It follows PALM's method of swapping the state buffers instead of copying them.

Built

  • The sweep out of place (7a84ce28). The fused sweep can read the step-start state from one set of arrays (src) and write the predicted state into another; every other path still updates in place.
  • The swap (deff3df2). In the first substage the workspace's arrays get uⁿ's halo shell, the sweep reads uⁿ and writes u* into them, and then Field.data and the now mutable RK3Workspace exchange arrays. uⁿ stays in the workspace for the stage combinations, with no copy. The passes (loop fusion off or not possible) and a workspace of another array type (a nest child's shell storage) still copy (_keep_start!).
  • Open boundaries rebind θ and qv by identity at every substep, because their arrays change.

Measured

  • Desktop, 24 threads, 1006 × 1006 × 156 moist, sweep plus copy per step: master 731 ms, swap 572 ms. At 8 threads: 759 against 597 ms. At 1 thread: 1856 against 1746 ms.

  • Fritz, Berlin 20 m (job 4474794, REPORT_after_584.md on issue/566-hpc-benchmark), s per step:

    build72 threads36 threads
    master5.656.65
    swap4.706.02
    chunked copy (not merged)4.855.95

  • Loop fusion off. Swap was 26 % (job 4474794) and then 7 % (job

    1. slower than master at 36 threads, with the same work: the

    same launches, the same serial time, and slower memory access spread over every launch. A placement check (job 4481036) found no gap: −4 % unpinned, −12 % pinned and +1.6 % with numactl --interleave=all. The differences follow how each process's ~76 GB lands on the node's 4 NUMA nodes. That is run-to-run variation from page placement, not a cost of the swap. With fusion on, swap was faster than master in every variant (−0.79 s per step unpinned, −0.84 s interleaved).

  • The same states: after 21 steps of Berlin 100 m, the swap and master agree exactly in u, v, w, θ and e. The odd step count ends with the arrays swapped.

  • Allocation: 16 B per step, as on master.

Tests (test/fused_sweep.jl):

  • the fused sweep with the swap against the passes with the copy over 3 steps, dry and moist, with SGS, buoyancy and Coriolis, within round-off;
  • the arrays alternate between steps;
  • NaN in the workspace does not leak into the result;
  • a workspace of another array type copies;
  • an out-of-place sweep with each post-predictor operator against its reference.

A bug the tests caught. _bind! first emptied the open-boundary identity table, which also dropped the tracers that nesting binds (nesting/boundary_data.jl). As a result nest_external.jl failed 3 tests: those tracers' halos were not filled. Fixed in e918e33e, which drops only the names _bind! rebinds. No expectation proved wrong.

Not merged (not_merged.md): the chunked threaded copy, which was the alternative to the swap, plus two variants of it that measured slower.

Phase 74 — Adjoint model via automatic differentiation

Full reverse-mode AD through the solver with Enzyme.jl: adjoint of dynamics, physics and I/O, with checkpointing for long windows. Validated by: gradient verification against finite differences for every prognostic and parameter.

Phase 75 — 4D-Var

Strong- and weak-constraint 4D-Var with the observation operators, background-error covariance modelling, and a quasi-Newton minimiser. Validated by: identical-twin experiments; assimilation of real tower and lidar data.

Phase 76 — Ensemble Kalman filtering

LETKF and ensemble-square-root filters with covariance localisation and inflation, run over an ensemble of LES members. Validated by: identical-twin experiments; comparison of EnKF and 4D-Var analyses.

Phase 77 — Hybrid and coupled assimilation

Hybrid EnVar combining ensemble and static covariances; coupled assimilation across atmosphere, land and urban surfaces so soil moisture and wall temperatures are constrained too. Validated by: joint-state analysis skill against withheld observations.

Phase 78 — Parameter and source inversion

Estimate uncertain physics parameters (SGS coefficients, roughness, soil hydraulic properties) and unknown emission sources (location and strength of a chemical release) from observations. Validated by: recovery of known parameters and known source terms in synthetic experiments; a real tracer-release campaign.

Phase 79 — Uncertainty quantification

Calibrate-emulate-sample workflow, Gaussian-process emulators of the model, Sobol sensitivity analysis, and calibrated predictive uncertainty on model output. Deliverable: uncertainty bands on the flagship urban-heat-island prediction.

Performance, scale and portability

Deliberately placed after physics: a fast model of the wrong physics is worthless, and premature optimisation would have frozen interfaces too early. By this point the physics is settled, so it is safe to optimise hard.

Phase 80 — Production MPI

Real domain decomposition (3-D pencil), non-blocking halo exchange overlapped with computation, distributed FFT via PencilFFTs.jl, distributed multigrid, parallel I/O, and dynamic load balancing for the spatially varying cost of chemistry and radiation. Validated by: bit-reproducibility against serial for the benchmark suite; strong and weak scaling to 10⁴ cores.

Phase 81 — GPU port

Full KernelAbstractions.jl port of every kernel, GPU-resident FFT and multigrid, GPU microphysics and chemistry, and a single-source CPU/GPU code path. Validated by: bit-comparable results within tolerance; 10–50× speed-up over single-CPU-core on realistic cases.

Phase 81a, the CPU half, has shipped (epic #291; its record is the next section). The KernelAbstractions port landed early (epic #291) for CPU multithreading; the GPU backend, GPU-resident FFT/MG and SoA surfaces remain. Measured (KA-o #308, Ryzen 9 7900): Berlin flagship 155 -> 88 ms/step at 12 threads (1.77x), 256³ DNS 1.15 h -> 48 min (1.43x; steprk3! 1.87x); thread-invariant at 1/4/12/24 (3746 hashes). The scaling targets of #308 (≥ 2.5× at 4 threads, ≥ 4× at 12) were missed: the stencils are memory-bound at the 50–75 GB/s DRAM bandwidth of that machine, while compute-bound kernels such as WENO-Z5 reach 8.6×. The scaling epic #406 (shipped) took the 256³ DNS `steprk3!to 3.03× at 12 threads (0.509 s;dns_step!` 2.25 → 0.573 s) and the flagship to 2.02× (2.29× with the parent prefetched).

Phase 81a — CPU port to KernelAbstractions (epic #291)

Phase 81's port, pulled forward for single-node multithreading through KernelAbstractions' CPU backend, with one code path that the GPU backend will reuse.

✅ SHIPPED (src/Backends.jl and every hot loop in src/; KA-a to KA-n, #294–#307 / PRs #342, #367, #341, #377, #353, #378, #352, #384, #381, #382, #391, #383, #379, #385; scaling report KA-o #308 / PR #399; design docs/src/design/ka_port.md). KernelAbstractions port landed early (epic #291) for CPU multithreading; the GPU backend, GPU-resident FFT/MG and SoA surfaces remain. Measured (KA-o #308, Ryzen 9 7900): Berlin flagship 155 -> 88 ms/step at 12 threads (1.77x), 256³ DNS 1.15 h -> 48 min (1.43x; step_rk3! 1.87x); thread-invariant at 1/4/12/24 (3746 hashes). What was built:

  • Primitives (KA-a). A real CPUBackend on KernelAbstractions.CPU(); launch! over storage-index ranges (one i-row per work item, serial below 32,768 points, at least 16,384 points per task); canonical eight-lane reductions; init_threads! (BLAS pinned to one thread when Julia runs threaded); the opt-in thread-invariance harness (AEOLUS_THREAD_TESTS=1, test/thread_invariance.jl).
  • Serial baseline (KA-c). Compile-time integer powers in WENO-Z/TENO, allocation-free halos and solvers, an RK3 workspace: flagship allocation 30.4 → 1.0 MB per step.
  • Deterministic sums (KA-b). Every order-dependent sum on the step path goes through plane_reduce!, plane_map! or range_max, so its order does not depend on the thread count.
  • Kernels. Finite volume on a GridMetrics abstraction that folds about twenty duplicated StretchedGrid kernels into one source (KA-d); halos, open boundaries and nesting operators as owner-computes gathers (KA-f); one matrix-free pcg! for all four CG solvers (KA-g); threaded FFTW by default and the stretched Thomas solve over wavenumbers (KA-h); colour-indexed multigrid (KA-i); the step cores with one fused CFL sweep (5 → 1) and one fused step guard (11 → 1) and per-simulation workspaces (KA-j); IBM, wall stress, canopy drag and large-scale forcing (KA-k); every SGS closure (KA-l); column physics one work item per column, with prefilled or locked caches (KA-m); radiosity LU caching, threaded view factors, multi-column two-stream and Philox4x32-10 counter-based random streams for Monte-Carlo and superdroplets (KA-n); WENO/TENO scalar transport and the positivity limiter (KA-e).
  • Dependency policy (2026-10-06). "Minimal dependencies" replaces "no binary dependencies". KernelAbstractions and StaticArrays are direct dependencies.

Measured (KA-o; Ryzen 9 7900, 12 cores / 24 threads, dual-channel DDR5, Julia 1.13.1; speed-up = new code at 1 thread / at N threads):

  • Flagship CaseConfig(:summer) (64×64×64 child in its 192×192×53 parent): step_case! 155.2 → 90.7 / 87.5 / 97.0 ms at 4 / 12 / 24 threads (1.71× / 1.77× / 1.60×). The child alone (nest off): 100.3 → 46.5 ms at 12 threads (2.16×). Per entry at 12 threads: aqchem 4.98×, fogdew 5.19×, scalar_advection 2.08×, dynamics 1.76×, the nest parent 1.48×.
  • 256³ DNS: dns_step! 3.28 → 2.29 s (1.43×); step_rk3! alone 2.21 → 1.18 s (1.87×); the 1264-step run 1.15 h → 48 min. The unported per-step history diagnostics cost 0.88 s of the 2.29 s.
  • Kernels at 256³, 12 threads: WENO-Z5 8.57× and set! sin·exp 8.07× (compute-bound); axpy 1.06×, laplacian 1.65×, momentum_advect! 1.80× (memory-bound).
  • Launch overhead: 263 threaded and 569 serial launches per flagship step, under 3 % of the step at 12 threads. SMT: 24 threads gain on compute-bound work and lose on memory-bound work (−18 %); 12 threads is the better setting on this machine.
  • Thread invariance: 3746 hashes identical at 1/4/12/24 threads (bitwise invariance is no longer required since #493).
  • Serial wins on the way (new code at 1 thread vs the pre-port loops): CG-family solves 0.41–0.61×; multigrid 0.43–0.59×; the radiosity day call 3.78 → 1.80 s and the night call 1.89 s → 17 ms (LU cache); Subsidence(:local) 15.1 → 0.15 ms; canopy drag 1.01 → 0.10 ms; fog_adjust! 11.4 → 5.0 ms.

Not met — the targets of #308 were MISSED and are not changed after the fact: ≥ 2.5× at 4 threads and ≥ 4× at 12, for the flagship (1.71× / 1.77×) and for the DNS (1.43× / 1.43×; 48 min against the ≈ 30 min aimed for). The stencils are memory-bound: streaming kernels reach 50–75 GB/s, the DRAM bandwidth of this dual-channel desktop, while compute-bound kernels such as WENO-Z5 reach 8.6×. An Amdahl fit gives a serial fraction of 45–53 %, against the 10–15 % estimated; most of it is parallel code that stops at 1.5–2× on bandwidth or on launches too small to split (the 64³ flagship). Also open:

  • FFTW MEASURE plans are not thread-invariant (3 of 13 solvers differ at 64×64×32): production keeps MEASURE, the tests use ESTIMATE. MEASURE planning of a threaded 256³ c2r transform segfaults at 10–14 threads (#397).
  • The radiosity ≥ 10× target is met at night only; by day the SW operator is refactorised every call (μ₀-dependent albedos).
  • Sparse lists (immersed walls, canopy faces) do not thread at the flagship size: launch_weighted! weights by points, not cost.
  • Remaining for Phase 81: the GPU backend, GPU-resident FFT and multigrid, SoA surfaces.
  • Follow-up: the scaling epic #406, shipped (next block): 256³ step_rk3! 3.03× at 12 threads over its own 1 thread, dns_step! 2.25 → 0.573 s; flagship 2.02× (2.29× with the prefetched parent), short of its 3–4× target.

Bugs the tests caught:

  • A rejected adaptive step left the blown-up NaN νt/κt in the simulation, where diagnostics and crash checkpoints read them; the rollback now restores them (KA-j, #346).
  • The process-global WeakKeyDict step workspaces raced when two domains were stepped from different tasks (KA-j).
  • The Mie log-derivative buffer was one global vector: two tasks calling mie_sphere at once corrupted each other's recursion (KA-m's first-use test).
  • CGPoissonSolver divided by a vanishing p·Ap at breakdown and kept going (KA-g).
  • A KA-d commit deleted three launch kernels; the suite at -t 1 missed it (every launch is serial there), the threaded benchmarks caught the UndefVarError.
  • The 1-thread gate (new ≤ 1.10× old) caught performance bugs the tests could not: widened Vararg arguments (axpy 375× the old loop), an in-place aliasing path (1.8×), a boxed accumulator in column_sums (roughness 97–101 ms against 8.3 ms), loads not hoisted from the argument tuple (WENO-5 6.3 against 2.1 ms).
  • The harness worker at -t 1 left OpenBLAS at its default thread count, and LAPACK's bits depend on it (2216 differing smoke hashes); the worker now pins BLAS to one thread.

Expectations that were wrong:

  • interior_dot against Base sum(abs2, view) at rtol 1e-13: Base's sequential sum was the less accurate side (2.9e-13 against 3.1e-16 for the eight-lane order, both measured against BigFloat).
  • A backends.jl test field was identically ±0 instead of spanning four decades (KA-b).
  • stretched asked which module defines the StretchedGrid method; with one kernel per operator that question is obsolete (KA-d).
  • multigrid asserted that MG-CG's dot product equals sum(x .* y), untrue after the planned reduction-order change; it now checks interior_dot. Iteration counts are unchanged in all 28 measured CG cases and 22 multigrid cases (KA-g, KA-i).
  • "init_threads! keeps the FFTW policy": changed by the maintainer's threaded-FFTW decision (KA-h).
  • logging_blowup expected [:v, :νt, :κt] non-finite after a rollback: it described the missing restore (KA-j).
  • Monte-Carlo shadow test argmin(profile) == i_expect assumed one darkest cell; the τ = 20 shadow is a six-cell plateau within the noise. Three other Monte-Carlo assertions held only for the old random realisation; their photon counts were raised, tolerances unchanged (KA-n).
  • Bit-pattern references re-recorded after named last-bits changes: the P3 precipitation-fraction golden values and the CFG Berlin fixture digests after KA-m's fused saturation vapour pressure (≤ 8.3e-16 relative in es over 230–320 K); 13 Berlin fixture digests after KA-l's dual-strain gather (Berlin e moves 4.6e-10 m² s⁻² after 12 steps); and, in the merge train, the `inputdynamic` bitwise qv digests, which went stale after KA-m's fused e_s.

Phase 82 — Multi-GPU and heterogeneous execution

Multi-GPU per node and multi-node GPU with CUDA-aware MPI, plus heterogeneous splitting where radiation or chemistry runs on the CPU while dynamics runs on the GPU. Validated by: scaling on a GPU cluster; efficiency metrics per component.

Phase 83 — Mixed and reduced precision

Float32 and mixed-precision execution with error analysis per component, plus stochastic rounding where it helps. Validated by: benchmark-suite skill unchanged within tolerance; memory and bandwidth savings quantified.

Phase 84 — Performance engineering

Cache-blocking, fused kernels, SIMD verification, memory-layout optimisation, roofline analysis, and elimination of every remaining allocation on the hot path. Deliverable: documented roofline position per kernel and a regression-tested performance budget.

Phase 84a — Multithreaded scaling beyond the KA port (epic #406)

The follow-up to KA-o (#308): the stencils are bound by memory bandwidth and launch overhead, so move fewer bytes and launch fewer, larger kernels.

✅ SHIPPED (SC-a #401 / PR #410, SC-b #402 / PR #409, SC-c #403 / PR #411, SC-d #404 / PR #412, SC-e #405 / PR #413, SC-f #400 / PR #408; design docs/src/design/ka_port.md, sections SC-a to SC-e; nesting docs/src/theory/nesting_time.md). What was built:

  • Roofline profile and pinning (SC-a). benchmarks/bench_roofline.jl times every launch of a step and books its compulsory bytes against STREAM: the 256³ DNS step was 98 % bandwidth-bound in its launches. Pinning (JULIA_EXCLUSIVE, taskset, one CCD) is within 5 % of unpinned at 12 threads and 18 % slower at 4: the launch setting stays julia -t 12, no pinning.
  • Threaded halo fills (SC-b). fill_halos! of a field set (tuple or vector) fills every field in one threaded launch per phase (x/y phases by k-planes, z by j-rows). A set of 4 fields at 192×192×53, 12 threads: 458 → 156 µs.
  • Concurrent parent and child (SC-c). One-way nests prefetch the parent's next window on its own task (prefetch_parent_step!, step_nested!(run, n; concurrent = true)); the Berlin nest does so under run_case!. Two-way nests and :shared_min_dt stay sequential (the chain is serial). Bit-identical to sequential stepping.
  • Kernel fusion (SC-d). Tendencies written once (advection + ν∇², no memset or scratch), the divergence straight into the spectral and column solvers' source buffer (source_buffer, solve_source_buffer!), one correction pass that also writes p and the RK3 combination. DNS bytes per step 29.1 → 14.9 GB, threaded launches 107 → 55. Bit-identical to the pre-fusion cores.
  • Temporal blocking (SC-e). After SC-d every remaining kernel ran at 88–120 % of the DRAM triad, so spatial blocking had nothing to win. FiniteVolume.blocked_predictor! sweeps the tendencies and the predictor slab by slab, row by row, in cache, for the hook-free core (the DNS): those kernels' compulsory bytes 7.92 → 2.26 GB per step.
  • DNS statistics on the reduction primitives (SC-f, #400). Skewness, ε and the CFL maximum moved off serial broadcasts (0.661 + 0.223 + 0.161 s → 0.064 s per step at 12 threads).

Target missed: the flagship's 3–4× at 12 threads. It reaches 2.0× (2.3× with the prefetched parent): its 64³ child and the nest exchange are small launches, and SGS, buoyancy and the hooks keep it off SC-e's blocked path. The DNS, which is one large hook-free core, reaches 3.0×. Next for the DNS: the FFTs of the three Poisson solves (about 0.29 s of the 0.51 s step, outside launches).

Bugs the tests caught:

  • NestedRun.ahead was an Any field; the #329 typed-state test caught it (SC-c).
  • step_rk3! allocated 272 bytes per step after the fusion (a mixed Field/Array tuple in interior_map!); the allocation tests caught it, now 16 bytes (SC-d).
  • Writing all five tendencies in one launch stopped vectorising (39.2 against 18.9 ms at 1 thread); the 1-thread gate caught it (SC-d).
  • The sweep's row loops, inlined, did not vectorise (2× slower at 1 thread); split into @noinline @simd ivdep functions (SC-e).

Expectations that were wrong:

  • The thread-invariance worker stepped a variable-step case for nsteps steps, not to its horizon, so threaded runs prefetched a parent window the loop never reached (90 of 3825 hashes differed, parent only); the harness loop was wrong, not the code (SC-c).
  • max|∇·u| ≈ 0 after an RK3 step from a non-solenoidal start: RK3 combines with the step-start state, so the result is solenoidal only if that state is (SC-d's own new test, twice).
  • ka_fusion compared the tendency fields on the hook-free path, where SC-e made them unused scratch; it now compares the state (SC-e).
  • No reference digest was re-captured for SC-b to SC-e: each is bit-identical to the code it replaces (SC-f re-captured the 256³ record, whose S and ε moved in their last bits with the new summation order).

Open: #397 (FFTW MEASURE planning of a threaded 256³ c2r transform segfaults at 10–14 threads; upstream; workaround AEOLUS_FFTW_PLANNER=estimate, documented in benchmarks/bench_ka_scaling.jl).

Fused sweep for every term (#533)

Follow-up to SC-e (#405): a contract so that any term, built-in or component, can run inside the blocked tendency + predictor sweep, and any configuration gets the fused step (umbrella #531: the step is memory-bandwidth bound). Planned in nine steps, one PR each (the issue).

Step 1: the local-term interface and its driver (branch issue/533-sweep-interface). What was built:

  • The interface (src/operators/fused_sweep.jl, moved out of FiniteVolume.jl): SweepTerm with sweep_term(x, ctx) (nothing = not sweepable), footprint(t) -> (rx, ry, rz), sweep_inputs(t), prepare!(t, sim, Δt) and the point kernel add_tendency!(t, tb, i, j, k, st) (tb[i, SWEEP_W] += …), or its row form add_row! (a @noinline @simd ivdep loop by default, the rule of SC-e).
  • The driver: blocked_predictor!(bufs, terms, u, v, w, pu, s1, s2, g) takes a compile-time tuple of terms. The first, BaseTendencies(ν) (SC-d's advection + molecular diffusion), initialises a row; every further term adds in tuple order. The ring holds 1 + max ry rows (the predictor lags max ry rows) and each slab keeps max rz edge planes per end (sweep_lags; SC-e's fixed 2 rows and 1 plane are the case ry = rz = 1). sweep_buffers and sweep_slabs take the lag and the edge depth; nothing depends on the machine.
  • The decision: Simulations._blocked_core (a fixed list) became _sweep_terms: the substage is fused when every term is a sweep term, the pipeline's body-force hook contributing its components' terms (hook_terms, in pipeline order; budgets on keep the hook). Type-stable (a concrete term tuple or nothing, @inferred in the tests) for the built-in terms and concretely typed components; not yet for a domain with an urban site: UrbanEntry holds its coupling in an abstract field, so its sweep_term infers as Any and the decision is a dynamic dispatch per substage. Harmless today (every urban coupling returns nothing and runs one loop per term); the entry is made concrete when the urban terms move into the sweep.
  • Reporting what cannot be fused: a substage that is not fused is counted in the simulation's SweepReport; the first one records every term that kept it off the sweep with its documented reason and logs it once per process (event = :not_fused). A run writes [loop_fusion.<domain>] (setting, substages_fused = "all"/"some"/"none", not_fusable) to results.toml. The reasons live in one allowlist, Simulations.SWEEP_FALLBACKS.
  • The input key [timestep] loop_fusion = "if_possible" | "always" | "never" (Simulations.set_loop_fusion!), added in review on the model of PALM's loop_optimization namelist parameter ('cache' / 'vector', parin.f90, check_parameters.f90:269). "if_possible" is the default and today's behaviour; "always" stops the run at its first step with an error naming every term without a fused form, as PALM stops when a scheme is not implemented for its loop_optimization (PAC0038, PAC0040); "never" runs one loop per term (the reference). The anelastic core refuses "always" (no fused form before plan step 8). Once every term has a fused form (plan step 6), "always" can become the default. The report's first names, path / fallback, were renamed in review to substages_fused / not_fusable, and its table from [fused_sweep] to [loop_fusion], to match the key.
  • The completeness guard (test/fused_sweep.jl): every method of Components.run_stage! / Urban.run_stage! on :tendencies, :body_force or :post_predictor (19 types today) and every built-in term of _euler_projection! (found in its source: the add_*! calls and the sim.has_* switches) has a sweep_term or an allowlist entry, and no entry is stale. Today only the base tendencies are sweep terms; everything else is allowlisted with the plan step that will move it.

No behaviour change in the step. The DNS core takes the fused sweep as before (bit-identical to one loop per term in the tests, which compare within ROUNDOFFRTOL); every configuration with another term runs one loop per term as before. `steprk3!on the fused sweep allocates 16 bytes per step (as before); a composed sweep with test terms allocates 0. One output change: since every run records[loop_fusion], everyaeolus runnow writesoutput/results.toml(also into a configuredoutput.path), where a run without[checks]` and without component results wrote none before.

Tests (test/fused_sweep.jl, 779 assertions): the base term against the fused tendencies + predictor for 1–5 slabs, serial and threaded, regular and stretched, dry and moist, Euler and the RK3 combination; three synthetic terms composed (pointwise; radius 2 in y and z, which needs the 3-row ring and 2 edge planes; a prepare! horizontal-mean reduction) against their whole-field passes, with the slabs also run in reverse order (one thread runs them forward, where slab s reads slab s+1's front planes before they are touched, so only the back edge was checked; reversed, a front edge kept shallower than a term's rz fails — mutation-checked: 1 front plane instead of 2 fails exactly the 20 reversed comparisons); step!/step_rk3! fused against unfused; built-in terms, hooks, budgets and components reported with their reasons; a test component with a sweep term keeps a domain fused and agrees with its run_stage! reference; results.toml of a Taylor–Green DNS run (fused) and of the dry CBL (unfused: SGS, buoyancy, SurfaceThetaFlux, Sponge).

Measured: 256³ DNS step_rk3! (bench_ka_scaling.jl dns 256 1 3, AEOLUS_FFTW_PLANNER=estimate), base origin/master 9053fe04 and this branch alternately, one process each, under the benchmark lock; minimum of 2 processes, s/step (Ryzen AI 9 HX 370; the 1-minute load at start was 1.0–8.4, other agents' unlocked jobs, so the spread between processes of the same code was up to 40 % at 12 threads):

threads12612
base1.4770.8520.4990.453
step 11.4200.8300.5180.450

Equal within the noise (±4 %): no regression, as expected for the same arithmetic in the same loops. Not measured in this step, although the issue asks for them per step: bytes per step against bench_roofline.jl, 24 threads, and the configured cases (Berlin, a non-urban case with SGS). Nothing on their hot path changed (they run one loop per term as before); the byte reduction and those measurements come with plan step 2 on.

Bug the tests caught (the code was wrong, the expectation right): the first version wrote the substage counts to results.toml; checks.jl (a resumed run's results.toml equals an uninterrupted run's) caught it, since the counts restart with a session. results.toml now has substages_fused and the reasons; the counts stay in the SweepReport.

Deviations: the issue's "lag 1 + max ry" is implemented as a ring of 1 + max ry rows with the predictor max ry rows behind (the base term's ry = 1 included), the same rule counted from the base. Plan step 1 does not yet book budgets or run post-predictor operators in the sweep; a body_force! passed as a plain function always falls back (only pipeline components can be terms). The interface's SweepRow indexes fields by constant (SWEEP_U … SWEEP_QV) rather than by name.

Step 2: pointwise terms (branch issue/533-s2-pointwise). What was built:

  • Terms (src/operators/sweep_pointwise.jl): BuoyancyTerm (dry, or moist θᵥ = θ(1 + 0.61 qv) when the sweep carries qv; footprint (0, 0, 1)), CoriolisTerm (the four-point averages; (1, 1, 0)), SurfaceFluxTerm (rows of the first and last level only), DampingTerm (the one damping kernel's −λ(ξ)(φ − φ_target); along z the rate once per row: the sponge, sponge_term; along x per point: the fringe) and OptionalTerm(on, term). Each evaluates its whole-field kernel's expression in the same order; the kernels stay (the reference and every other caller). damping_levels(g, loc): the level heights without a copy.
  • Built-in terms (Simulations._builtin_terms): buoyancy, Coriolis, surface flux and sponge follow the base tendencies as OptionalTerms on sim.has_*, so every Boussinesq configuration has one plan type (one compiled sweep whatever is switched on; one branch per row and term). The sponge's targets (sim.sponge_target, a NamedTuple of abstract fields) are read once into DampingTargets (kind + one concrete slot per kind), cached in a new StepWorkspaces slot sponge, rebuilt when the target is replaced; an in-place update of a level vector (the nest) is seen.
  • Components: Coriolis, SurfaceThetaFlux, Sponge (a retargeted sponge refreshes its level targets in prepare! from the stage-start state, as run_stage! does before its pass), Fringe (target refreshed to the substage time in prepare!), the urban MoistBuoyancy (perturbation with its fluid-cell level means in prepare!, the w-face sum in the sweep) and GeostrophicCoupling in its pressure-gradient mode (level tendencies and the recorded diagnostics in prepare!, LargeScaleForcing.geostrophic_level_tendency now the one evaluation behind apply_forcing! too; the retargeted sponge's constant per row). SimulationCouplings: the slab's floor flux is a SurfaceFluxTerm; with obstacles its IBM penalty (plan step 5) keeps it unfused.
  • Allowlist: builtin.buoyancy, .coriolis, .surface_flux, .sponge, Components.Coriolis, .SurfaceThetaFlux, .Sponge, .Fringe and Urban.MoistBuoyancy removed. Kept with a narrowed reason: Urban.GeostrophicCoupling (the nudging mode relaxes plane means: plan step 3) and SimulationDriver.SimulationCouplings (the IBM penalty: plan step 5). The urban terms are not exercised end to end in a run yet: Urban.UrbanSite (step 5) keeps every urban domain unfused.

Bug the tests caught (the code was wrong): a domain with Coriolis and LargeScale (componentsbothcores.jl) crashed Julia 1.13.3's code generation (segfault in box_union). Whether a component attaches to a stage is a run-time value (LargeScale's stages depend on its options), and with it deciding the plan's length inference widened hook_terms to Union{Nothing, Tuple{Vararg{CoriolisTerm}}}; the substage then went through a dynamic call. Step 1 never met it because every real component returned nothing. Each component with a sweep term now enters hook_terms as OptionalTerm(attached, term) at every in-core stage, so the plan type depends on the component types only (regression test in test/fused_sweep_pointwise.jl). The first SpongeTargets with Union{T, Vector{T}, Array{T,3}} fields was replaced by DampingTargets at the same time (no unions in the sweep's arguments).

Test expectations that changed (right for step 1, outdated by this step): components_pipeline.jl expected a stage-6 Coriolis component to turn the fused sweep off (it now uses a recorder component without a sweep term for that, and asserts Coriolis stays fused); test/fused_sweep.jl's examples of terms that are not fused are now the SGS closure and the post-predictor hook, and the dry CBL reports only builtin.sgs.

Tests (test/fused_sweep_pointwise.jl, 780 assertions after the review fixes): each term in blocked_predictor! against the base tendencies + its add_*! operator or run_stage! + the predictor, for 1–5 slabs (and reversed), serial and threaded, regular and stretched, dry and moist, Euler and RK3 (agreement at ROUNDOFFRTOL; measured bit-identical); step!/`steprk3!with every built-in pointwise term and domains with the components (rest andmeansponge, a time-dependent fringe target),loopfusion = "always"against"never"(also at 6 threads); the geostrophic diagnostics againstrunstage!'s (exact for the level means and wind means, rtol 1e-9 for the mean tendencies, whichrunstage!takes as a difference of tendency-field means);steprk3!with all four built-in terms allocates 16 bytes (as the DNS). Full suite green on the final branch (118 jobs, 5997 assertions; validationurbanchecks.jl33/33 andgabls1.jl8/8 with it);urbanrun.jl,urbanchecks.jlgreen; validationurbanchecks.jl` 33/33.

Measured: a configured case whose only terms besides advection and diffusion are these: the dry CBL of examples/dry_convective_bl with [sgs] model = "none", [coriolis] f = 1e-4, dynamics.nu = 1, RK3, 192 × 192 × 64 (built-in buoyancy; SurfaceThetaFlux, Sponge, Coriolis components; loop_fusion = "if_possible" reports every substage fused). step!(domain) (benchmarks/bench_loop_fusion.jl, case bench_loop_fusion_cbl.toml) with "never" (one loop per term: these cases' code before this step) and "if_possible", alternating in blocks of 3 steps within one process (4 blocks; minimum per setting; short runs, the interleaving guards against the boost/sustained drift of #535), two processes per thread count, under the benchmark lock, other agents' unlocked tests running (1-min load 1.2–14); ms/step, the minimum of the two processes, and the speed-up of each process (Ryzen AI 9 HX 370):

The first 1-thread process ran at a 1-min load of 15 (681 / 544 ms); the ratio within a process is the robust number. The byte model of the issue (compulsory arrays per substage, counted once per kernel as bench_roofline.jl does) for the tendency + predictor part of this case: one loop per term streams 8 (base tendencies) + 2 (buoyancy) + 4 (Coriolis) + 1.5 (sponge, top quarter) + 6 (u, v, w predictor) + 3 (θ with the RK3 combination) ≈ 24.5 arrays, the fused sweep 5 (u, v, w, θ, the step-start θ): 196 → 40 B per point and substage; the projection is unchanged. A model, not a hardware count: not measured in this step are the bytes by counter, the STREAM-relative GB/s, 24 threads and a second machine.

Deviations: the urban GeostrophicCoupling records its mean tendencies dtu, dtv, dtu_sp, dtv_sp as the level means of what it adds (in run_stage! a difference of two tendency-field means; equal within round-off). hook_terms now holds one OptionalTerm per component and in-core stage (an off one costs a branch per row). A component's sweep term stands for everything it adds in stages 6 and 7 (the interface has no stage argument): one attached to both is switched on once, at :tendencies (step 1 would have added it at both). SimulationCouplings, the one such component today, has no term when it attaches to :body_force (obstacles).

Review of step 2 (bugs, the code was wrong; a regression test each in the "review of step 2" testset of test/fused_sweep_pointwise.jl):

  • DampingTerm did not check a level-vector target's length, which _add_damping! does: under "if_possible" a sponge target of Nz + 4 values ran on its first Nz, a shorter one read out of bounds. It now throws the same DimensionMismatch. The axis is passed as a Val (with the check the constructor was no longer inlined, and the term's axis parameter, until then found by constant propagation, became unknown: step_rk3! then allocated 6.7 kB per step, which the allocation test of the built-in plan rejects).
  • SimulationCouplings' sweep_term returned Union{Nothing, SurfaceFluxTerm}, so the plan's tuple length was again a run-time value (hook_terms inferred as a Vararg tuple) for every TOML run with a slab surface. Obstacles are now a type parameter of the component and the term's type follows from it; "the plan type depends on the component types only" holds now for every component with a sweep term.
  • The step core did not specialise on body_force!/post_predictor! (an argument of a Function type that is only passed on is not specialised on), so a Domain's plan was built and the sweep entered through a dynamic call. step! (Euler, 20 × 12 × 12, 1 thread) of a domain with a Coriolis component allocated 1120 B fused, now 0; Coriolis + SurfaceThetaFlux + Sponge 4832 → 2368 B (one loop per term: 1488 B, the sponge component's own). What remains is the plan tuple itself, heap-allocated once per substage when it holds more than the built-in terms (496 B with Coriolis and SurfaceThetaFlux): plan construction, independent of the grid, not in the sweep. The CBL timings above were taken before this fix (a dynamic call per substage, against 77–242 ms per step) and were not re-measured.
  • The core sponge's 3-D targets (a Field, a halo-padded array: DampingTargets kind 2) are now tested in step!/step_rk3!, fused against one loop per term, on both grids, serial and threaded.

Step 3: terms that need a reduction of the stage-start state (branch issue/533-s3-reductions). What was built:

  • The column form of the large-scale forcings (src/large_scale_forcing/fused_sweep.jl, in LargeScaleForcing, the one place it is computed): every forcing is, per level, affine in its field's own column, c0 + cm (φ[k−1] − φ[k]) + cp (φ[k+1] − φ[k]) + λ (r − φ[k]). column_forcing! fills these coefficients (ColumnForcing, scratch held by the component, opaque to the structural checkpoint) before the sweep: the geostrophic pressure gradient and the advective tendencies are c0; nudging and the mean-gradient subsidence c0 from the plane means; the local (upwind) subsidence cm/cp; a ReferenceDamping λ, r (a table, a function, a number, a level vector or :mean; two dampings of one field combine into one rate and its weighted target). The plane sums are taken once per field per substage, in the order of _plane_mean_level! (so the means are those of the passes), and give the records too (tendency_integrals, damping_tendency). add_column_rows! adds a row: per level and field a constant, a damping or the full stencil loop, chosen once per row; untouched levels and fields are skipped.
  • Components.LargeScale has a sweep term (LargeScaleTerm): prepare! evaluates the forcings with the window of run_stage! (clock = :step or :core, the substage's sim.t) and the Coriolis parameter of the domain's Coriolis component. The reductions are of the stage-start state exactly as the whole-field method reads it: in _euler_projection! nothing changes the interior between the halo fill and the body-force hook, and prepare! runs at that point (_blocked_projection!, before the sweep). Its SWEEP_FALLBACKS entry was reworded, not removed: a forcing without a column form (a damping toward a 3-D target, Field or array; a forcing type of another module) still makes the component return nothing, and the report then names this reason instead of "no documented reason" (column_forcing_supported, decided by types so the term infers).
  • Urban.UrbanSite has a sweep term (UrbanSiteTerm): prepare! runs the captures of its :tendencies stage (the fixed-gradient top's heat flux, the open faces' heat) on the stage-start state; the open faces' θ relaxation (ReferenceDamping toward the boundary profile) is put into OpenBoundaryHeat.sweep's column coefficients (capture_boundary_heat!(…; fused = true)) instead of the tendency field. Its allowlist entry is removed. No urban domain is fused yet (the other urban couplings, SGS and the open boundaries keep it on one loop per term), so this is checked at the term level.

Tests (test/fused_sweep_reductions.jl, 804 assertions): every forcing's column form against apply_forcing! on regular and stretched grids, dry and moist, and its records; LargeScale (all forcings at once, both subsidence forms, five dampings) inside blocked_predictor! against base + run_stage! + predictor for 1–5 slabs (also reversed), serial and threaded, dry and moist, Euler and RK3; step!/step_rk3! of a domain with LargeScale fused (loop_fusion = "always") against one loop per term ("never") for both clocks, every substage fused, the tendency fields untouched; the term type-stable (@inferred hook_terms); an unsupported forcing reported; the urban site's term on the small synthetic city, periodic (top flux) and lateral = "open" (relaxation): the same records as the stage, the relaxation tendency = the stage's. Tolerance: the per-level contributions are summed before they are added and the upwind difference is written as cp (φ[k+1] − φ[k]), so the states agree within agree (ROUNDOFF_RTOL = 1e-10).

Test expectation that proved wrong: the records were first compared with a tolerance relative to the record itself; a damping toward the slab mean has records that are zero up to round-off (1e-13 point by point, exactly 0 from the plane sums), so the tolerance is now relative to the summed terms (1e-12 of λmax N |φ|max). The code was right.

Allocations: prepare! allocates per substage, not per point; the first version allocated more than the passes (75 kB per RK3 step against 53 kB), from functions passed through unspecialised arguments (forcing_mean(w_s, …) boxed a Float64 per level) and a loop variable of unstable type; with function barriers 12.7 kB per step (12 704 B on the 20×12×12 test case with all forcings, and the same 12 704 B at 24×16×24: the test asserts the count does not change with the grid, so an allocation per level, row or point fails it).

Measured: a viscous Boussinesq domain with LargeScale (advective θ and u, nudging of u, v, θ, mean-gradient and local subsidence of θ; step_rk3!, AEOLUS_FFTW_PLANNER=estimate), one loop per term (loop_fusion = "never", the code this configuration runs on the base branch) and fused, interleaved in one process (2 steps each, 6 rounds, minimum, < 60 s per process, under the benchmark lock), with the fused DNS core alone as the floor; s/step, Ryzen AI 9 HX 370, other agents' jobs running (1-minute load 3.6–24 over the runs):

By the byte count: the passes of these forcings move 19 field-passes per substage (5 reads: u, v, θ for the nudging, θ for each subsidence; 7 read-modify-writes of tendency fields: 152 B/point), the fused form 3 (the plane sums of u, v, θ: 24 B/point), and the substage's base tendencies and predictor become fused too. Not measured: a configured case (GABLS, the urban site) — none is fused yet, since SGS, the surface layer, buoyancy or the urban couplings keep it on one loop per term (plan steps 2, 4, 5); their step runs the reference code as before.
